and
J
as well as the kernels
K_{\mu}
and
J_{\mu}
obtained by rescaling
K
and
J
are not smooth. However,
\|\partial^{a}K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\|\lesssim[\mu]^{-[a]}
for every
a\in\mathfrak{M}
such that
|a|\leq{\mathtt{g}}
and
\|\partial^{a}J_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\nu]^{-[a]}
for every
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}
. As a result, the function obtained by convolving
V\in C(\mathbb{M}^{n}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
with the kernel
K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}
is more regular but not smooth. Let us mention that we shall frequently bound expressions of the form
\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}
with the parameter
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
varying from bound to bound. Note that
\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}+\mathtt{f}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}
for any
{\mathtt{g}},\mathtt{f}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
by Remark
5.21
. For this reason, the precise value of
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
is usually of little importance. Note also that it holds

K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}:=K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}},\otimes(n+m)}_{\mu}\ast(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes n}\otimes J_{\nu}^{\otimes m}).

(5.17)

In particular, if
{\mathtt{g}}=0
or
\mu=0
we have

K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}:=\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes n}\otimes J_{\nu}^{\otimes m}.

(5.18)

As we will see, for any
\nu\in(0,1]
the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
belong to the space
\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
. We will also show that they can be uniformly bounded in the space
\mathcal{D}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}
for sufficiently big
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. More specifically, we will prove that for any
\mathtt{w}\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
almost surely satisfy bounds of the type

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}

(5.19)

uniformly in
\nu\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in[0,1]
. This bound implies, in particular, that the force coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\nu}=F^{i,m}_{\nu,0}
satisfy the bound

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes n}\otimes J_{\nu}^{\otimes m})\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i,m}_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\nu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}

(5.20)

uniformly in
\nu\in(0,1]
. If the force
F_{\nu}[\varphi]
depends on derivatives of
\varphi
, the validity of the above bound depends crucially on the presence of the kernel
J_{\nu}
and the estimate
\|\partial^{a}J_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\nu]^{-[a]}
for every
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}
(the restriction to
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}
such that
|a|<\sigma
is related to the assumption that the SPDE under consideration is semi-linear). Finally, we remark that we will prove that the cumulants
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}F^{i_{1},m_{1}}_{\nu,\mu},\ldots,F^{i_{n},m_{n}}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
of the effective force coefficients at stationarity belong to
\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m};0}_{\mathrm{t}}
, where
\mathsf{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})
, and can be uniformly bounded in the space
\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m};{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mathrm{t}}
for sufficiently big
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. More precisely, we shall establish bounds of the type

\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}F^{i_{1},m_{1}}_{\nu,\mu},\ldots,F^{i_{n},m_{n}}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\lesssim[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{1},m_{1})+\ldots+\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{n},m_{n})+(n-1)\mathsf{D}}

(5.21)

uniform in
\nu\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in[0,1]
.

Definition 5.24
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\mathsf{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
,
m=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}
. For a permutation
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m_{1}}
we define the map
\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\,:\,\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}}
by

\big\langle\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}(V),{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{n}\psi_{q}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{m}\varphi_{q}\big\rangle:=\big\langle V,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{n}\psi_{q}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{m_{1}}\varphi_{\pi(q)}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=m_{1}+1}^{m}\varphi_{q}\big\rangle,

(5.22)

where
\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{n},\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in\ C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
. For a permutation
\omega\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
we define the map
\mathbf{Y}^{\omega}\,:\,\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{t}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\omega(\mathsf{m})}_{\mathrm{t}}
, where
\omega(\mathsf{m}):=(m_{\omega(1)},\ldots,m_{\omega(n)})
, by the equality

\big\langle\mathbf{Y}^{\omega}(V),{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{n}\psi_{q}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{n}\varphi_{q}\big\rangle:=\big\langle V,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{n}\psi_{\omega(q)}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{n}\varphi_{\omega(q)}\big\rangle,

(5.23)

where
\psi_{q}\in\ C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
,
\varphi_{q}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M}^{m_{q}})
for
q\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.

Remark 5.25
.

The maps
\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}:\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}\to\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}
and
\mathbf{Y}^{\omega}:\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathsf{m}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\omega(\mathsf{m})}
, defined analogously to the above definition, are bounded with norm one.

Definition 5.26
.

The vector space
\mathcal{G}
consists of kernels
G\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{M})
such that
\mathrm{supp}\,G\subset[-T,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}\subset\mathbb{M}
for some
T\in(0,\infty)
and
\partial^{a}G\in L^{1}(\mathbb{M})
,
\mathbf{T}|\partial^{a}G|\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})
for any
a\in\mathfrak{M}
, where
\mathbf{T}
denotes the periodization introduced in Def.
4.11
.

Definition 5.27
.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\hat{n}\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
m_{1},\ldots,m_{n+1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and

\begin{gathered}\mathsf{m}=(m_{1}+m_{n+1},m_{2},\ldots,m_{n})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n},\qquad\tilde{\mathsf{m}}=(m_{1}+1,m_{2},\ldots,m_{n+1})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n+1},\\
\hat{\mathsf{m}}=(m_{1}+1,m_{2},\ldots,m_{\hat{n}})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\hat{n}},\qquad\check{\mathsf{m}}=(m_{\hat{n}+1},\ldots,m_{n+1})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n-\hat{n}+1}.\end{gathered}

(5.24)

The bilinear map
\mathbf{A}\,:\,\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{V}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}}}\to\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}
is defined by

\mathbf{A}(G,V)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{1}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n+1}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n})\\
:=\int_{\mathbb{M}^{2}}V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n+1};\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n+1})\,G(y-x_{n+1})\,\mathrm{d}x_{n+1}

(5.25)

for every
G\in\mathcal{G}
and
V\in\mathcal{V}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}}}
. The trilinear map
\mathbf{B}\,:\,\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{V}^{\hat{\mathsf{m}}}\times\mathcal{V}^{\check{\mathsf{m}}}\to\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}
is defined by

\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{1}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n+1}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n})\\
:=\int_{\mathbb{M}^{2}}W(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\hat{n}};\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{\hat{n}})\,G(y-x_{n+1})\\
\times U(x_{n+1},x_{\hat{n}+1},\ldots,x_{n};\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n+1}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{\hat{n}+1}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n})\,\mathrm{d}x_{n+1}

(5.26)

for every
G\in\mathcal{G}
and
U\in\mathcal{V}^{\hat{\mathsf{m}}}
,
W\in\mathcal{V}^{\check{\mathsf{m}}}
. In the above equations
\mathrm{y}_{j}\in\mathbb{M}^{m_{j}}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,n+1\}
and the integral is over
x_{n+1},y\in\mathbb{M}
.

Remark 5.28
.

The RHS of the flow equation for the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
involves the map
\mathbf{B}
. Both of the maps
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{B}
appear in the flow equation for the cumulants of the effective force coefficients.

Lemma 5.29
.

Fix some
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\hat{n}\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\mathsf{m}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
,
\tilde{\mathsf{m}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n+1}
,
\hat{\mathsf{m}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\hat{n}}
,

\check{\mathsf{m}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n-\hat{n}+1}
as in Def.
5.27
. Let
G\in\mathcal{G}
,
V\in\mathcal{V}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}}}
,
W\in\mathcal{V}^{\hat{\mathsf{m}}}
and
U\in\mathcal{V}^{\check{\mathsf{m}}}
. Then

\|\mathbf{A}(G,V)\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq\|\mathbf{T}|G|\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}}}},

(5.27)

\|\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq\|G\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|W\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\check{\mathsf{m}}}}\,\|U\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\hat{\mathsf{m}}}},

(5.28)

where
\|\mathbf{T}|G|\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\|\mathbf{T}|G|\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}
and
\|G\|_{\mathcal{K}}=\|G\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{M})}
. If
V,W,U
are translationally invariant, then
\mathbf{A}(G,V)
and
\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)
are also translationally invariant.

Proof.

It is easy to see that the functions
\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{m}}\mathbf{A}(G,V)
,
\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{m}}\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)
are continuous and
2\pi
periodic in space. It is also straightforward to check that
\mathbf{A}(G,V)
,
\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)
are translationally invariant if
V
,
W
,
U
are translationally invariant. To prove the first bound note that

\|\mathbf{A}(G,V)\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n-1}}\int_{\mathbb{M}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{m+1}}|G(y+x_{1}-z)|\\
\times|\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{m}}V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},z;\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n+1})|\,\mathrm{d}z\,~\mathrm{d}x_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{n}.

(5.29)

Using periodicity of
\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{m}}V
we arrive at

\|\mathbf{A}(G,V)\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{m+1}}(\mathbf{T}|G|)(y+x_{1}-z)\\
\times|\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{m}}V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},z;\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n+1})|\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}x_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{n}.

(5.30)

This implies
\|\mathbf{A}(G,V)\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq\|\mathbf{T}G\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\|\mathbf{U}^{\mathsf{m}}V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}}}}=\|\mathbf{T}G\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}}}}
, which proves the first bound. The second bound follows from the estimate

\|\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n-1}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{m+2}}|W(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\hat{n}};\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{\hat{n}})|\\
\times|G(\mathrm{d}z)|\,|U(z-y,x_{\hat{n}+1},\ldots,x_{n};\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n+1}\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{\hat{n}+1}\ldots\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n})|\,\mathrm{d}x_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{n}.

(5.31)

This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.30
.

Fix some
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\hat{n}\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
\mathsf{m}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
,
\tilde{\mathsf{m}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n+1}
,
\hat{\mathsf{m}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\hat{n}}
,

\check{\mathsf{m}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n-\hat{n}+1}
as in Def.
5.27
. Let
V\in\mathcal{V}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}}}
,
W\in\mathcal{V}^{\hat{\mathsf{m}}}
,
U\in\mathcal{V}^{\check{\mathsf{m}}}
and
G\in\mathcal{G}
. For any
\nu,\mu\in[0,1]
and
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds

\begin{gathered}K_{\nu,\mu}^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{A}(G,V)=\mathbf{A}\big(\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{2}}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}G,\,K_{\nu,\mu}^{n+1,m+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast V\big),\\
K_{\nu,\mu}^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)=\mathbf{B}\big(\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{2}}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}G,\,K_{\nu,\mu}^{\hat{n},\hat{m}+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast W,\,K_{\nu,\mu}^{n-\hat{n}+1,\check{m};{\mathtt{g}}}\ast U\big).\end{gathered}

(5.32)

Proof.

The lemma follows immediately from Def.
5.27
of the maps
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{B}
.
∎

Remark 5.31
.

By the above lemma and the support properties of elements of the spaces
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m};{\mathtt{g}}}
the maps
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{B}
admit the following extensions

\mathbf{A}:\,\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{D}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}};{\mathtt{g}}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m};{\mathtt{g}}},\qquad\mathbf{B}:\,\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{D}^{\hat{\mathsf{m}};{\mathtt{g}}}\times\mathcal{D}^{\check{\mathsf{m}};{\mathtt{g}}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m};{\mathtt{g}}},

(5.33)

where
\mathsf{m},\tilde{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\check{\mathsf{m}}
are as in Def.
5.27
and
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
is arbitrary. Note that for any
V\in\mathcal{D}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}};{\mathtt{g}}}
,
W\in\mathcal{D}^{\hat{\mathsf{m}};{\mathtt{g}}}
,
U\in\mathcal{D}^{\check{\mathsf{m}};{\mathtt{g}}}
,
G\in\mathcal{G}
and
\mathtt{r}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
there exist

\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
,

\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{\hat{n}})
,

\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{n-\hat{n}+1})
such that

\mathtt{r}\mathbf{A}(G,V)=\mathtt{r}\mathbf{A}(G,\,\mathtt{v}V),\qquad\mathtt{r}\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)=\mathtt{r}\mathbf{B}(G,\,\mathtt{w}W,\,\mathtt{u}U).

(5.34)

Remark 5.32
.

Lemma
5.30
will be used together with Lemma
5.29
to bound RHS of various flow equations. In our applications the kernel
G
will usually coincide with
\dot{G}_{\mu}^{a}
introduced in Def.
4.19
, where
a\in\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}
and
\mu\in(0,1]
. Note that for any
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds

\|\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{2}}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{a}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma+[a]},\quad\|\mathbf{T}|\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}^{a}_{\mu}|\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma-\mathsf{D}+[a]}

(5.35)

uniformly in
\nu\in[0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,1]
by Lemma
4.23
and Lemma
4.13
(D) applied with
\mathtt{f}=\mathsf{d}+1
and
p=\infty
(recall that
\mathsf{D}=\sigma+\mathsf{d}
).

Remark 5.33
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

k\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
,
G\in\mathcal{G}
,
W\in\mathcal{V}^{k+1}
,
U\in\mathcal{V}^{m-k}
and
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Lemma
5.29
and Lemma
5.30
imply that

\begin{gathered}\|\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\|G\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|W\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\|U\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}},\\
K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)=\mathbf{B}\big(\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{2}}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}G,\,K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast W,\,K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast U\big).\end{gathered}

(5.36)

As a result, the map
\mathbf{B}
admits the extension
\mathbf{B}:\,\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{D}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\times\mathcal{D}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\to\mathcal{D}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}
and

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\|\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{2}}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}G\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast W\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast U\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}.

(5.37)

6
Taylor polynomial and remainder

In this section we use the Taylor theorem to decompose a distribution on
\mathbb{M}^{1+m}
into a local distribution, that is a distribution supported on the diagonal

\{(x,\ldots,x)\in\mathbb{M}^{1+m}\,|\,x\in\mathbb{M}\},

and a certain non-local remainder that is easy to control. More specifically, we introduce and study maps
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
, where
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
and
\mathtt{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
are such that
|a|<\mathtt{l}
. The map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
allows to express

\mathcal{X}^{m,a}V\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0},\quad|a|<\mathtt{l},

(6.1)

in terms of

\mathcal{X}^{m,b}V\in\mathcal{V}^{m;0},\quad b\in\mathfrak{M}^{m},~|b|=\mathtt{l}\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad v^{b}\in\mathcal{D}^{;0},\quad b\in\mathfrak{M}^{m},~|b|<\mathtt{l},

(6.2)

where

v^{b}=\mathbf{I}(\mathcal{X}^{m,b}V):=\int_{\mathbb{M}^{m}}((\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\mathcal{X}^{m,b}V)(\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\,;\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m})\in\mathcal{D}^{;0}.

(6.3)

Recall that the polynomials
\mathcal{X}^{m,a}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
were introduced in Def.
2.8
. We call
\mathbf{I}V
the local part of the distribution
V
. Note that distributions
V
that we encounter in this work are typically essentially localized close to the diagonal. Consequently, the distribution
\mathcal{X}^{m,b}V
obtained after the multiplication of
V
by a polynomial in the relative coordinates of the degree
|b|
is typically better behaved than the distribution
V
and its behavior improves as the degree
|b|
increases.

The results of this section will be used in the proof of uniform bounds for the relevant cumulants of the effective force coefficients as well as in the deterministic analysis to prove uniform bounds for the relevant effective force coefficients at stationarity. We remark that the results established in this section are useful if the parameters
\nu
and
\mu
satisfy the condition
\mu\geq\nu
. For
\nu>0
and
\mu\in[0,\nu)
one can bound the relevant coefficients using a simpler strategy that is also used to bound the irrelevant coefficients.

In the proof of bounds for the cumulants we apply the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
to express the expectations of the relevant effective force coefficients

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}

(6.4)

in terms of the expectations of the irrelevant coefficients
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
,
|b|=\mathtt{l}
, and the following expectations
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathbf{I}(\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}})\in\mathcal{D}^{;0}=C(\mathbb{H})
,
|b|<\mathtt{l}
, where
\mathtt{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is chosen sufficiently big so that
\varrho(i,m)+\mathtt{l}>0
. Note that by the stationarity
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}(x)\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
does not depend on
x\in\mathbb{M}
. The relevant coefficients
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\in\mathbb{R}
are fixed uniquely by the renormalization conditions at
\mu=1
and the flow equation. The bounds for relevant
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
follow from the bounds for the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
proved in this section as well as the bounds for
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
and the bounds for irrelevant
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
.

In the deterministic analysis we apply the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
to express the relevant effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
in terms of the irrelevant coefficients
F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
,
|b|=\mathtt{l}
, and the coefficients
f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathbf{I}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{;0}
,
|b|<\mathtt{l}
, where
\mathtt{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is again chosen sufficiently big so that
\varrho(i,m)+\mathtt{l}>0
. We prove the bounds for relevant
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
using the bounds for the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
proved in this section as well as the bounds for relevant
f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}
established in the probabilistic analysis and the bounds for irrelevant
F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}
. The map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
is also used to construct the relevant effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}
and bound
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}
.

Definition 6.1
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
and
V\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
we define
\partial^{a}V\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
by

\partial^{a}V(x;x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}):=\partial^{a_{1}}_{x_{1}}\ldots\partial^{a_{m}}_{x_{m}}V(x;x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}).

(6.5)

This map extends in an obvious way to
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})\supset\mathcal{D}^{m}
.

Definition 6.2
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
v\in\mathcal{D}
we define
\mathbf{L}^{m}v\in\mathcal{D}^{m}
by

\langle\,\mathbf{L}^{m}v\,,\,\psi\otimes\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{m}\,\rangle=\langle v\,,\,\psi\varphi_{1}\ldots\varphi_{m}\rangle,

(6.6)

where
\psi,\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
are arbitrary.

Remark 6.3
.

We call
\mathbf{L}^{m}(v)\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
the local extension of a distribution
v\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M})
. Note that
\mathbf{L}^{m}(v)
is a distribution supported on the diagonal.

Lemma 6.4
.

Fix
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. There exists a constant
c>0
such that the following statement is true. Assume that
v^{a}\in\mathbb{R}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{t}}
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
, are such that
|v^{a}|\leq C\,[\nu]^{[a]}
for some
C>0
and
v^{a}=0
unless
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}^{m}
. Let
V^{a}\in\mathcal{D}^{m}
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
, be defined by

V^{a}=\sum_{b\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}}\frac{1}{b!}\partial^{b}\mathbf{L}^{m}(v^{a+b}).

(6.7)

Then for all
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
it holds
V^{a}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}V^{0}
and

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J_{\nu}^{\otimes m})\ast V^{a}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq c\,C\,[\nu]^{[a]}.

(6.8)

Proof.

The identity
V^{a}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}V^{0}
follows from the fact that
\mathcal{X}^{m,a}\partial^{b}\mathbf{L}^{m}(v)=0
unless
b=a+c
for some
c\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
and

\mathcal{X}^{m,a}\frac{1}{(a+c)!}\partial^{a+c}\mathbf{L}^{m}(v)=\frac{1}{c!}\partial^{c}\mathbf{L}^{m}(v).

Next, recall that
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}^{m}
iff
a=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})
and
a_{q}=(\mathring{a}_{q},\bar{a}_{q})\in\mathfrak{M}=\mathbb{N}_{0}^{1+\mathsf{d}}
,
\mathring{a}_{q}=0
,
|a_{q}|<\sigma
for all
q\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. We note that
a+b\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}^{m}
implies that
b=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{m})\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}^{m}
. Moreover,
\|\partial^{b_{q}}J_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\nu]^{-|b_{q}|}
for all
q\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
by Lemma
4.13
(B). To conclude the proof it is now enough to use Def.
5.12
of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
.
∎

Definition 6.5
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\tau\in(0,\infty)
and
V\in C(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
we define
\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}V\in C(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
by

\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}V(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}):=\tau^{-(1+\mathsf{d})m}\,V(x;x+(y_{1}-x)/\tau,\ldots,x+(y_{m}-x)/\tau).

(6.9)

This map extends in an obvious way to
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})\supset\mathcal{D}^{m}
.

Definition 6.6
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. The linear map
\mathbf{I}\,:\,\mathcal{V}^{m}\to\mathcal{V}
is defined by

\mathbf{I}V(x):=\int_{\mathbb{M}^{m}}V(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m}).

(6.10)

The map
\mathbf{I}
is extended to
V\in\mathcal{D}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}
by the formula

\langle\mathbf{I}V,\psi\rangle:=\langle(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes(K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast J)^{\otimes m})\ast V,\psi\otimes 1^{\otimes m}_{\mathbb{M}}\rangle,

(6.11)

where
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
.

Remark 6.7
.

The map
\mathbf{I}
extracts the local part of a distribution. In particular, note that if
V\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
is proportional to the Dirac delta on the diagonal, then
V=\mathbf{L}^{m}(\mathbf{I}V)
, where
\mathbf{L}^{m}
is the local extension of a distribution introduced in Def.
6.2
.

Lemma 6.8
.

The map
\mathbf{I}
is well defined and has the following properties.

(A)

\mathbf{I}(K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V)=K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathbf{I}V

for any
V\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
,
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\nu,\mu\in[0,\infty)
.

(B)

\|\mathbf{I}V\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
for
V\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
.

(C)

Let
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
,
v\in\mathcal{V}
,
V=\partial^{a}\mathbf{L}^{m}v
. It holds
\mathbf{I}(\mathcal{X}^{m,a}V)=a!\,v
as well as
\mathbf{I}(\mathcal{X}^{m,b}V)=0
for all
b\in\mathfrak{M}
such that
b\neq a
.

Proof.

In order to prove that the map is well defined it is enough to use the identity
\int_{\mathbb{M}}K(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{M}}J(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=1
. Part (A) is a consequence of the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{M}}K_{\mu}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{M}}J_{\nu}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=1
. Part (B) follows immediately from Def.
5.12
of the norm
\mathcal{V}^{m}
. To prove Part (C) we note that
\partial^{b}\mathbf{L}^{m}(v)\in\mathcal{D}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}
for any
{\mathtt{g}}\geq|b|
and
\int_{\mathbb{M}}(\partial^{b}K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}})(\mathrm{d}x)=0
for any
{\mathtt{g}}\geq|b|
such that
b\neq 0
. Hence,
\mathbf{I}(\mathbf{L}^{m}v)=v
and
\mathbf{I}(\partial^{b}\mathbf{L}^{m}v)=0
unless
b=0
. Part (C) follows now from the above identities and the fact that
\mathcal{X}^{m,b}\mathbf{L}^{m}v=0
unless
b=0
.
∎

Definition 6.9
.

For
m,\mathtt{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
such that
|a|<\mathtt{l}
and two collections of distributions:
v^{b}\in\mathcal{D}
,
b\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
,
|b|<\mathtt{l}
, and
V^{b}\in\mathcal{D}^{m}
,
b\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
,
|b|=\mathtt{l}
, the distribution
\mathbf{X}_{\mathtt{l}}^{a}(v^{b},V^{b})\in\mathcal{D}^{m}
is defined by the equality

\mathbf{X}_{\mathtt{l}}^{a}(v^{b},V^{b}):=\sum_{|a+b|<\mathtt{l}}\,\frac{1}{b!}\partial^{b}\mathbf{L}^{m}(v^{a+b})\\
+\sum_{|a+b|=\mathtt{l}}\frac{|b|}{b!}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)^{|b|-1}\,\partial^{b}\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}(V^{a+b})\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(6.12)

where the sums above are over
b\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
.

Remark 6.10
.

This map is well defined by the bounds proved in Lemma
6.15
.

Remark 6.11
.

The usefulness of the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
comes from the identity proved in Theorem
6.12
. Observe that the first term on the RHS of Eq. (
6.12
) is a distribution on
\mathbf{M}^{1+m}
that is supported on the diagonal. The second term of the RHS of this equality is a certain non-local remainder, which can be controlled provided
\mathtt{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is chosen big enough. We show desirable bounds for
\mathbf{X}_{\mathtt{l}}^{a}(v^{b},V^{b})
in Theorem
6.14
stated below.

Theorem 6.12
.

Let
m,\mathtt{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
V\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
such that
\mathcal{X}^{m,b}V\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
for any
b\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
,
|b|\leq\mathtt{l}
. Then
\mathbf{I}(\mathcal{X}^{m,a}V)\in\mathcal{D}^{;0}
and

\mathcal{X}^{m,a}V=\mathbf{X}_{\mathtt{l}}^{a}(\mathbf{I}(\mathcal{X}^{m,b}V),\mathcal{X}^{m,b}V)

(6.13)

for all
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
such that
|a|<\mathtt{l}
.

Proof.

First recall that for any
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{N})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, it holds

\varphi(y)=\sum_{|b|<\mathtt{l}}\frac{1}{b!}(y-x)^{b}\,(\partial^{b}\varphi)(x)\\
+\sum_{|b|=\mathtt{l}}\frac{|b|}{b!}\,(y-x)^{b}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)^{\mathtt{l}-1}\,(\partial^{b}\varphi)(x+\tau(y-x))\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(6.14)

by the Taylor theorem, where the sums are over
b\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{N}
. Consequently, for any
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
we have

(\mathcal{X}^{m,a}\varphi)(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})=\sum_{|a+b|<\mathtt{l}}\frac{(-1)^{|b|}}{b!}\mathcal{X}^{m,a+b}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})(\partial^{b}\varphi)(x;x,\ldots,x)\\
+\sum_{|a+b|=\mathtt{l}}\frac{(-1)^{|b|}|b|}{b!}\mathcal{X}^{m,a+b}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)^{\mathtt{l}-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}^{\dagger}\partial^{b}\varphi)(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(6.15)

where the map
\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}^{\dagger}:=\tau^{(1+\mathsf{d})m}\mathbf{Z}_{1/\tau}
is a formal dual to the map
\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}
and the sums are over
b\in\mathfrak{M}
. In order to prove the theorem it is now enough to test
\mathcal{X}^{m,a}V
with
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
, apply the above formula and use the definitions of the maps
\mathbf{X}_{\mathtt{l}}^{a}
and
\mathbf{I}
.
∎

Remark 6.13
.

Note that the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
treats space and time on equal footing and can be used to build the solution theory for both elliptic and parabolic equations. By the above theorem we have

F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}(f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu},F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu})\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0},

(6.16)

where
f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathbf{I}(F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu})\in\mathcal{D}^{;0}
and
\mathtt{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
is such that
\varrho(i,m)+|\mathtt{l}|>0
. This identity will be used in the deterministic analysis to express the relevant effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\varrho(i,m,a)=\varrho(i,m)+[a]<0
, in terms of the irrelevant coefficients
F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}
,
|b|=\mathtt{l}
, and the relevant or irrelevant coefficients
f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}
,
|b|<\mathtt{l}
. As an aside, we remark that using the generalized Taylor formula from
[
26
, Appendix A]
it is possible to define a map
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
such that the property expressed in Eq. (
6.16
) holds for
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
takes as an input only irrelevant coefficients
F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}
and relevant coefficients
f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}
. In contrast to the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
, the map
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
does not treat space and time on equal footing. Although, the use of the map
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
would be more natural in the context of parabolic equations we prefer to use the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
since the analysis of properties of the map
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
is more involved.

Theorem 6.14
.

Let
m,\mathtt{l}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. There exists a constant
c>0
and
\mathtt{f}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that the following statement is true. Let
V^{b}\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
,
v^{b}\in\mathcal{V}
be as in Def.
6.9
and
\nu\geq 0
and
\mu>\nu
. Assume that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast V^{b}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq C\,[\mu]^{[b]},\quad|b|=\mathtt{l},\quad\quad\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast v^{b}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq C\,[\mu]^{[b]},\quad|b|<\mathtt{l},

(6.17)

for
b\in\mathfrak{M}
. Then

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;\mathtt{f}}\ast\mathbf{X}_{\mathtt{l}}^{a}(v^{b},V^{b})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq c\,C\,[\mu]^{[a]},

(6.18)

for
a\in\mathfrak{M}
such that
|a|<\mathtt{l}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathtt{h}=\lfloor\sigma/2\rfloor+1
. Since
\mathbf{R}_{\nu}J_{\nu}=\delta_{\mathbb{M}}
we obtain

K^{\ast({\mathtt{g}}+\mathtt{h}),\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}=K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast(K_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{h}}\otimes(\mathbf{R}_{\nu}K_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{h}})^{\otimes m}).

(6.19)

By Lemma
4.13
(C) for
\mu\geq\nu
it holds

\|K^{\ast({\mathtt{g}}+\mathtt{h}),\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast V^{b}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}.

(6.20)

On the other hand, since
\|J_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim 1
in order to conclude the proof it is enough to show that

\|K^{\ast\mathtt{f},\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast\mathbf{X}_{\mathtt{l}}^{a}(v^{b},V^{b})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim C\,[\mu]^{[a]},

(6.21)

where
\mathtt{f}=6{\mathtt{g}}+6\mathtt{h}+\mathtt{l}
. This bound follows from Def.
6.9
of the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
, the bound (
6.20
), Lemma
6.15
and the bounds
\|\partial^{c}K^{\ast\mathtt{l}}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-[c]}
,
c\in\mathfrak{M}
,
|c|\leq\mathtt{l}
, proved in Lemma
4.13
(A).
∎

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
\mathtt{h}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. The following bounds:

(A)

\|K^{\ast 3\mathtt{h},\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast\mathbf{L}^{m}v\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{\ast\mathtt{h}}_{\mu}\ast v\|_{\mathcal{V}}
,

(B)

\|K^{\ast 6\mathtt{h},\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{\ast\mathtt{h},\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}

hold uniformly in
\tau,\mu\in(0,1]
and
v\in\mathcal{V}
,
V\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
.

Proof.

It follows easily from the definitions given in this section, Def.
4.2
of the rescaling map
\mathbf{S}_{\mu}
and Eq. (
5.9
) defining the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
that

\mathbf{S}_{\mu}\mathbf{L}^{m}v=\mathbf{L}^{m}\mathbf{S}_{\mu}v,\quad\mathbf{S}_{\mu}\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}V=\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}\mathbf{S}_{\mu}V,\quad K_{\mu}=\mathbf{S}_{\mu}K,\quad\mathbf{S}_{\mu}(H\ast V)=\mathbf{S}_{\mu}H\ast\mathbf{S}_{\mu}V,

(6.22)

\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}=[\mu]^{\mathsf{D}}\|\mathbf{S}_{\mu}V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}},\qquad\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}=\|\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}

(6.23)

for any
\tau,\mu>0
and
v\in\mathcal{V}
,
V\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
,
H\in\mathcal{K}^{1+m}
. By the above identities it is enough to prove the lemma for
\mu=1
and all
v\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{M})
and
V\,:\,\mathbb{M}\to\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{M}^{m})
such that
\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}<\infty
. Using
\mathbf{P}^{\mathtt{h}}K^{\ast\mathtt{h}}=\delta_{\mathbb{M}}
we obtain

(K^{\ast 3\mathtt{h},\otimes(1+m)}\ast\mathbf{L}^{m}v)(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\\
=\int_{\mathbb{M}}(K^{\ast\mathtt{h}}\ast v)(z)~\mathbf{P}^{\mathtt{h}}(\partial_{z})K^{\ast 3\mathtt{h}}(x-z)K^{\ast 3\mathtt{h}}(y_{1}-z)\ldots K^{\ast 3\mathtt{h}}(y_{m}-z)\,\mathrm{d}z\\
=\int_{\mathbb{M}}(K^{\ast\mathtt{h}}\ast v)(z)~H^{\ast\mathtt{h}}_{0}(x-z,y_{1}-z,\ldots,y_{m}-z)\,\mathrm{d}z,

(6.24)

where
H_{0}\in\mathcal{K}^{1+m}
is defined in the lemma below and
\mathbf{P}(\partial_{z})=1-\Delta_{z}
. This proves the bound (A) since

K^{\ast 3\mathtt{h},\otimes(1+m)}\ast\mathbf{L}^{m}v=H_{0}^{\ast\mathtt{h}}\ast\mathbf{L}^{m}(K^{\ast\mathtt{h}}\ast v).

(6.25)

The bound (B) follows from the identities

\begin{gathered}K^{\ast 3,\otimes(1+m)}=(K^{\ast 3}\otimes\check{K}^{\otimes m}_{\tau})\ast\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes K^{\otimes m}),\\
K^{\ast 3,\otimes(1+m)}=H_{\tau}\ast\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}(K\otimes\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes m}),\end{gathered}

(6.26)

where the kernels
\check{K}_{\tau}
and
H_{\tau}
are defined in the lemma below. Indeed, applying the above equalities recursively and using
\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}H\ast\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}V=\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}(H\ast V)
we arrive at

K^{\ast 6\mathtt{h},\otimes(1+m)}\ast\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}V=\check{H}^{\ast\mathtt{h}}_{\tau}\ast\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}(K^{\ast\mathtt{h},\otimes(1+m)}\ast V),\quad\check{H}_{\tau}:=(K^{\ast 3}\otimes\check{K}^{\otimes m}_{\tau})\ast H_{\tau},

(6.27)

which implies Part (B) since
\|\check{H}_{\tau}\|_{\mathcal{K}^{1+m}}\lesssim 1
uniformly in
\tau\in(0,1]
by the lemma below. It remains to establish the identities (
6.26
). The first one follows from the fact that
\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes K^{\otimes m})=\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes\breve{K}_{\tau}^{\otimes m}
, where
\breve{K}_{\tau}(x):=\tau^{-1-\mathsf{d}}K(x/\tau)
satisfies the equation
\mathbf{P}(\tau\partial_{x})\breve{K}_{\tau}=\delta_{\mathbb{M}}
. To show the second one we observe that by the definition of
\check{H}_{\tau}
given in the lemma below we have

\mathbf{Z}_{1/\tau}(H_{\tau})(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\\
=\tau^{(1+\mathsf{d})m}\mathbf{P}(\partial_{x})\,K^{\ast 3}(x)K^{\ast 3}(x+\tau(y_{1}-x))\ldots K^{\ast 3}(x+\tau(y_{1}-x)).

(6.28)

After convolving both sides with the kernel
K\otimes\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes m}
we obtain

\mathbf{Z}_{1/\tau}(H_{\tau})\ast\big(K\otimes\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes m}\big)=\mathbf{Z}_{1/\tau}(K^{\ast 3,\otimes(1+m)})

(6.29)

The second of the identities (
6.26
) follows after applying the map
\mathbf{Z}_{\tau}
to both sides of the above equality.
∎

Lemma 6.16
.

The distributions
\check{K}_{\tau}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M})
and
H_{\tau}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
defined by

\begin{gathered}\check{K}_{\tau}(x):=\mathbf{P}(\tau\partial_{x})K^{\ast 3}(x),\\
\ H_{\tau}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}):=\mathbf{P}(\partial_{x}+(1-\tau)\partial_{y})\,K^{\ast 3}(x)K^{\ast 3}(y_{1})\ldots K^{\ast 3}(y_{m}),\end{gathered}

(6.30)

where
\partial_{y}:=\partial_{y_{1}}+\ldots+\partial_{y_{m}}
, are polynomials in
\tau>0
of degree
3
whose coefficients belong to
\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{K}^{1+m}
, respectively.

Remark 6.17
.

The lemma follows immediately from the fact that
\partial^{a}K\in\mathcal{K}
for any multi-index
a
such that
|a|=1
. Note that the statement remains true when the kernel
K^{\ast 3}
is replaced by
K
. However, the proof of this stronger version is slightly non-trivial.

7
Effective force coefficients at stationarity

In this section we define effective force coefficients

F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m}\subset\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m}),\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0},~(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\},~\mu\in[0,1].

(7.1)

The coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
are multi-linear translationally invariant functionals of the noise
\varXi_{\nu}
. Since the noise
\varXi_{\nu}
is a stationary random field the same is true for the coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
. At later stage of the construction we shall introduce other effective force coefficients which depend on the initial data and in general are not stationary.

We have
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
, where
\mathcal{X}^{m,a}
is some polynomial and the coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m}
are such that the effective force functional
F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
given by the following formal power series

\langle F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\lambda^{i}\,\langle F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle

(7.2)

satisfies the flow equation

\langle\partial_{\mu}F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle=-\langle\mathrm{D}F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}[\varphi,\dot{G}_{\mu}\ast F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}[\varphi]],\psi\rangle

(7.3)

with the boundary condition
F_{\kappa;\nu,0}[\varphi]=F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi]
, where
\varphi,\psi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
and we used the notation introduced in Sec.
2.1
. The force
F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi]
is a generalization of the macroscopic force
F_{\nu}[\varphi]
given by Eq. (
1.7
). The force
F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi]
involves the regularized noise
\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
obtained by convolving the noise
\varXi_{\nu}
with some smooth kernel. Note that if
\kappa=0
, then the force
F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi]
and the effective force
F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
coincide with the force
F_{\nu}[\varphi]
and the effective force
F_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
at stationarity introduced in Sec.
2.1
. The extra parameter
\kappa\in[0,1]
of the generalized effective force
F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
will play a role in Sec.
9
in which we establish the convergence in probability as
\nu\searrow 0
of the relevant coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=f^{i,m,a}_{0;\nu,\mu}
of the effective force
F_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
using a certain diagonal argument applied previously in a similar context for example in
[
27
,
39
]
.

The results of this section have a preliminary character and rely only on the fact that the realizations of the noise
\varXi_{\nu}
belong to
C(\mathbb{H})=\mathcal{D}^{;0}
for any
\nu\in(0,1]
and the realizations of (a modification of) the white noise
\varXi_{0}
belong to
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{H})\subset\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{H})=\mathcal{D}
for any
\alpha<-\dim(\varXi)
. We construct the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
pathwise for
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
\mu\in[0,1]
and prove that they satisfy some simple bounds.

7.1
Regularized noise and force

Definition 7.1
.

Fix
M\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
such that
\mathrm{supp}\,M\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|_{\mathbb{M}}<1\}
. For
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
the regularized noise is defined by the equality

\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}:=M_{\kappa}\ast\varXi_{\nu}.

(7.4)

Here
M_{0}=\delta_{\mathbb{M}}
and
M_{\kappa}:=\mathbf{S}_{\kappa}M\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
for
\kappa\in(0,1]
, where
\mathbf{S}_{\kappa}
is the rescaling map introduced in Def.
4.2
. For
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
the force
F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi]\in C(\mathbb{M})
is defined by the equality

F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi](x):=\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}(x)+\sum_{(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}}\lambda^{i}\,f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}~\partial^{a_{1}}\varphi(x)\ldots\partial^{a_{m}}\varphi(x),

(7.5)

where the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
, by assumption depend continuously on
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
, are differentiable in the parameter
\kappa
if
\kappa\in(0,1]
and vanish identically unless
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\{0,\ldots,m_{\flat}\}
and
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}^{m}
. The force coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu}\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
are defined by the equality

\langle F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\lambda^{i}\,\langle F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle,

(7.6)

where
\psi,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
. We also set
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu}
, where the polynomial
\mathcal{X}^{m,a}
was introduced in Def.
2.8
. We omit index
\kappa
if
\kappa=0
.

Remark 7.2
.

The force coefficients satisfy the conditions

F_{\kappa;\nu}^{0,0}=\varXi_{\kappa;\nu},\qquad F_{\kappa;\nu}^{i,m,a}=\sum_{b\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}^{m}}\frac{1}{b!}\,\partial^{b}\mathbf{L}^{m}(f^{i,m,a+b}_{\kappa;\nu}),\quad i\in\mathbb{N}_{+},m\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

(7.7)

where the map
\mathbf{L}^{m}
was introduced in Def.
6.2
. The second of the above equalities follows from Lemma
6.4
. We also have
\mathbf{I}(F_{\kappa;\nu}^{i,m,a})=f_{\kappa;\nu}^{i,m,a}
, where the map
\mathbf{I}
was introduced in Def.
6.6
. Let us also remark that all sums in the above definition are finite.

7.2
Effective force and flow equation

In this section we define the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
. For a list of multi-indices
a=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
and a permutation
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
we set
\pi(a):=(a_{\pi(1)},\ldots,a_{\pi(m)})
. Recall that the polynomial
\mathcal{X}^{m,a}
was introduced in Def.
2.8
. We have

\mathcal{X}^{m,a}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\\
=\mathcal{X}^{m,\pi(a)}(x;y_{\pi(1)},\ldots,y_{\pi(m)})

(7.8)

For all
y_{0},z\in\mathbb{M}
it holds

\mathcal{X}^{m,a}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})=\sum_{b,c,d}\frac{a!}{b!c!d!}\mathcal{X}^{1+k,(b_{1+k}+\ldots+b_{m},b_{1},\ldots,b_{k})}(x;y_{0},y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\\
\mathcal{X}^{1,c_{1+k}+\ldots+c_{m}}(y_{0};z)\,\mathcal{X}^{m-k,(d_{1+k},\ldots,d_{m})}(z;y_{1+k},\ldots,y_{m}),

(7.9)

where the sum is over all
b_{p},c_{p},d_{p}
with
p\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
such that
b_{p}=a_{p}
,
c_{p}=0
,
d_{p}=0
for
p\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
and
b_{p}+c_{p}+d_{p}=a_{p}
for
p\in\{1+k,\ldots,m\}
. Throughout the paper we use the following schematic notation for the sums of the above type

\mathcal{X}^{m,a}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\\
=\sum_{b+c+d=a}\frac{a!}{b!c!d!}\mathcal{X}^{1+k,b}(x;y_{0},y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\mathcal{X}^{1,c}(y_{0},z)\mathcal{X}^{m-k,d}(z;y_{1+k},\ldots,y_{m}).

(7.10)

We note that the formula (
7.9
) was obtained by rewriting
(x-y_{p})^{a_{p}}
,
p\in\{1+k,\ldots,m\}
, as

(x-y_{p})^{a_{p}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{p},c_{p},d_{p}\\
b_{p}+c_{p}+d_{p}=a_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{a_{p}!}{b_{p}!c_{p}!d_{p}!}~(x-y_{0})^{b_{p}}(y_{0}-z)^{c_{p}}(z-y_{p})^{d_{p}}.

(7.11)

The effective force coefficients at stationarity
F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
satisfy the following flow equation

\big\langle\partial_{\mu}^{\phantom{i}}F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\,,\,\psi\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{m}\varphi_{q}\big\rangle=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\,(k+1)\\
\times\big\langle F^{j,k+1}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\otimes F^{i-j,m-k}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{k}\varphi_{\pi(q)}\otimes\mathbf{V}\dot{G}_{\mu}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=k+1}^{m}\varphi_{\pi(q)}\big\rangle,

(7.12)

where
\mathbf{V}\dot{G}_{\mu}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M})
is defined by
\mathbf{V}\dot{G}_{\mu}(x,y):=\dot{G}_{\mu}(x-y)
. By the above flow equation and the identity (
7.9
) the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
satisfy the following flow equation

\big\langle\partial_{\mu}^{\phantom{i}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\,,\,\psi\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{m}\varphi_{q}\big\rangle=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\\
\times\big\langle F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\otimes F^{i-j,m-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=1}^{k}\varphi_{\pi(q)}\otimes\mathbf{V}\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\displaystyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\textstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.5}{\hskip 0.0pt$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$\hskip 0.0pt}}}}}}_{q=k+1}^{m}\varphi_{\pi(q)}\big\rangle,

(7.13)

where
\mathbf{V}\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu}=\mathcal{X}^{1,c}\mathbf{V}\dot{G}_{\mu}
for
c\in\mathfrak{M}
. In the above equation we employed the schematic notation specified below Eq. (
7.9
). These flow equations can be written compactly with the use of the map
\mathbf{B}
introduced in Def.
5.27
and the map
\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}
introduced in Def.
5.24
. We have

\partial_{\mu}F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}(k+1)\,\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}_{\mu},F^{j,k+1}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},F^{i-j,m-k}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\big)

(7.14)

and

\partial_{\mu}^{\phantom{i}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\\
=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu},F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},F^{i-j,m-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\big).

(7.15)

Definition 7.3
.

For
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
\mu\in[0,1]
the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, are defined by the flow equation (
7.15
) together with the conditions

F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=0~~\textrm{if}~~m>im_{\flat},\qquad F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,0}=F_{\kappa;\nu}^{i,m,a},\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0},

(7.16)

where the force coefficients
F_{\kappa;\nu}^{i,m,a}
were introduced in Def.
7.1
. We also set
F^{i,m}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}:=F^{i,m,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
and
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}:=\mathbf{I}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
, where the map
\mathbf{I}
was introduced in Def.
6.6
.

Remark 7.4
.

By the properties of the maps
\mathbf{L}^{m}
and
\mathbf{I}
we have
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,0}=f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu\phantom{0}}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

For every
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
are uniquely defined random distributions satisfying the condition
F^{0,0,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
and
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
. Moreover, for any
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
the functions

\displaystyle{}^{2}\ni(\kappa,\mu)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{V}^{m},\quad\nu\in(0,1],

(7.17)

\displaystyle(0,1]\times[0,1]\ni(\kappa,\mu)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{V}^{m},\quad\nu=0,

are almost surely continuous in the full domain and continuously differentiable in the interior of the domain.

Proof.

The effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
are constructed pathwise. The construction is recursive. We first note that
F^{0,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=0
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}_{\sigma}
. Hence,
\partial_{\mu}F^{0,0,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=0
by the flow equation (
7.15
). Consequently,

F^{0,0,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
. Note that almost surely
\varXi_{\nu}\in C(\mathbb{H})
for
\nu\in(0,1]
and
\varXi_{0}\in\mathcal{D}=\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{H})
. Hence for every
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
the functions

\displaystyle\ni\kappa

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathtt{w}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}\in\mathcal{V}=C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{H}),\qquad\nu\in(0,1],

(7.18)

\displaystyle(0,1]\ni\kappa

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathtt{w}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}\in\mathcal{V}=C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{H}),\qquad\nu=0,

are almost surely continuous in the full domain and continuously differentiable in the interior of the domain. Now pick any
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and assume that all
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
with
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
have been constructed. We define
\partial_{\mu}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
with
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
by the RHS of the flow equation (
7.15
). Subsequently,
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
is defined by the equation

F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(7.19)

The continuity and differentiability of the first term on the RHS of the above equality follows from the properties of the force coefficients assumed in Def.
7.1
. The well-definiteness of the second term and its continuity and differentiability properties follow from the support property of the effective force coefficients stated in Lemma
7.6
and the bounds established in Lemma
7.9
. The equality
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
follows from the fact that the coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
and
\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
satisfy the same flow equation (
7.13
) and both coincide with
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m,0}_{\kappa;\nu}
if
\mu=0
.
∎

7.3
Support properties

Lemma 7.6
.

For every
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
and
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
it holds almost surely

\mathrm{supp}\,F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\subset\{(x,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{1+m}\,|\,\mathring{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathring{x}_{m}\in[\mathring{x}-2(i-1)\mu,\mathring{x}]\}.

(7.20)

Proof.

We first note that the lemma is true if
i=0
or
\mu=0
or
m>im_{\flat}
. Next, we fix some
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and assume that the lemma is true for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
such that either
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. We shall prove the lemma for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
such that
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
. To this end, we observe that by the flow equation (
7.13
), the induction hypothesis and
\mathrm{supp}\,\dot{G}_{\mu}^{c}\subset[0,2\mu]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
it holds

\mathrm{supp}\,\partial_{\mu}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\subset\{(x,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{1+m}\,|\,\mathring{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathring{x}_{m}\in[\mathring{x}-2(i-1)\mu,\mathring{x}]\}.

(7.21)

Since

F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(7.22)

this implies the statement of the lemma.
∎

Remark 7.7
.

Since
\mathrm{supp}\,\dot{G}_{\mu}^{c}\subset[0,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
for
\mu\in[0,1]
the above lemma implies that for every
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{v}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,2i]
it holds

\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}^{\phantom{i}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\\
=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu},\mathtt{w}F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},\mathtt{v}F^{i-j,m-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\big).

(7.23)

We will often use the following observation. Fix
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. For any
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
there exists
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{v}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,2i]
and
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{v}-[0,(i-1)i]
.

7.4
Preliminary deterministic analysis

Definition 7.8
.

For
R>0
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we set
R^{i,m}=R^{1+im_{\flat}-m}
.

Lemma 7.9
.

Fix
\nu\in(0,1]
and
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
. Assume that for some
R>1
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and all
\kappa\in[0,1]
,
r\in\{0,1\}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
it holds

\|\mathtt{u}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}^{\phantom{r}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq R\,[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r},\qquad|\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}|\leq R^{i,m}\,[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}.

(7.24)

Then it holds

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\mu]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)s},

(7.25)

for all
\kappa,\mu\in[0,1]
,
r,s\in\{0,1\}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying the condition
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
.
The constants of proportionality in the above bounds depend only on
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and are otherwise universal.

Remark 7.10
.

The proof is very similar to that of the next lemma and is omitted.

Lemma 7.11
.

Fix
\varepsilon,\kappa,\nu\in(0,1]
and
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
. Assume that for some
R>1
and
\delta\in(0,1]
it holds

\begin{gathered}\|\mathtt{u}\varXi_{\kappa;0}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq R,\qquad\|\mathtt{u}(\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}-\varXi_{\kappa;0})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\delta R,\\
|f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0}|\leq R^{i,m},\qquad|f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}-f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0}|\leq\delta R^{i,m}\qquad\textrm{for all}\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}.\end{gathered}

(7.26)

Then:

(A)

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}^{s}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)s}
,

(B)

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}^{s}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)s}
,

(C)

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}^{s}(F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}-F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)s}

for all
\mu\in[0,1]
,
s\in\{0,1\}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying the condition
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
. The constants of proportionality in the above bounds depend only on
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and are otherwise universal.

Remark 7.12
.

A slightly stronger statement is in fact true. Fix
i_{\circ},m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. If the assumption of the above lemma holds for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
i<i_{\circ}
or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
, then the statement holds for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that either (1)
i<i_{\circ}
or (2)
i=i_{\circ}
,
m>m_{\circ}
or (3)
i=i_{\circ}
,
m=m_{\circ}
,
s=1
.

Proof.

We first note that for
m>im_{\flat}
we have
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=0
and the bounds (A), (B), (C) hold trivially. Next, we observe that
F^{0,0,0}_{\kappa;0,\mu}=\varXi_{\kappa;0}
and
F^{0,0,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
for
\mu\in[0,1]
. Recall that for
m=0
it holds
\|v\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}=\|v\|_{\mathcal{V}}
. As a result, the bounds (A), (B), (C) hold true if
i=0
.

Fix
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Assume that the bounds (A), (B), (C) are true for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
such that either
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. We shall prove the bounds for all
(i,m,a)
such that
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
. Fix some
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
. We first observe that by the flow equation (
7.15
) and the support properties of the effective force coefficients stated in Lemma
7.6
it holds

\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}^{\phantom{i}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\\
\times\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu},\mathtt{w}F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},\mathtt{v}F^{i-j,m-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\big),

(7.27)

where
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
is arbitrary such that
\mathtt{v}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,2i]
and
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{v}-[0,(i-1)i]
. Note that Eq. (
7.27
) is also valid for
\nu=0
. Recall that
\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m}=K^{m;0}
. Using Remark
5.33
with
{\mathtt{g}}=0
and
\nu=1
we obtain

\|K^{m;0}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}^{i,m,a}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\|\mathbf{R}\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\\
\times\|K^{k+1;0}\ast\mathtt{w}F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}^{j,k+1,b}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K^{m-k;0}\ast\mathtt{v}F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k,d}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}

(7.28)

and

\|K^{m;0}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}(F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}^{i,m,a}-F_{\kappa;0,\mu}^{i,m,a})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\|\mathbf{R}\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\\
\times\Big(\|K^{k+1;0}\ast\mathtt{w}(F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}^{j,k+1,b}-F_{\kappa;0,\mu}^{j,k+1,b})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K^{m-k;0}\ast\mathtt{v}F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k,d}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}\\[5.69054pt]
+\|K^{k+1;0}\ast\mathtt{w}F_{\kappa;0,\mu}^{j,k+1,b}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K^{m-k;0}\ast\mathtt{v}(F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k,d}-F_{\kappa;0,\mu}^{i-j,m-k,d})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}\Big).

(7.29)

To get the last bound we used the multi-linearity of the map
\mathbf{B}
. Let us also recall that by Lemma
4.23
we have

\|\mathbf{R}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{c}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}

(7.30)

for
c\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}_{\sigma}
. For
s=1
the bounds (A), (B), (C) follow now from the induction hypothesis. For
s=0
and
\mu=0
the bounds (A), (B), (C) follow from the assumed bounds for the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
, the equation

F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}=\sum_{b\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{m}_{\sigma}}\frac{1}{b!}\partial^{b}\mathbf{L}^{m}(f^{i,m,a+b}_{\kappa;\nu}).

(7.31)

and Lemma
6.4
applied with
\nu=1
. To prove the case
s=0
and
\mu\in(0,1]
we use the bounds

\|K^{m;0}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\|K^{m;0}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\|K^{m;0}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,

(7.32)

\|K^{m;0}\ast\mathtt{w}(F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}-F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\|K^{m;0}\ast\mathtt{w}(F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}-F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\\
+\int_{0}^{\mu}\|K^{m;0}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\eta}(F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}-F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0,\eta})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(7.33)

and the results established above.
∎

8
Cumulants of effective force coefficients

In this section we prove uniform bounds for the joint cumulants of the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
at stationarity introduced in Sec.
7
. The bounds are stated in Theorem
8.43
. Their validity relies crucially on certain renormalization conditions that fix the relevant force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
. The results established in this section are used in Sec.
9
to prove uniform bounds for the relevant force coefficient
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=f^{i,m,a}_{0;\nu,\mu}
as well as to show their convergence in probability as
\nu\searrow 0
.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
I=\{1,\ldots,p\}
and
\zeta_{q}
,
q\in I
, be random variables. The joint cumulant of the multi-set
(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I}=(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{p})
is defined by

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{p}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\equiv\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=(-\mathrm{i})^{p}\partial_{t_{1}}\ldots\partial_{t_{p}}\log\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\exp(\mathrm{i}t_{1}\zeta_{1}+\ldots+\mathrm{i}t_{p}\zeta_{p})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\big|_{t_{1}=\ldots=t_{p}=0}.

(8.1)

For example,
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}-\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta_{1}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta_{2}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
I=\{1,\ldots,p\}
and
\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{p},\Phi,\Psi
be random variables. Then

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta_{1}\ldots\zeta_{p}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}{\textstyle\prod}_{q\in I}\zeta_{q}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\sum_{r=1}^{p}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I_{1},\ldots,I_{r}\subset I,\\
I_{1}\cup\ldots\cup I_{r}=I\\
I_{1},\ldots,I_{r}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=1}^{r}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{l}}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}},

(8.2)

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{p}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\sum_{r=1}^{p}(-1)^{r-1}(r-1)!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I_{1},\ldots,I_{r}\subset I,\\
I_{1}\cup\ldots\cup I_{r}=I\\
I_{1},\ldots,I_{r}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\prod_{l=1}^{r}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}{\textstyle\prod}_{q\in I_{l}}\zeta_{q}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}

(8.3)

and

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I},\Phi\Psi\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I},\Phi,\Psi\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I_{1},I_{2}\subset I\\
I_{1}\cup I_{2}=I\end{subarray}}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{1}},\Phi\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}~\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{2}},\Psi\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}.

(8.4)

Remark 8.3
.

For the proof of the above lemma see Proposition 3.2.1 in
[
44
]
.

Definition 8.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
I=\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\zeta_{q}\in\mathcal{D}^{m_{q}}
,
q\in I
, be random distributions. By definition the joint cumulant of the list
(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I}=(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n})
is the distribution

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\equiv\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}},\qquad\mathsf{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n},

(8.5)

defined by the equality

\langle\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}},\psi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\psi_{n}\otimes\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{n}\rangle:=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\langle\zeta_{1},\psi_{1}\otimes\varphi_{1}\rangle,\ldots,\langle\zeta_{n},\psi_{n}\otimes\varphi_{n}\rangle\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}},

(8.6)

where
\psi_{q}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
,
\varphi_{q}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M}^{m_{q}})
,
q\in I
, are arbitrary.

Definition 8.5
.

A list
(i,m,a,s,r)
, where
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}_{\sigma}
,
s,r\in\{0,1\}
is called an index. For
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we call

\mathtt{I}\equiv((i_{1},m_{1},a_{1},s_{1},r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},a_{n},s_{n},r_{n}))

(8.7)

a list of indices. We set

•

n(\mathtt{I}):=n
,

•

i(\mathtt{I}):=i_{1}+\ldots+i_{n}
,

•

\mathsf{m}(\mathtt{I}):=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})
,

•

m(\mathtt{I}):=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}
,

•

a(\mathtt{I}):=[a_{1}]+\ldots+[a_{m}]
,

•

s(\mathtt{I}):=s_{1}+\ldots+s_{n}
,

•

r(\mathtt{I}):=r_{1}+\ldots+r_{n}
.

We use the following notation for the joint cumulants of the effective force coefficients at stationarity

E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}:=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\mu}^{s_{1}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}F^{i_{1},m_{1},a_{1}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},\ldots,\partial_{\mu}^{s_{n}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{n}}F^{i_{n},m_{n},a_{n}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}(\mathtt{I});0}_{\mathrm{t}}.

(8.8)

Remark 8.6
.

Note that if
r(\mathtt{I})=0
and
s(\mathtt{I})=0
, then
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
is well defined for all
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
. If
r(\mathtt{I})=1
or
s(\mathtt{I})=1
, then
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
is well defined if
\kappa\in(0,1]
or
\mu\in(0,1]
, respectively.

Remark 8.7
.

The main result of this section stated in Theorem
8.43
implies that
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
can be bounded uniformly in the space
\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}(\mathtt{I});{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mathrm{t}}
for some
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
depending on the list of indices
\mathtt{I}
.

Definition 8.8
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. For any list of indices
\mathtt{I}
we define
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})\in\mathbb{R}
by the following equality

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})=-n(\mathtt{I})(\dim(\varXi)+\varepsilon)+m(\mathtt{I})(\dim(\varPhi)+\varepsilon)\\
+i(\mathtt{I})(\dim(\lambda)-\varepsilon m_{\flat})+a(\mathtt{I}).

(8.9)

For any
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
we define
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)\in\mathbb{R}
by the following equality

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)=-\dim(\varXi)-\varepsilon+m\,(\dim(\varPhi)+\varepsilon)+i\,(\dim(\lambda)-\varepsilon m_{\flat})+[a].

(8.10)

We also set

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,0),\qquad\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,0)

(8.11)

and omit
\varepsilon
in the notation if
\varepsilon=0
. Moreover, we define

\varepsilon_{\diamond}:=\dim(\varXi)/3\wedge\dim(\lambda)/(m_{\flat}+3)\wedge\min_{(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}}\varrho(i,m,a)/(2+(i_{\diamond}+1)m_{\flat}).

(8.12)

Remark 8.9
.

We have

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{1},m_{1},a_{1})+\ldots+\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{n},m_{n},a_{n}),

for a list of indices
\mathtt{I}
of the form Eq. (
8.7
).

Remark 8.10
.

Observe that the above definition of
\varrho_{0}(i,m,a)=\varrho(i,m,a)
is consistent with Def.
2.4
. Moreover, for any
\varepsilon>0
and
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
such that
m\leq im_{\flat}
it holds
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)<\varrho(i,m,a)
. Similarly, for any
\varepsilon>0
and any list of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
m(\mathtt{I})\leq i(\mathtt{I})m_{\flat}
it holds
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})<\varrho(\mathtt{I})
.

Remark 8.11
.

We claim that
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)>\varepsilon
for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\diamond})
and
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
. Indeed, for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
such that
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\diamond}+1\}
we have

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon=\varrho(i,m,a)-\varepsilon(2+im_{\flat}-m)>\varrho(i,m,a)-\varepsilon_{\diamond}(2+(i_{\diamond}+1)m_{\flat})>0.

(8.13)

Moreover, for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
such that
i>i_{\diamond}+1
it holds

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{\diamond}+1,m,a)+(i-i_{\diamond}-1)(\dim(\lambda)-\varepsilon m_{\flat})\geq\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{\diamond}+1,m,a)>\varepsilon

(8.14)

since
(i_{\diamond}+1,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}_{\sigma}
.

We also claim that
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}>0
for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\diamond})
and all lists of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
\varrho(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}>0
and
m(\mathtt{I})\leq i(\mathtt{I})m_{\flat}
. If
n(\mathtt{I})=1
, then the above statement follows from the previous paragraph. If
n(\mathtt{I})=2
, then
i(\mathtt{I})>0
and

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})+2\dim(\varXi)\geq-2\varepsilon+(\dim(\lambda)-\varepsilon m_{\flat})>0

(8.15)

If
n(\mathtt{I})>2
, then

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}\geq n(\mathtt{I})\,(\dim(\varXi)-\varepsilon)-\mathsf{D}\geq n(\mathtt{I})\,(\dim(\varXi)/3-\varepsilon)>0

(8.16)

Remark 8.12
.

By the above remarks
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)\neq 0
for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
such that
m\leq im_{\flat}
and
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
for all list of indices such that
m(\mathtt{I})\leq i(\mathtt{I})m_{\flat}
.

8.1
Flow equation for cumulants

Lemma 8.13
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
i_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
a_{1}\in\mathfrak{M}^{m_{1}}_{\sigma}
,
r_{1}\in\{0,1\}
and
I\equiv\{2,\ldots,n\}
. For any random distributions
\zeta_{q}\in\mathcal{D}^{m_{q}}
,
q\in I
, we have the following flow equation, in the notation of Def.
8.4
:

\mathopen{\hbox{${\big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\langle}$}}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}F^{i_{1},m_{1},a_{1}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I}\mathclose{\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}}=-\frac{1}{m_{1}!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m_{1}}}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{1}}\sum_{k=0}^{m_{1}}\sum_{r=0}^{r_{1}}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a_{1})}\frac{(k+1)a_{1}!}{b!c!d!}\,\\
\times\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I_{1},I_{2}\subset I\\
I_{1}\cup I_{2}=I\\
I_{1}\cap I_{2}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu},\mathopen{\hbox{${\big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{1}}\mathclose{\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}},\mathopen{\hbox{${\big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}-r}F^{i_{1}-j,m_{1}-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{2}}\mathclose{\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}}\big)\\
+\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{A}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu},\mathopen{\hbox{${\big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I},\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}-r}F^{i_{1}-j,m_{1}-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}}\big)\Bigg).

(8.17)

Proof.

This follows immediately from Eq. (
7.13
) and the relation between cumulants expressed in Eq. (
8.4
).
∎

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and

\mathtt{J}\equiv(\mathtt{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathtt{J}_{n})=((i_{1},m_{1},a_{1},s_{1},r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},a_{n},s_{n},r_{n}))

(8.18)

be a list of indices such that
s_{l}=1
for some
l\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.

(A)

The distribution
E^{\mathtt{J}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}(\mathtt{J})}_{\mathrm{t}}
can be expressed as a linear combination of distributions of the form

\mathbf{Y}^{\omega}\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{A}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu},E^{\mathtt{K}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\big)\qquad\qquad\textrm{or}\qquad\qquad\mathbf{Y}^{\omega}\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu},E^{\mathtt{L}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},E^{\mathtt{M}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\big),

(8.19)

where
c\in\mathfrak{M}
is some multi-index,
\mathtt{K}
,
\mathtt{L}
,
\mathtt{M}
are some lists of indices,
\omega\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
,
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m_{l}}
are some permutations and the maps
\mathbf{Y}^{\omega}
,
\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}
were introduced in Def.
5.24
. The lists of indices
\mathtt{K}
,
\mathtt{L}
,
