\mathtt{M}
satisfy the following conditions

\begin{split}&n(\mathtt{K})=n(\mathtt{J})+1,\\
&i(\mathtt{K})=i(\mathtt{J}),\\
&m(\mathtt{K})=m(\mathtt{J})+1,\\
&a(\mathtt{K})+[c]=a(\mathtt{J}),\\
&s(\mathtt{K})=s(\mathtt{J})-1,\\
&r(\mathtt{K})=r(\mathtt{J}),\\
&\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{J})=\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{K})-\mathsf{D}+\sigma,\end{split}\quad\quad\textrm{or}\qquad\quad\begin{split}&n(\mathtt{L})+n(\mathtt{M})=n(\mathtt{J})+1,\\
&i(\mathtt{L})+i(\mathtt{M})=i(\mathtt{J}),\\
&m(\mathtt{L})+m(\mathtt{M})=m(\mathtt{J})+1,\\
&a(\mathtt{L})+a(\mathtt{M})+[c]=a(\mathtt{J}),\\
&s(\mathtt{L})+s(\mathtt{M})=s(\mathtt{J})-1,\\
&r(\mathtt{L})+r(\mathtt{M})=r(\mathtt{J}),\\
&\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{J})=\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{L})+\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{M})+\sigma.\end{split}

(8.20)

(B)

Fix some
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Suppose that the bound

\|K^{n(\mathtt{I}),m(\mathtt{I});{\mathtt{g}}}_{\kappa\vee\nu,\mu}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}(\mathtt{I})}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r(\mathtt{I})}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\sigma s(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}}

(8.21)

holds uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in[\kappa\vee\nu,1]
for all lists of indices
\mathtt{I}\in\{\mathtt{K},\mathtt{L},\mathtt{M}\}
, where
\mathtt{K},\mathtt{L},\mathtt{M}
are arbitrary lists of indices satisfying the conditions specified in Part (A) given the list of indices
\mathtt{J}
. Then the above bound holds uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in[\kappa\vee\nu,1]
for the list of indices
\mathtt{I}=\mathtt{J}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can assume that
l=1
. Part (A) of the theorem follows from Lemma
8.13
applied with

\zeta_{q}\equiv\partial_{\mu}^{s_{q}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{q}}F^{i_{q},m_{q},a_{q}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},\qquad q\in\{2,\ldots,n\}.

(8.22)

We observe that the multi-index
c\in\mathfrak{M}
and the permutation
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m_{1}}
in the statement of the theorem coincide with the respective objects in the expression (
8.17
). The permutation
\omega\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
is trivial because of the assumption
l=1
. Moreover, it holds

\begin{gathered}\mathtt{K}=((j,k+1,b,0,r),\,\mathtt{J}_{2},\ldots,\mathtt{J}_{n},\,(i_{1}-j,m_{1}-k,d,0,r_{1}-r)),\\
\mathtt{L}=(j,k+1,b,0,r)\sqcup(\mathtt{J}_{q})_{q\in I_{1}},\qquad\mathtt{M}=(i_{1}-j,m_{1}-k,d,0,r_{1}-r)\sqcup(\mathtt{J}_{q})_{q\in I_{2}},\end{gathered}

(8.23)

where
\sqcup
denotes the concatenation of lists,
I_{1}\cup I_{2}=I=\{2,\ldots,n\}
,
I_{1}\cap I_{2}=\emptyset
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,i_{1}\}
,
k\in\{0,\ldots,m_{1}\}
,
b\in\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}^{1+k}
,
d\in\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}^{m-k}
and
r\in\{0,1\}
coincide with the respective objects in the expression (
8.17
). This implies that the lists
\mathtt{K}
,
\mathtt{L}
,
\mathtt{M}
satisfy the conditions specified in Part (A) of the theorem. Only the last of these conditions is nontrivial and it follows from Eq. (
8.9
) and the fact that
\dim(\varXi)+\dim(\varPhi)=\mathsf{d}=\mathsf{D}-\sigma
. Part (B) follows from Part (A), Lemma
5.29
, Lemma
5.30
and the bounds

\|\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{2}}_{\kappa\vee\nu}\mathbf{P}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{a}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{[a]},\qquad\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}_{\kappa\vee\nu}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\dot{G}^{a}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim[\mu]^{[a]-\mathsf{D}}.

(8.24)

uniform in
\nu\in[0,1]
and
\mu\in[\kappa\vee\nu,1]
. These bounds follow immediately from the bounds stated in Remark
5.32
.
∎

Remark 8.15
.

Note that for
\mu\in[\kappa\vee\nu,1]
the bound (
8.21
) coincides with the bound (
8.95
) stated in Theorem
8.43
, which is the main result of Sec.
8
. Unfortunately, the result stated in Part (B) of the above theorem with the bound (
8.21
) replaced by the bound (
8.95
) and
\mu\in[\kappa\vee\nu,1]
replaced by
\mu\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
is in general false even for
{\mathtt{g}}=0
. Let us mention that such a result for
{\mathtt{g}}=0
would be true if the following bounds

\|\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{2}}_{\kappa\vee\nu}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{a}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon+[a]}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma},\quad\|\mathbf{T}|\mathbf{R}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}_{\kappa\vee\nu}\dot{G}^{a}_{\mu}|\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon-\mathsf{D}+[a]}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma},

(8.25)

were satisfied uniformly in
\nu\in[0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
. However, the second of the above bounds does not hold (compare the bounds stated in Remark
5.32
). For the above-mentioned reason the proof of the main bound (
8.95
) for
\mu\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
requires a special treatment and is discussed in Sec.
8.3
.

8.2
Bounds for cumulants of noise

In this section we prove bounds for moments and cumulants of the noises
\varXi_{\nu}
and
\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}=M_{\kappa}\ast\varXi_{\nu}
, which follow from Assumption
3.2
.

Remark 8.16
.

By Assumption
3.2
(C) for any
\nu\in(0,1]
and any compact sets
\mathcal{O}_{1}
,
\mathcal{O}_{2}\subset\mathbb{H}
such that

\inf_{x_{1}\in\mathcal{O}_{1},x_{2}\in\mathcal{O}_{2}}|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{H}}\geq[\nu]=\nu^{1/\sigma}

(8.26)

the
\sigma
-algebras
generated by
\{\varXi_{\nu}(x)\,|\,x\in\mathcal{O}_{1}\}
and
\{\varXi_{\nu}(x)\,|\,x\in\mathcal{O}_{2}\}
are independent. Recall that the distance function
|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}|_{\mathbb{H}}
was introduced in Def.
3.1
.

Lemma 8.17
.

Let
C,c>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
be fixed. Suppose that the kernel
H\in\mathcal{K}
is such that
\mathbf{T}H\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})
and
\|H\|_{\mathcal{K}}\leq 1
and
\|\mathbf{T}H\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\leq c
and the cumulants of a random field
\zeta\in C(\mathbb{H})
satisfy the bound

\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{m-1}}|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\zeta(x_{1}),\zeta(x_{2}),\ldots,\zeta(x_{m})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\,\mathrm{d}x_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{m}\leq c\,C^{m}

(8.27)

for all
m\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Then

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{H}}\,|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(H\ast\zeta(x))^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\leq B_{n}\,c^{n}\,C^{n},

(8.28)

where
B_{n}
is the number of all partitions of the set
\{1,\ldots,n\}
.

Remark 8.18
.

Recall that
\mathbf{T}H
denotes the periodization in space of a kernel
H
, which was introduced in Def.
4.11
. The above lemma follows immediately from the identity
H\ast\zeta=\mathbf{T}H\star\zeta
, where
\star
is the convolution in
\mathbb{H}
, and the relation (
8.2
) between moments and cumulants. Note also that it holds

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{H}}\,|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(H\ast\zeta(x))^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\leq\|H\|_{\mathcal{K}}^{n}\sup_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathbb{H}}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|\zeta(x_{1})|\ldots|\zeta(x_{n})|\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{H}}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|\zeta(x)|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}.

(8.29)

Lemma 8.19
.

Assumption
3.2
implies the following:

(A)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
the bound

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{H}}|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(x))^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\lesssim[\nu]^{-n\dim(\varXi)}

(8.30)

holds uniformly in
\nu\in(0,1]
.

(B)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
the bound

\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n-1}}|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(x_{1}),\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(\mathrm{d}x_{2}),\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(\mathrm{d}x_{n})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\lesssim[\nu]^{n\dim(\varXi)-\mathsf{D}}

(8.31)

holds uniformly in
\nu\in[0,1]
.

(C)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\varepsilon\in[0,1\wedge\sigma]
the bounds

\begin{gathered}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{H}}\,|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}_{\kappa}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}(x))^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\lesssim[\kappa\vee\nu]^{-n\dim(\varXi)},\\
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{H}}\,|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}(\mathbf{P}_{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}(x))^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\lesssim[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)}[\kappa\vee\nu]^{-n\dim(\varXi)-n\varepsilon}\end{gathered}

(8.32)

hold uniformly in
\kappa,\nu\in(0,1]
.

(D)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}\in\{0,1\}
and
\varepsilon\in[0,1\wedge\sigma]
the bound

\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n-1}}|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}K_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}(x_{1}),K_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{2}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}(\mathrm{d}x_{2}),\ldots,K_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{n}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}(\mathrm{d}x_{n})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\\
\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\kappa\vee\nu\vee\mu]^{n\dim(\varXi)-\mathsf{D}-\varepsilon r}

(8.33)

holds uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, where
r=r_{1}+\ldots+r_{n}
.

Remark 8.20
.

Let
\mathsf{m}=(0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
. By the above lemma

\|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu},\ldots,\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{n}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n})}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{-n\dim(\varXi)-\varepsilon r},

(8.34)

\|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu},\ldots,\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{n}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{-n\dim(\varXi)-\varepsilon r+(n-1)\mathsf{D}}

(8.35)

uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
. The extra factor
[\kappa\vee\nu]^{(n-1)\mathsf{D}}
on the RHS of the second bound is due to the fact that by

\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}=M_{\kappa}\ast\varXi_{\nu},\qquad\mathrm{supp}\,M_{\kappa}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|_{\mathbb{M}}<[\kappa]\},

(8.36)

and Remark
8.16
the cumulant
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu},\ldots,\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{n}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\in C(\mathbb{H}^{n})
is not zero only on a subset of
\mathbb{H}^{n}
whose volume is proportional to
[\kappa\vee\nu]^{(n-1)\mathsf{D}}
.

Proof.

First note that if
n=1
, then the lemma holds true trivially since by assumption
\varXi_{\nu}
is centered. The bound (A) follows from Assumption
3.2
(B) and the equality

\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(\mathring{x},\bar{x})=[\nu]^{-\dim(\varXi)}\,(\mathbf{P}\mathrm{\underaccent{\bar}{\Xi}}_{\nu})(\mathring{x}/\nu,\bar{x}/[\nu]).

(8.37)

If
\nu=0
, then
\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}
coincides with the white noise in
\mathbb{H}
and the only non-vanishing cumulant is the covariance. Consequently, the bound (B) for
\nu=0
follows from the equality
n\dim(\varXi)-\mathsf{D}=0
for
n=2
and

\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}}|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\varXi_{0}(x_{1}),\varXi_{0}(\mathrm{d}x_{2})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|=1.

(8.38)

The bound (A) implies that

|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(x_{1}),\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(x_{n})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\lesssim[\nu]^{-n\dim(\varXi)}

(8.39)

holds uniformly in
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathbb{H}
and
\nu\in(0,1]
. By the independence condition stated in Remark
8.16
the cumulant
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(x_{1}),\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(x_{n})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
vanishes unless

|\mathring{x}_{p}-\mathring{x}_{q}|\leq\nu\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad|\bar{x}_{p}^{r}-\bar{x}_{q}^{r}+2\pi k|\leq[\nu]\quad\mathrm{for~some}\quad k\in\mathbb{Z}

(8.40)

for all
r\in\{1,\ldots,\mathsf{d}\}
and
p,q\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
p\neq q
. Using the above mention property together with the bound (
8.39
) we obtain the bound (B) for
\nu\in(0,1]
.

Recall that
\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}=M_{\kappa}\ast\varXi_{\nu}
, where
M_{\kappa}=\mathbf{S}_{\kappa}(M)
. Set
\mathtt{f}=\mathsf{d}+1
. Let
N=\mathbf{P}^{\mathtt{f}+1}M\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{M})
and
N_{\kappa}=\mathbf{S}_{\kappa}(N)
. Thus,
\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}_{\kappa}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}=K_{\kappa}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast N_{\kappa}\ast\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}
. Note that

\|\mathbf{T}K_{\kappa}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\lesssim[\kappa]^{-\mathsf{D}}
by Lemma
4.13
(D) and
\|K_{\kappa}\|_{\mathcal{K}},\|N_{\kappa}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim 1
. As a result, the first of the bounds (C) follows for
\kappa\leq\nu
from the bound (A) and for
\kappa>\nu
from the bound (B), Lemma
8.17
and the fact that
\varXi_{\nu}
is centered. The second of the bounds (C) follows by the same argument since

\mathbf{P}_{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}=\mathbf{P}_{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}M_{\kappa}\ast\varXi_{\nu}=\mathbf{P}_{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}(K^{\ast(\mathtt{f}+1)}_{\kappa}\ast N_{\kappa})\ast\varXi_{\nu}\\
=K^{\ast\mathtt{f}}_{\kappa}\ast(\partial_{\kappa}N_{\kappa}+(\mathtt{f}+1)N_{\kappa}\ast\mathbf{P}_{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}K_{\kappa})\ast K_{\nu}\ast\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}

(8.41)

and for
\varepsilon\in[0,1\wedge\sigma]

\|(\partial_{\kappa}N_{\kappa}+(\mathtt{f}+1)N_{\kappa}\ast\mathbf{P}_{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}K_{\kappa})\ast K_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}[\kappa\vee\nu]^{-\varepsilon}

(8.42)

uniformly in
\kappa,\nu\in(0,1]
by Lemma
4.16
and Lemma
4.18
.

In order to show the bound (D) observe that we have

K_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}=\partial_{\kappa}^{r}M_{\kappa}\ast K_{\nu}\ast K_{\mu}\ast\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}.

(8.43)

and by Lemma
4.18
it holds

\|\partial_{\kappa}^{r}M_{\kappa}\ast K_{\nu}\ast K_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\kappa\vee\nu\vee\mu]^{-\varepsilon r}

(8.44)

for
r\in\{0,1\}
. The bound (D) follows now from the estimate

\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n-1}}|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}K_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}(x_{1}),K_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}(\mathrm{d}x_{2}),\ldots,K_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{n}}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}(\mathrm{d}x_{n})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\\
\leq\|\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}M_{\kappa}\ast K_{\nu}\ast K_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\ldots\|\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{n}}M_{\kappa}\ast K_{\nu}\ast K_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\\
\times\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n-1}}|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(x_{1}),\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(\mathrm{d}x_{2}),\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}(\mathrm{d}x_{n})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|,

(8.45)

the bound (B) and the fact that
\varXi_{\nu}
is centered.
∎

8.3
Preliminary bounds

The aim of this section is to establish an auxiliary result that will be used in Sec.
8.5
to prove the bounds (
8.95
) for the cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
in the range of parameters
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
. This range of parameters requires a special treatment because of the issue mentioned in Remark
8.15
. In contrast to the proof of the bounds (
8.95
) in the full range of parameters, which is the main result of Sec.
8
, the analysis of this subsection does not involve any fine tuning (or renormalization) of the force coefficients. All the bounds proved in this subsection are straightforward in nature and morally follow from dimensional analysis. Unfortunately, to establish them rigorously we need to introduce several new objects.

Recall that the fractional heat kernel
G_{\mu}
with UV cutoff
\mu\in(0,\infty)
was introduced in Def.
4.19
. Our analysis so far was based on the decomposition of scales expressed by the first equality below

G-G_{\mu}=\int_{0}^{\mu}\dot{G}_{\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta=\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}G_{\kappa\vee\nu;\eta,0}\,\mathrm{d}\eta+\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}G_{\kappa\vee\nu;\mu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(8.46)

In this section we use a more sophisticated decomposition expressed by the second equality. The kernel
\partial_{\eta}G_{\kappa\vee\nu;\eta,0}
have compact support and the force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu,0}
, which are defined as in Sec.
7
but with the kernel
\dot{G}_{\mu}
replaced by
\partial_{\eta}G_{\kappa\vee\nu;\eta,0}
, satisfy a certain independence condition similar to the independence condition of the noise
\varXi_{\nu}
stated in Remark
8.16
. This allows us to exploit an argument similar to the one mentioned in Remark
8.20
in the proof of bounds for cumulants of
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu,0}
. We note that the original effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
can be obtained by integrating the RHS of a certain flow equation involving the kernel
\partial_{\eta}G_{\kappa\vee\nu;\mu,\eta}
with the initial condition coinciding with
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu,0}
. The kernels
\partial_{\eta}G_{\kappa\vee\nu;\mu,\eta}
are more regular at spatial scales of order
[\kappa\vee\nu]
and in particular satisfy the bounds stated in Remark
8.15
. Recall that the second of these bounds is violated by the kernel
\dot{G}_{\mu}
, which is the reason why the analysis presented in this section is needed.

Definition 8.21
.

For
\nu\in(0,\infty)
,
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in[0,\infty)
we define

G_{\nu;{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}(\mathring{x},\bar{x}):=G_{{\tilde{\mu}}}(\mathring{x},\bar{x})\,(1-\chi(|\bar{x}|^{\sigma}/\nu))+G_{{\mu}}(\mathring{x},\bar{x})\,\chi(|\bar{x}|^{\sigma}/\nu).

(8.47)

where
G_{\mu}
is the fractional heat kernel with the cutoff
\mu\in[0,\infty)
introduced in Def.
4.19
and the function
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is such that
\chi(t)=0
for
t\leq 1
and
\chi(t)=1
for
t\geq 2
was fixed in Def.
4.19
. For
\mu\in(0,\infty)
and
a\in\mathfrak{M}
we set

\begin{gathered}\dot{G}_{\nu;\mu,0}:=\partial_{\mu}G_{\nu;\mu,0},\qquad\dot{G}_{\nu;0,\mu}:=\partial_{\mu}G_{\nu;\mu,0},\\
\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;\mu,0}:=\mathcal{X}^{a}\dot{G}_{\nu;\mu,0},\qquad\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\mu}:=\mathcal{X}^{a}\dot{G}_{\nu;0,\mu},\end{gathered}

(8.48)

where
\mathcal{X}^{a}(x)=x^{a}
.

Remark 8.22
.

Note that
G_{\nu;\mu,\mu}=G_{\mu}
and
\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;\mu,0}+\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\mu}=\dot{G}^{a}_{\mu}
. Moreover, for all
\nu\in[0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,\nu]
we have

\mathrm{supp}\,\dot{G}_{\nu;\mu,0}^{a}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,\mathring{x}\in[\mu,2\mu],|\bar{x}|\leq 2[\nu]\}.

(8.49)

Lemma 8.23
.

For any
a\in\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}
and
r\in\{0,1\}
it holds

(A)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;\mu,0}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\nu]^{\sigma-\sigma r-\varepsilon+[a]}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}
,

(B)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\nu]^{\sigma-\sigma r-\varepsilon+[a]}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}
,

(C)

\|\mathbf{T}|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\mu}|\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim[\nu]^{\sigma-\sigma r-\varepsilon-\mathsf{D}}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}

uniformly in
\nu\in[0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,\nu)
, where
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}
,
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}=\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{M})}
, the periodization
\mathbf{T}
was introduced in Def.
4.11
and
\mathsf{D}=\mathsf{d}+\sigma
.

Remark 8.24
.

The bound (C) does not hold for the kernel
\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;\mu,0}
. Only this bound does:

\|\mathbf{T}|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;\mu,0}|\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim[\nu]^{\sigma-\sigma r-\varepsilon}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma-\mathsf{D}}.

Proof.

First note that the bound (A) follows from the bound (B) by the equality
\dot{G}_{\nu;\mu,0}=\dot{G}^{a}_{\mu}-\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;\mu,0}
and Lemma
4.23
. For any spatial multi-index
b\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}
it holds

\begin{gathered}\partial^{b}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\mu}=[\nu]^{[a]-|b|-\sigma r}\,\mathbf{S}_{\nu}\big(\partial^{b}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\tau}|_{\nu=1,\tau=\mu/\nu}\big),\end{gathered}

(8.50)

where the rescaling operator
\mathbf{S}_{\nu}
was introduced in Def.
4.2
and
\partial^{b}\equiv\partial^{b}_{x}
. Using the properties of the function
\chi
and Remark
4.21
we obtain that for
\nu=1
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}
,
b\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}
,
r\in\{0,1\}
the bound

|\partial^{b}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\tau}(\mathring{x},\bar{x})|=|\mathring{x}/\tau^{2}\,\dot{\chi}(\mathring{x}/\tau)\,\partial^{b}x^{a}G(x)\,\partial_{\nu}^{r}(1-\chi(|\bar{x}|^{\sigma}/\nu))|\\
\lesssim|\mathring{x}|/\tau^{2}\,1_{[\tau,2\tau]}(\mathring{x})\,|\mathring{x}|^{1+\mathring{a}}\,|x|_{\mathbb{M}}^{|\bar{a}|-\sigma-\mathsf{d}}\,1_{[1,\infty)}(|\bar{x}|)\lesssim 1_{[\tau,2\tau]}(\mathring{x})\,1_{[1,\infty)}(|\bar{x}|)\,|\bar{x}|^{|\bar{a}|-\sigma-\mathsf{d}},

(8.51)

where
\dot{\chi}(t)=\partial_{t}\chi(t)
, holds uniformly in
\tau\in(0,1]
and
x=(\mathring{x},\bar{x})\in\mathbb{M}
. This bound implies that for
\nu=1
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}
,
b\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}
,
r\in\{0,1\}
the bounds

\|\partial^{b}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\tau}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim 1,\qquad\sup_{\mathring{x}\in\mathbb{R}}\|\partial^{b}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\tau}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})}\lesssim 1

(8.52)

hold uniformly in
\tau\in(0,1]
. As a result, using the equality
\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\nu}=1-[\nu]^{2}\Delta_{\bar{x}}
and the properties of the rescaling operator
\mathbf{S}_{\nu}
we obtain that for any
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the bounds

\begin{gathered}\|\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}_{\nu;0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\nu]^{[a]-\sigma r}\qquad\sup_{\mathring{x}\in\mathbb{R}}\|\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\mu}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})}\lesssim[\nu]^{[a]-\sigma r-\sigma}\end{gathered}

(8.53)

hold uniformly in
\nu\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,\nu)
. The bounds (B) and (C) follow now from Lemma
4.13
(C), (D) and the bounds

\begin{gathered}\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\leq\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\bar{K}_{\nu}^{\ast\mathtt{h}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})}\,\|\bar{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathtt{h}}_{\nu}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}},\\
\|\mathbf{T}|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}^{a}_{\nu;0,\mu}|\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\|\mathbf{T}\bar{K}_{\nu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\bar{K}_{\nu}^{\ast\mathtt{h}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})}\,\sup_{\mathring{x}\in\mathbb{R}}\|\bar{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathtt{f}+\mathtt{h}}_{\nu}\partial_{\nu}^{r}\dot{G}_{\nu;0,\mu}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})}\end{gathered}

(8.54)

applied with
\mathtt{f}=\mathsf{d}+1
and
\mathtt{h}=\lfloor\sigma/2\rfloor+1
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 8.25
.

For
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in[0,1]
the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, are defined by the flow equations

\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{\phantom{i}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\\
\times\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;{\tilde{\mu}},0},F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0},F^{i-j,m-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}\big),

(8.55)

\partial_{{\mu}}^{\phantom{i}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\\
\times\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;0,{\mu},},F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}},F^{i-j,m-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}\big).

(8.56)

together with the conditions

F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}=0~~\textrm{if}~~m>im_{\flat},\qquad F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,0,0}=F_{\kappa;\nu}^{i,m,a},\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0},

(8.57)

where the force coefficients
F_{\kappa;\nu}^{i,m,a}
were introduced in Def.
7.1
.

Remark 8.26
.

For every
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in[0,1]
the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, are uniquely defined random distributions. Their construction is pathwise and similar to the recursive construction described in the proof of Lemma
7.5
. At each step of the recursion we first define
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}
with the use of the flow equation (
8.55
) with the initial condition
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,0,0}=F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
. Next, we define
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}
using the flow equation (
8.56
) with the initial condition
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}
that has already been constructed above.

Remark 8.27
.

Let us make a few crucial observation based on Remark
2.2
. First, note that it holds

F_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}[\varphi]=F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi+(G-G_{\kappa\vee\nu;{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}})\ast F_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}[\varphi]]

(8.58)

In particular, because
G_{\kappa\vee\nu;\mu,\mu}=G_{\mu}
we get

F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu,\mu}[\varphi]=F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi+(G-G_{\mu})\ast F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu,\mu}[\varphi]].

(8.59)

Since the above equation defines the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu,\mu}
uniquely and since the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
satisfy

F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}[\varphi]=F_{\kappa;\nu}[\varphi+(G-G_{\mu})\ast F_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}[\varphi]]

(8.60)

we obtain that

F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu,\mu},\qquad\partial_{\mu}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},\mu}\big|_{{\tilde{\mu}}=\mu}+\partial_{\mu}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},\mu}\big|_{{\tilde{\mu}}=\mu}

(8.61)

for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
.

We start our analysis of the properties of the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}
with the following deterministic lemma.

Remark 8.28
.

Recall that for
R>0
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we set
R^{i,m}=R^{1+im_{\flat}-m}
.

Lemma 8.29
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
(\nu,\kappa)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
. Assume that for some
R>1
and all
r\in\{0,1\}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
it holds

\begin{gathered}\|\mathtt{u}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}^{\phantom{r}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq R\,[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{-\dim(\varXi)-2\varepsilon},\\
|\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}|\leq R^{i,m}\,[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}.\end{gathered}

(8.62)

Then it holds

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m}_{\kappa\vee\nu})\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{{\tilde{s}}}\partial_{{\mu}}^{{s}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\\
\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[{\tilde{\mu}}]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma){\tilde{s}}}\,[{\mu}]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma){s}}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon{\tilde{s}}-\varepsilon{s}},

(8.63)

for all
r,{\tilde{s}},{s}\in\{0,1\}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
,
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
and all
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying the condition
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
. The constants of proportionality in the above bounds depend only on
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and are otherwise universal.

Proof.

The proof uses the strategy of the proof of Lemma
7.11
and is also by induction on
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. In the inductive step we first prove the statement for: (1)
{\tilde{s}}=1
,
{s}=0
,
{\tilde{\mu}}\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
,
{\mu}=0
and (2)
{\tilde{s}}\in\{0,1\}
,
{s}=1
,
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
using the induction hypothesis, the flow equations

\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{\phantom{i}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{u+v+w=r}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\\
\times\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\partial_{\kappa}^{u}\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;{\tilde{\mu}},0},\partial_{\kappa}^{v}F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0},\partial_{\kappa}^{w}F^{i-j,m-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}\big),

(8.64)

\partial_{{\mu}}^{\phantom{i}}\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{{\tilde{s}}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{\tilde{v}+\tilde{w}={\tilde{s}}}\sum_{u+v+w=r}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\\
\times\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\partial_{\kappa}^{u}\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;0,{\mu}},\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{\tilde{v}}\partial_{\kappa}^{v}F^{j,k+1,b}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0},\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{\tilde{w}}\partial_{\kappa}^{w}F^{i-j,m-k,d}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}\big),

(8.65)

the bounds for
\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;{\tilde{\mu}},0}
and
\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;0,{\mu}}
established in Lemma
8.23
(A), (B) as well as Remark
5.33
applied with
{\mathtt{g}}=0
. The statement for
{\tilde{s}}={s}=0
,
{\tilde{\mu}}={\mu}=0
follows from the assumed bounds for the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
, an analog of Eq. (
7.31
) as well as the Lemma
6.4
. To prove the statement for
{\tilde{s}}={s}=0
,
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
we use the results established so far, the equations

F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}=F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}+\int_{0}^{{\tilde{\mu}}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta,0}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,\qquad F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}=F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}+\int_{0}^{{\mu}}\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,

(8.66)

and the estimate

\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\rho-\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\leq\sigma/\varepsilon~[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\rho}

(8.67)

valid for
\mu\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
and any
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
.
∎

Lemma 8.30
.

For every
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
{\tilde{\mu}}\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
and
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
it holds almost surely

\mathrm{supp}\,F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}\subset\big\{(x,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{1+m}\,\big|\,\forall_{p\in\{1,\ldots,m\}}|x_{p}-x|_{\mathbb{M}}\leq 2(i-1)\,[\nu\vee\kappa]\big\}.

(8.68)

Remark 8.31
.

The proof of the above lemma is similar to the proof of Lemma
7.6
and is based on the fact that for
{\tilde{\mu}}\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
the kernel
\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;{\tilde{\mu}},0}
has the following support property

\mathrm{supp}\,\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;{\tilde{\mu}},0}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|_{\mathbb{M}}\leq 2\,[\kappa\vee\nu]\}.

(8.69)

Definition 8.32
.

A list
(i,m,a,{\tilde{s}},{s},r)
, where
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}_{\sigma}
,
{\tilde{s}},{s},r\in\{0,1\}
is called an extended index. For
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we call

\mathtt{I}\equiv((i_{1},m_{1},a_{1},{\tilde{s}}_{1},{s}_{1},r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},a_{n},{\tilde{s}}_{n},{s}_{n},r_{n}))

(8.70)

a list of extended indices. The quantities
n(\mathtt{I})
,
i(\mathtt{I})
,
\mathsf{m}(\mathtt{I})
,
m(\mathtt{I})
,
a(\mathtt{I})
,
s(\mathtt{I})
,
r(\mathtt{I})
are as defined in Def.
8.5
and
{\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{I})={\tilde{s}}_{1}+\ldots+{\tilde{s}}_{n}
. We introduce the following notation for the joint cumulants of the effective force coefficients

E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}:=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{{\tilde{s}}_{1}}\partial_{{\mu}}^{{s}_{1}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}F^{i_{1},m_{1},a_{1}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}},\ldots,\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{{\tilde{s}}_{n}}\partial_{{\mu}}^{{s}_{n}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{n}}F^{i_{n},m_{n},a_{n}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}(\mathtt{I});0}_{\mathrm{t}},

(8.71)

where we used the notation introduced in Def.
8.4
.

The following lemma is an analog of Lemma
8.14
for cumulants introduced in the above definition. Note that in the lemma the parameter
{\tilde{\mu}}
plays a similar role to the parameter
\kappa
. The proof of the lemma relies on the flow equation in the parameter
{\mu}
.

Lemma 8.33
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and

\mathtt{J}\equiv(\mathtt{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathtt{J}_{n})=((i_{1},m_{1},a_{1},{\tilde{s}}_{1},,{s}_{1},r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},a_{n},{\tilde{s}}_{n},{s}_{n},r_{n}))

(8.72)

be a list of extended indices such that
{s}_{l}=1
for some
l\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.

(A)

The distribution
E^{\mathtt{J}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}
can be expressed as a linear combination of distributions of the form

\mathbf{Y}^{\omega}\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{A}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;0,\mu},E^{\mathtt{K}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}\big)\quad\textrm{or}\quad\mathbf{Y}^{\omega}\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;0,\mu},E^{\mathtt{L}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}},E^{\mathtt{M}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}\big),

(8.73)

where the objects appearing above satisfy all the conditions specified in Part (A) of Lemma
8.14
and
{\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{K})={\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{J})
,
{\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{L})+{\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{M})={\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{J})
.

(B)

Suppose that the bound (
8.77
), stated in the lemma below, holds uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
for all lists of indices
\mathtt{I}\in\{\mathtt{K},\mathtt{L},\mathtt{M}\}
, where
\mathtt{K},\mathtt{L},\mathtt{M}
are arbitrary lists of indices satisfying the conditions mentioned in Part (A) given the list of indices
\mathtt{J}
. Then the above-mentioned bound holds uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
for the list of indices
\mathtt{I}=\mathtt{J}
.

Proof.

The claim (A) is proved using the fact that the cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}
satisfy a flow equation analogous to the flow equation (
8.17
). The claim (B) is a consequence of Lemma
5.29
, Lemma
5.30
applied with
{\mathtt{g}}=0
and the bounds for
\dot{G}^{c}_{\kappa\vee\nu;0,\mu}
proved in Lemma
8.23
(B), (C).
∎

Lemma 8.34
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\sigma})
. Suppose that the family of noises
\varXi_{\nu}
,
\nu\in[0,1]
, satisfies Assumption
3.2
and the force coefficients satisfy the bounds

|\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}|\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)},\qquad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I},

(8.74)

uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
.

(A)

Let
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{H}
be an open bounded region. The random variable
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
restricted to the region
\mathcal{O}\times\mathbb{M}^{m}\subset\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}^{m}
, is measurable with respect to the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\{\varXi(z)\,|\,z\in\mathcal{O}^{i}_{\kappa,\nu}\}
, where

\mathcal{O}^{i}_{\kappa,\nu}:=\{z\in\mathbb{H}\,|\,\exists_{x\in\overline{\mathcal{O}}}~|z-x|_{\mathbb{H}}<[\kappa]+2i\,[\kappa\vee\nu]\,\},\qquad i\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\kappa,\nu\in[0,1].

(8.75)

(B)

For all
r,{\tilde{s}},{s}\in\{0,1\}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
the following bound

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m}_{\kappa\vee\nu})\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{{\tilde{s}}}\partial_{{\mu}}^{{s}}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\\
\lesssim\,[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[{\tilde{\mu}}]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma){\tilde{s}}}\,[{\mu}]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma){s}}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{n\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-n\varepsilon{\tilde{s}}-n\varepsilon{s}},

(8.76)

holds uniformly in
(\nu,\kappa)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
.

(C)

For every list of extended indices
\mathtt{I}
the following bound

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes n(\mathtt{I})}\otimes J_{\kappa\vee\nu}^{\otimes m(\mathtt{I})})\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\\
\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r(\mathtt{I})}\,[{\tilde{\mu}}]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma){\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{I})}[{\mu}]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma){s}(\mathtt{I})}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\varepsilon{\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{I})-\varepsilon{s}(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}}

(8.77)

holds uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
.

Remark 8.35
.

The following slightly stronger statement is in fact true. Fix
i_{\circ},m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. If the assumption of the above lemma holds for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
i<i_{\circ}
or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
, then the statement holds for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and all lists of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
i=\mathrm{i}(\mathtt{I})
,
m=m(\mathtt{I})
and either (1)
i<i_{\circ}
or (2)
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
or (3)
i=i_{\circ}
,
m=m_{\circ}
,
s=1
.

Remark 8.36
.

Part (B) of Lemma
8.34
applied with
{\tilde{\mu}}={\mu}
and Eq. (
8.61
) together imply that for all
r,s\in\{0,1\}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
the following bound

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m}_{\kappa\vee\nu})\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\partial_{\mu}^{s}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\\
\lesssim\,[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\mu]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)s}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{n\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-n\varepsilon s}

(8.78)

holds uniformly in
(\nu,\kappa)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
.

Remark 8.37
.

Part (C) of Lemma
8.34
and Eq. (
8.61
) together imply that for all lists of indices
\mathtt{I}
the following bound

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes n(\mathtt{I})}\otimes J_{\kappa\vee\nu}^{\otimes m(\mathtt{I})})\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\\
\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r(\mathtt{I})}\,[\mu]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)s(\mathtt{I})}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\varepsilon s(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}}

(8.79)

holds uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
, where
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
was introduced in Def.
8.5
.

Proof.

Part (A) is proved by induction on
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
using a strategy similar to the strategy of the proof of Lemma
7.6
. Note that
\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}=M_{\kappa}\ast\varXi_{\nu}
, where
\mathrm{supp}\,M_{\kappa}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|_{\mathbb{M}}<[\kappa]\}
. This implies Part (A) for
i=0
. To prove Part (A) for
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we use the flow equation (
8.55
) and the support property of the effective force coefficients established in Lemma
8.30
.

To prove Part (B) note that
F^{0,0,0}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}=\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
is the only non-zero effective force coefficient
F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}
with
i=0
. As a result, for
i=0
the bound stated in Part (B) follows from the result of Lemma
9.6
, which relies only on Lemma
8.19
(C). This together with the assumed bound for the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
implies that for any
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})

the assumption of Lemma
8.29
is satisfied with some random constant
R=R_{\kappa;\nu}(\mathtt{u})
such that
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}R_{\kappa;\nu}^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim 1
uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Consequently, Part (B) follows from Lemma
8.29
.

Let us turn to the proof of Part (C). First note that Part (B) implies that for any
\mathtt{w}_{p}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
,
(i_{p},m_{p},a_{p})\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
,
{\tilde{s}}_{p},r_{p}\in\{0,1\}
it holds

\mathopen{\hbox{${\big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\langle}$}}\textstyle\prod_{p=1}^{n}\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J_{\nu}^{\otimes m_{p}})\ast\mathtt{w}_{p}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{p}}\partial_{{\tilde{\mu}}}^{{\tilde{s}}_{p}}F^{i_{p},m_{p},a_{p}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m_{p}}}\mathclose{\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\big\rangle}$}}\\
\lesssim\prod_{p=1}^{n}\,[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r_{p}}[{\tilde{\mu}}]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma){\tilde{s}}_{p}}[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i_{p},m_{p},a_{p})-\varepsilon{\tilde{s}}_{p}}

(8.80)

uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
{\tilde{\mu}}\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
. By the Fubini theorem, the relation (
8.3
) between cumulants and moments and Def.
5.12
of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
it holds

\int_{\mathbb{M}^{m}}|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}V_{1}(x_{1};\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{1}),\ldots,V_{n}(x_{n};\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\lesssim\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|V_{1}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m_{1}}}\ldots\|V_{2}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m_{n}}}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}

(8.81)

for any
V_{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{m_{1}},\ldots,V_{n}\in\mathcal{V}^{m_{n}}
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathbb{M}
, where
m=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}
and the constant of proportionality depends only on
n
(note that the expression on the LHS of the above inequality involves the cumulant and the expression on the RHS is the expectation of a product). Using the above bounds we obtain for any list
\mathtt{I}
of extended indices such that
{s}(\mathtt{I})=0
and any
T>0
the bound

\int_{\mathbb{M}^{m(\mathtt{I})}}|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes n(\mathtt{I})}\otimes J_{\kappa\vee\nu}^{\otimes m(\mathtt{I})})\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{y}_{n})|\\
\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r(\mathtt{I})}[{\tilde{\mu}}]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma){\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{I})}[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\varepsilon{\tilde{s}}(\mathtt{I})}

(8.82)

uniform over
(\nu,\kappa)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
{\tilde{\mu}}\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in[-T,T]\times\mathbb{T}
.

Next, observe that Part (A) implies that given two regions
\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{2}\subset\mathbb{H}
the random variables
F^{i_{1},m_{1},a_{1}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}\in\mathcal{D}^{m_{1};0}
and
F^{i_{2},m_{2},a_{2}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}\in\mathcal{D}^{m_{2};0}
restricted to
\mathcal{O}_{1}\times\mathbb{M}^{m_{1}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{2}\times\mathbb{M}^{m_{2}}
, respectively, are independent if
|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{H}}>2\,[\kappa]+2(i_{1}+i_{2})\,[\kappa\vee\nu]
for all
x_{1}\in\mathcal{O}_{1}
and
x_{2}\in\mathcal{O}_{2}
. In consequence, the cumulant
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}
vanishes outside the region

\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{n+m}\,|\,\forall_{p,q\in\{1,\ldots,n\},p\neq q}\,|x_{p}-x_{q}|_{\mathbb{H}}\leq 2(i(\mathtt{I})+1)\,[\kappa\vee\nu]\}.

(8.83)

This together with the bound (
8.82
) and translational invariance of
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}
implies Part (C) for
{\mu}=0
and any list of extended indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
{s}(\mathtt{I})=0
.

We prove Part (C) for
{\mu}\in[0,\kappa\vee\nu]
by induction. The statement is clearly true for all list of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
m(\mathtt{I})>i(\mathtt{I})m_{\flat}
since then
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}=0
. It is also true if
i(\mathtt{I})=0
as then
m(\mathtt{I})=0
for non-zero cumulants and
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}
coincides with a cumulant of
\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
. In particular, it is independent of
{\mu}
and vanishes if
{s}(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
. Hence, for all lists of extended indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
i(\mathtt{I})=0
the bound stated in Part (C) is implied by the special case of this bound for
{\mu}=0
, which was proved in the previous paragraph. Fix
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Assume that the statement is true for all lists of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that either
i(\mathtt{I})<i_{\circ}
, or
i(\mathtt{I})=i_{\circ}
and
m(\mathtt{I})>m_{\circ}
. We shall prove the statement for all
\mathtt{I}
such that
i(\mathtt{I})=i_{\circ}
and
m(\mathtt{I})=m_{\circ}
. We first consider the case
{s}(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
. Note that by Lemma
8.33
(A) the cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}
can be expressed in terms of the cumulants for which Part (C) has already been established. Hence, the statement with
s(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
is a consequence of the inductive assumption and Lemma
8.33
(B). To prove the statement for lists of extended indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
s(\mathtt{I})=0
we use the equality

E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}=E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}+\sum_{q=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{{\mu}}E^{\mathtt{I}_{q}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,

(8.84)

where

\mathtt{I}_{q}=((i_{1},m_{1},a_{1},{\tilde{s}}_{1},0,r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{q},m_{q},a_{q},{\tilde{s}}_{q},1,r_{q}),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},a_{n},{\tilde{s}}_{n},0,r_{n})).

(8.85)

The claim follows from the bound

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes n(\mathtt{I})}\otimes J_{\kappa\vee\nu}^{\otimes m(\mathtt{I})})\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},{\mu}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes n(\mathtt{I})}\otimes J_{\kappa\vee\nu}^{\otimes m(\mathtt{I})})\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},0}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\\
+\sum_{q=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{{\mu}}\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}^{\otimes n(\mathtt{I})}\otimes J_{\kappa\vee\nu}^{\otimes m(\mathtt{I})})\ast E^{\mathtt{I}_{q}}_{\kappa;\nu,{\tilde{\mu}},\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(8.86)

as well as the statement with
{\mu}=0
or
{s}(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
, which has already been proved above.
∎

8.4
Renormalization conditions

Assumption 8.38
.

Let us fix constants
i_{\flat},m_{\flat}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that
\varrho(i_{\flat}+1)>0
and
\varrho(1,m_{\flat}+1)>0
and a family of continuous functions
[0,1]\ni\nu\mapsto\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
such that
|\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu}|\lesssim[\nu]^{\varrho(i,m,a)-\varepsilon}
uniformly in
\nu\in[0,1]
for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
vanishes identically unless
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\{0,\ldots,m_{\flat}\}
and
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{m}_{\sigma}
. We assume the following.

(A)

The irrelevant force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
, satisfy the conditions

f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}=f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}=\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu},\qquad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+},

(8.87)

for all
(\nu,\kappa)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
.

(B)

The relevant force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
, are chosen in such a way that the conditions

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,1}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,1}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu},\qquad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-},

(8.88)

hold for all
(\nu,\kappa)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
, where the effective force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,1}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
, are defined in terms of the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
as specified in Def.
7.3
.

Remark 8.39
.

Note that
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
implies in particular that
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\{0,\ldots,m_{\flat}\}
and
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}
. Moreover, observe that if
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
, then
\varrho(i,m,a)<0
and the bound
|\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu}|\lesssim[\nu]^{\varrho(i,m,a)-\varepsilon}
uniform in
\nu\in[0,1]
holds trivially for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
by the assumed continuity of the function
\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}
. Furthermore, if
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
, then
\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{0}=0
.

Remark 8.40
.

The conditions (
8.88
) are called the renormalization conditions and the parameters
\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
,
\nu\in[0,1]
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
, are called the renormalization parameters.

Remark 8.41
.

We shall prove that given
\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
, as in the above assumption there exist a unique choice of the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
, such that the conditions (A) and (B) are satisfied. We show that the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
, depend continuously on
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
, are differentiable in the parameter
\kappa
for
\kappa\in(0,1]
, vanish identically unless
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\{0,\ldots,m_{\flat}\}
and
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}
and satisfy the bound assumed in Lemma
8.34
. Note that the irrelevant force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
, do not depend on the parameter
\kappa\in[0,1]
. In general this is not the case for the relevant coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
.

8.5
Uniform bounds

We now proceed to the prove of the uniform bounds for the cumulants of the effective force coefficients. The bounds are stated in Theorem
8.43
. In the proof of this theorem we use the following simple lemma.

Lemma 8.42
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\mathsf{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
,
m=m_{1}+\ldots+m
,
\epsilon>0
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
and
(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto V_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{\mathsf{m}}
be a family of integrable functions parameterized by
\nu\in[0,1]
. Assume that for some
C>0
,
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\nu\in[0,1]
and all
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds

\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq C\,[\mu]^{\epsilon-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\rho-\epsilon}.

(8.89)

Set
U_{\nu,\mu}:=\int_{0}^{\mu}V_{\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta
.

(A)

For all
\mu\in[0,\nu]
it holds

\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast U_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq C\,\sigma/\epsilon\,[\mu]^{\epsilon}\,[\nu]^{\rho-\epsilon}\leq C\,\sigma/\epsilon\,[\nu]^{\rho}.

(8.90)

(B)

If
\rho>0
, then for all
\mu\in[0,1]
it holds

\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast U_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq C\,(\sigma/\epsilon+\sigma/\rho)\,[\mu]^{\epsilon}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\rho-\epsilon}\leq C\,(\sigma/\epsilon+\sigma/\rho)\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\rho}.

(8.91)

Proof.

By Lemma
4.10
and Remark
5.21
we have

\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast U_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq\int_{0}^{\mu}\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\eta}\ast V_{\nu,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(8.92)

For
\mu\leq\nu
we obtain

\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast U_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq C\sigma/\epsilon\,[\mu]^{\epsilon}\,[\nu]^{\rho-\epsilon}.

(8.93)

For
\mu>\nu
we get

\|K^{n,m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast U_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\leq C\sigma/\epsilon\,[\nu]^{\rho}+C\sigma/\rho\,[\mu]^{\rho}\leq C\,(\sigma/\epsilon+\sigma/\rho)\,[\mu]^{\rho}.

(8.94)

This completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 8.43
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\sigma}\wedge\varepsilon_{\diamond}/2)
. Suppose that the family of noises
\varXi_{\nu}
,
\nu\in[0,1]
, satisfies Assumption
3.2
. There exists a unique choice of the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
such that Assumption
8.38
holds true. With the above choice of the force coefficients for every list of indices
\mathtt{I}
there exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
depending on
\mathtt{I}
such that the following bound

\|K^{n(\mathtt{I}),m(\mathtt{I});{\mathtt{g}}}_{\kappa\vee\nu,\mu}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}(\mathtt{I})}}\\
\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r(\mathtt{I})}\,[\mu]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)s(\mathtt{I})}\,[\kappa\vee\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\varepsilon s(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}}

(8.95)

holds uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
\mu\in[0,1]
.

Remark 8.44
.

Together, the equality

\partial^{r}_{\kappa}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}=\mathbf{I}(\partial^{r}_{\kappa}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu})=\mathbf{I}E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,0},\quad\mathtt{I}=(i,m,a,0,r),~~(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I},~~r\in\{0,1\},

(8.96)

the property of the map
\mathbf{I}
proved in Lemma
6.8
(B) and the bound stated in the above theorem imply that the force coefficient
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}
satisfies the bound

|\partial^{r}_{\kappa}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}|\leq\|\mathbf{I}E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,0}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})}=[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}

(8.97)

uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
.

Proof.

The proof is by ascending induction on
i(\mathtt{I})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and descending induction on
m(\mathtt{I})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Note that the theorem is trivially true for all list of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
m(\mathtt{I})>i(\mathtt{I})m_{\flat}
since then
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=0
.

The base case:

We first prove the theorem for list of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
i(\mathtt{I})=0
. In this case
m(\mathtt{I})=0
for non-zero cumulants and the cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
coincide with the cumulants of the noise
\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
. Evidently,
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
is independent of
\mu
. Hence, it is enough to consider the case
s(\mathtt{I})=0
. As a result, the statement of the theorem for list of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
i(\mathtt{I})=0
follows from Lemma
8.19
(D) since for list of indices
\mathtt{I}
of the above-mentioned form it holds

\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}=-n(\mathtt{I})(\dim(\varXi)+2\varepsilon)+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}<n(\mathtt{I})\dim(\varXi)-\mathsf{D}-\varepsilon r(\mathtt{I})

(8.98)

since
r(\mathtt{I})\leq n(\mathtt{I})
.

Induction step:

Fix
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Assume that the theorem is true for all lists of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that either
i(\mathtt{I})<i_{\circ}
, or
i(\mathtt{I})=i_{\circ}
and
m(\mathtt{I})>m_{\circ}
. We shall prove the theorem for all
\mathtt{I}
such that
i(\mathtt{I})=i_{\circ}
and
m(\mathtt{I})=m_{\circ}
.

We first consider the case
s(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
. By Remark
8.44
the bound assumed in Lemma
8.34
is satisfied for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
such that
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. As a result, for
\mu\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
the bound (
8.95
) with
s(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
follows from the inductive assumption, Lemma
8.34
(C), Remark
8.35
and Remark
8.37
. Note that by Lemma
8.14
(A) the cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
can be expressed in terms of the cumulants for which the statement of the theorem has already been established. In consequence, for
\mu\in[\kappa\vee\nu,1]
the bound (
8.95
) with
s(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
follows from the inductive assumption and Lemma
8.14
(B).

Now consider the case
s(\mathtt{I})=0
, that is

\mathtt{I}=((i_{1},m_{1},a_{1},0,r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},a_{n},0,r_{n})).

(8.99)

It follows from Def.
8.5
of the cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
that

E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,0}+\sum_{q=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\mu}E^{\mathtt{I}_{q}}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,\quad E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,1}-\sum_{q=1}^{n}\int_{\mu}^{1}E^{\mathtt{I}_{q}}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,

(8.100)

where

\mathtt{I}_{q}=((i_{1},m_{1},a_{1},0,r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{q},m_{q},a_{q},1,r_{q}),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},a_{n},0,r_{n})).

(8.101)

Note that
s(\mathtt{I}_{q})=1
, hence the bound (
8.95
) has already been established for
E^{\mathtt{I}_{q}}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}
. We will use the first of Eqs. (
8.100
) to bound the irrelevant cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
, i.e. those with
\mathtt{I}
such that
\varrho(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}>0
. The second equality will be used to bound certain relevant contributions to the cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
with
\mathtt{I}
such that
\varrho(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}\leq 0
.

We first analyse the relevant contributions. We note that the inequality
\varrho(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}\leq 0
implies
n(\mathtt{I})=1
. Consequently,
\mathtt{I}=(i,m,a,0,r)
for some
r\in\{0,1\}
and
a\in\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}
such that
\varrho(i,m,a)\leq 0
, or equivalently,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
. Hence,

E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial^{r}_{\kappa}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}},\qquad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-},~r\in\{0,1\}.

(8.102)

The relevant contributions mentioned in the previous paragraph have the following form

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathbf{I}(\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}})=\mathbf{I}(K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\kappa\vee\nu,1}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu})\in\mathbb{R},

(8.103)

where the map
\mathbf{I}
was introduced in Def.
6.6
. Note that by the translational invariance
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
is a constant function. The application of the map
\mathbf{I}
to both sides of the second of Eqs. (
8.100
) yields

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,1}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}-\int_{\mu}^{1}\mathbf{I}(E^{\mathtt{I}_{1}}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta,

(8.104)

where
E^{\mathtt{I}_{1}}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\eta}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
. Recalling that
\mathcal{V}^{m}\equiv\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}
and using Lemma
6.8
we arrive at

|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\leq|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,1}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|+\int_{\mu}^{1}\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\kappa\vee\nu,\eta}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}_{1}}_{\kappa;\nu,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(8.105)

By the renormalization conditions (
8.88
) we have

|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,1}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|=|\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu}|\lesssim 1,\qquad\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{\phantom{j}}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,1}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=0

(8.106)

for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
. Hence, the bound (
8.105
) and the bound (
8.95
) for
E^{\mathtt{I}_{1}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
imply that for
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
it holds

|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\lesssim 1+\int_{\mu}^{1}[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\eta]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}[\kappa\vee\nu\vee\eta]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\\
\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\kappa\vee\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}.

(8.107)

We will use this bound twice in the remainder of the proof.

First, note that by the above bound

|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\kappa\vee\nu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}

(8.108)

holds uniformly in
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
. As a result, the bound assumed in Lemma
8.34
is satisfied for
i=i_{\circ}
,
m=m_{\circ}
and all
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}_{\sigma}
. Consequently, for
\mu\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
the bound (
8.95
) follows from Lemma
8.34
(C), Remark
8.35
and Remark
8.37
.

It remains to prove the theorem for
\mu\in[\kappa\vee\nu,1]
and
s(\mathtt{I})=0
. Let us first consider the case
\varrho(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}>0
. We use the first of Eqs. (
8.100
). As argued in the previous paragraph, the first term on the RHS of this equation is bounded by the RHS of the inequality (
8.95
). The same bound for the second term follows from the bounds for
E^{\mathtt{I}_{q}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
proved above and Lemma
8.42
(B) applied with
\rho=\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}
and
\epsilon=\varepsilon
. Note that
\rho>0
by Remark
8.11
.

The case
\varrho(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)\mathsf{D}\leq 0
and
\mu\in[\kappa\vee\nu,1]
requires special treatment. As argued above, in this case the cumulant
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
has the form (
8.102
). If
m=0
, then
a=0
and

E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial^{r}_{\kappa}F^{i,0,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,0,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}.

(8.109)

Hence, in this case the statement of the theorem follows from the bound (
8.107
). By the previous paragraph, for
\mu\geq\kappa\vee\nu
and all
(i,m,b)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
it holds

\|K_{\kappa\vee\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\mu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,b)}.

(8.110)

Moreover, for all
(i,m,b)\in\mathfrak{I}
we have

|\mathbf{I}(\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}})|\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\mu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,b)}.

(8.111)

For
(i,m,b)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
this follows from the bound (
8.110
), Lemma
6.8
and translational invariance. For
(i,m,b)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
the bound (
8.111
) coincides with the bound (
8.107
). We note that for any
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
such that
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
it holds

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}(\mathbf{I}(\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}),\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}})

(8.112)

by Theorem
6.12
, where the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
was introduced in Def.
6.9
. If
i=1
, then we set
\mathtt{l}=\lceil\sigma\rceil
and note that
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,b}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=0
for all
|b|=\mathtt{l}
by locality. If
i>1
, then we set
\mathtt{l}=\sigma_{\diamond}
where
\sigma_{\diamond}=\sigma
if
\sigma\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\sigma_{\diamond}=\lceil\sigma\rceil-1
otherwise. By Assumption
3.13
in both cases the RHS of the above equality involves only objects for which the bounds (
8.110
) and (
8.111
) hold true. Consequently, the statement of the theorem follows from Theorem
6.14
applied with
C\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m)}
.
∎

9
Probabilistic analysis

The primary result of this section is Theorem
9.1
, which exhibits and bounds the random fields
f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}=\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
for
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
, where
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=f^{i,m,a}_{0;\nu,\mu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
,
\nu\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, are the relevant effective force coefficients. The proof relies on the Kolmogorov type argument presented in Sec.
9.1
, the bounds for cumulants of the effective force coefficients established in Sec.
8
and a certain diagonal argument taken from
[
27
,
39
]
exploiting the extra parameter
\kappa\in[0,1]
of the coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
. Let us note that the probabilistic results of this section together with the deterministic results proved in Sec.
10
imply the main result of this paper stated in Theorem
3.17
.

Theorem 9.1
.

There exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, a coupling between the random fields
(\varXi_{\nu},\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})
,
\nu\in[0,1]
, a universal family of random continuous functions

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{H}),\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-},

(9.1)

and a random distribution
\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T})
such that for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and every
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
the following conditions are satisfied:

(A
1
)

\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{-\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}<\infty
,

(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}
,

(B
1
)

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\,[\nu]^{-\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}\,\|\mathtt{u}f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}=0
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
,

(C
1
)

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}[\mu]^{-\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}(f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}}=0
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}
.

(A
2
)

\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{-\beta_{\varepsilon}}\,\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}<\infty
,

(B
2
)

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\,[\nu]^{-\beta_{\varepsilon}}\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}=0
,

(C
2
)

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{-\beta_{\varepsilon}}\,\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast(\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}-\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}=0
.

These bounds hold almost surely and the limits exist in probability.

Remark 9.2
.

Recall that
f^{0,0,0}_{\nu,\mu}=\varXi_{\nu}
,
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(0,0,0)=-\dim(\varXi)-\varepsilon=-(\mathsf{d}+\sigma)/2-\varepsilon
,
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}
,
\beta_{\varepsilon}=-\dim(\phi)-\varepsilon
,
\dim(\phi)
was introduced in Def.
2.10
and
(\mathtt{u}f)(\mathring{x},\bar{x})=\mathtt{u}(\mathring{x})f(\mathring{x},\bar{x})
.

Remark 9.3
.

The random functions (
9.1
) are constructed in such a way that they are measurable with respect to the sigma algebra of the white noise
\varXi_{0}
and depend only on the choice of the renormalization parameters
\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{0}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
. For a fixed choice of the renormalization parameters the family of functions (
9.1
) does not depend on a choice of the family of noises
\varXi_{\nu}
,
\nu\in(0,1]
, and a choice of the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
,
\nu\in(0,1]
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
, satisfying our assumptions. This property is called universality.

Proof.

The construction of the coupling as well as the proofs of Parts (A
1
) and (C
1
) for
(i,m,a)=(0,0,0)
and Parts (A
2
) and
(C
2
) are contained in Sec.
9.3
. Parts (A
1
) and (C
1
) for
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}=\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}\setminus\{(0,0,0)\}
are established in Sec.
9.5
. Part (B
1
) for
(i,m,a)=(0,0,0)
and Part (B
2
) are proved in Sec.
9.2
. Part (B
1
) for
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}=\mathfrak{I}_{0}\setminus\{(0,0,0)\}
follows from Remark
8.44
. Recall that the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}=f^{i,m,a}_{0;\nu,0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
, are deterministic.
∎

9.1
Probabilistic estimate

In this section we prove a probabilistic estimate similar in spirit to the Kolmogorov continuity theorem. We will need the following simple lemma.

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
n\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
be such that
n>\mathsf{d}
.

(A)

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all random fields
\zeta\in L^{n}(\mathbb{H})
and
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|K_{\mu}\ast\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\leq C\,[\mu]^{-\mathsf{D}}\,\int_{\mathbb{H}}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|\zeta(x)|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,\mathrm{d}x.

(9.2)

(B)

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all random fields
\zeta\in L^{n}(\mathbb{T})
and
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\bar{K}_{\mu}\ast\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\leq C\,[\mu]^{-\mathsf{d}}\,\int_{\mathbb{T}}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|\zeta(\bar{x})|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}.

(9.3)

(C)

For every
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all random fields
\hat{\zeta}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})
and
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\mathtt{w}(K_{\mu}\ast\hat{\zeta})\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\leq C\,[\mu]^{-\mathsf{D}}\,\sup_{x\in\mathbb{H}}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|\hat{\zeta}(x)|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}.

(9.4)

Proof.

Note that
K_{\mu}\ast\zeta=\mathbf{T}K_{\mu}\star\zeta
, where
\star
is the convolution in
\mathbb{H}
and
\mathbf{T}K_{\mu}
is the periodization of
K_{\mu}
(see Def.
4.11
). Using the Young inequality for convolutions we obtain

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|K_{\mu}\ast\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\leq\|\mathbf{T}K_{\mu}\|_{L^{n/(n-1)}(\mathbb{H})}^{n}~\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{n}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}.

(9.5)

By Lemma
4.13
(D) and the Fubini theorem we get

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|K_{\mu}\ast\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-\mathsf{D}}\,\int_{\mathbb{H}}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|\zeta(x)|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,\mathrm{d}x,

(9.6)

which proves Part (A). The proof of Part (B) is the same. To prove Part (C) first recall that
\mathbf{P}_{\mu}=(1+\mu\partial_{\mathring{x}})(1-[\mu]^{2}\Delta_{\bar{x}})
and set
\dot{\mathtt{w}}(\mathring{x}):=\partial_{\mathring{x}}\mathtt{w}(\mathring{x})
. Note that it holds

\|\mathtt{w}(K_{\mu}\ast\hat{\zeta})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\leq\|K_{\mu}\ast\mathbf{P}_{\mu}\mathtt{w}(K_{\mu}\ast\hat{\zeta})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\\
\leq\|K_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\hat{\zeta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}+\mu\|K_{\mu}\ast\dot{\mathtt{w}}(K_{\mu}\ast\hat{\zeta})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}.

(9.7)

Part (C) follows now from Part (A) applied with
\zeta=\mathtt{w}\hat{\zeta}
and
\zeta=\dot{\mathtt{w}}(K_{\mu}\ast\hat{\zeta})
and the bound stated in Remark
8.18
applied with
H=K_{\mu}
.
∎

Lemma 9.5
.

Fix
n\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
, such that
n>\mathsf{d}
,
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varepsilon>\mathsf{D}/n
. There exists a constant
c>0
such that the following statement is true. Let
\zeta:(0,1]\to L^{n}(\mathbb{H})
be a differentiable random function. Assume that

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\partial_{\mu}^{s}\zeta_{\mu}^{\phantom{l}}\|^{n}_{L^{n}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\\
\leq C\,[\mu]^{n(\rho+\varepsilon-\sigma s)}

(9.8)

for some
C>0
and all
s\in\{0,1\}
and
\mu\in(0,1]
. Then it holds

\mathopen{\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\big\|[\mu]^{-\rho}\,K_{\mu}\ast\zeta_{\mu}\big\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}}\leq c\,C.

(9.9)

Proof.

Let
\hat{\zeta}:(0,1]\to L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})
be defined by

\hat{\zeta}_{\mu}:=[\mu]^{-\rho}\,K_{\mu}\ast\zeta_{\mu},\quad\mu\in(0,1].

(9.10)

By Lemma
9.4
and Lemma
4.16
the function
\hat{\zeta}
is differentiable and
