\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\partial_{\mu}^{s}\hat{\zeta}_{\mu}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim C\,[\mu]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma s-\mathsf{D}/n)}

(9.11)

for
s\in\{0,1\}
. Moreover, it holds

\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\|\hat{\zeta}_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\leq\|\hat{\zeta}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}+\int_{0}^{1}\|\partial_{\mu}\hat{\zeta}_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\,\mathrm{d}\mu.

(9.12)

By the Minkowski inequality we get

\mathopen{\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\|\hat{\zeta}_{\mu}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}}^{\!\frac{1}{n}}\leq\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\hat{\zeta}_{1}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}^{\frac{1}{n}}+\int_{0}^{1}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\partial_{\mu}\hat{\zeta}_{\mu}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}^{\frac{1}{n}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu.

(9.13)

The statement of the lemma follows now from the bounds (
9.11
) and (
9.13
).
∎

9.2
Simple bounds

In this section we show that

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\,[\nu]^{\dim(\varXi)+\varepsilon}\,\|\mathtt{u}\varXi_{\nu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}=0,\qquad\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\,[\nu]^{\dim(\phi)+\varepsilon}\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}=0

(9.14)

in distribution for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and every
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
. This implies the convergence in probability for every coupling between
(\varXi_{\nu},\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})
,
\nu\in[0,1]
.

Lemma 9.6
.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1\wedge\sigma)
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
it holds

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\mathtt{u}\varXi_{\nu}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim[\nu]^{-n(\dim(\varXi)+\varepsilon)}

(9.15)

uniformly in
\nu\in(0,1]
and for
r\in\{0,1\}

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\mathtt{u}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\kappa\vee\nu]^{-n(\dim(\varXi)+2\varepsilon)}

(9.16)

uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
,
\nu\in[0,1]
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma for
n\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that
n\varepsilon>\mathsf{D}
. The general case is implied by the Jensen inequality. The first bound follows from Lemma
8.19
(A) and Lemma
9.4
(C) applied with
\hat{\zeta}=\mathbf{P}_{\nu}\varXi_{\nu}
. The second bound is the consequence of Lemma
8.19
(C) and Lemma
9.4
(C) applied with
\hat{\zeta}=\mathbf{P}_{\kappa\vee\nu}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
and
\hat{\zeta}=\mathbf{P}_{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
.
∎

Lemma 9.7
.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
it holds

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim[\nu]^{-n(\dim(\phi)+\varepsilon)}

(9.17)

uniformly in
\nu\in(0,1]
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma for
n\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that
n\varepsilon>\mathsf{d}
. The general case is a consequence of the Jensen inequality. Since
\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\nu}\bar{K}_{\nu}=\delta_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}}
by Lemma
4.13
(C) we obtain

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}=\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\bar{K}_{\nu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\nu}^{\mathtt{f}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\lesssim\|\bar{K}_{\nu}\ast\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\nu}^{\mathtt{f}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})},

(9.18)

where
\mathtt{f}=\lfloor\sigma/2\rfloor+2
. Using the equality
\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\nu}=(1-[\nu]^{2}\Delta_{\bar{x}})
and the bound stated in Assumption
3.8
we arrive at

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\nu}^{\mathtt{f}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}(x)|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim[\nu]^{-n\dim(\phi)}

(9.19)

uniformly in
\nu\in(0,1]
and
x\in\mathbb{T}
. Finally, we apply Lemma
9.4
(B) with
\zeta=\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\nu}^{\mathtt{f}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}
.
∎

9.3
Coupling

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
Y_{\nu}
,
\nu\in[0,1]
, be a family of random variables valued in some Polish space
(\mathcal{Y},d)
. Assume that
\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}Y_{\nu}=Y_{0}
in law. Then there exists a coupling between
Y_{\nu}
,
\nu\in[0,1]
, such that
\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}Y_{\nu}=Y_{0}
in probability.

Proof.

We follow the method described in the proof of Theorem 6.5 in
[
27
]
. By
[
47
, Theorem 6.9]
the convergence in law is equivalent to the convergence in the Wasserstein sense for the bounded metric
d({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\wedge 1
. Hence,

\inf_{\mathbb{E}}\mathbb{E}(d(Y_{\nu},Y_{0})\wedge 1)

(9.20)

converges to zero as
\nu\searrow 0
, where the infimum is taken over all joint distributions
\mathbb{E}
of the random variables
Y_{\nu}
and
Y_{0}
with marginals coinciding with
\mathrm{Law}(Y_{\nu})
and
\mathrm{Law}(Y_{0})
. By
[
47
, Theorem 4.1]
for every
\nu\in(0,1]
the infimum (
9.20
) is attained for some joint distribution
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}
. The desired coupling
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}
is obtained with the use of
[
14
, Theorem A.1]
by gluing together the joint distributions
\mathbb{E}_{\nu}
,
\nu\in(0,1]
, along the common marginal
\mathrm{Law}(Y_{0})
. We have

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}d(Y_{\nu},Y_{0})\wedge 1\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{\nu}(d(Y_{\nu},Y_{0})\wedge 1)=0,

(9.21)

which is equivalent to convergence in probability.
∎

Lemma 9.9
.

Let
{\mathtt{g}}=\lfloor\dim(\varXi)\vee\dim(\phi)\rfloor+1
. There exists a coupling between the random fields
(\varXi_{\nu},\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})
,
\nu\in[0,1]
, such that for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and every
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
it holds

(A)

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{\dim(\varXi)+\varepsilon}\,\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}(\varXi_{\nu}-\varXi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}=0
,

(B)

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{\dim(\phi)+\varepsilon}\,\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast(\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}-\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}=0

in probability.

Proof.

Let us fix some sequence of functions
\mathtt{u}_{n}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, such that
\mathtt{u}_{n}(\mathring{x})=1
for
\mathring{x}\in[-n,n]
. For
(v,\phi)\in C(\mathbb{H})\times C(\mathbb{T})
we define the family of semi-norms

\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}[\mu]^{\dim(\varXi)+\varepsilon}\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}+\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}[\mu]^{\dim(\phi)+\varepsilon}\|\bar{K}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}

(9.22)

parameterized by
\varepsilon\in\{2^{-n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}
and
\mathtt{u}\in\{\mathtt{u}_{n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}
. Let
\mathcal{Y}
be the Fréchet space obtained by the completion of
C(\mathbb{H})\times C(\mathbb{T})
equipped with the above family of semi-norms and let
d
be a metric inducing the same topology. The statement follows now from the previous lemma, Remark
9.11
and the fact that
\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\,(\varXi_{\nu},\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})=(\varXi_{0},\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}})
in law in
(\mathcal{Y},d)
. The existence of the above limit in law follows from Remark
3.9
.
∎

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. The following bound

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{v}v\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\sum_{\mathtt{f}=0}^{\mathtt{g}}\mu^{\mathtt{f}}\,\|(\partial_{\mathring{x}}^{\mathtt{f}}\mathtt{v})(K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast v)\|_{\mathcal{V}}

(9.23)

holds uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
,
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and
v\in\mathcal{V}
.

Proof.

We have

K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{v}v=K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{v}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}(K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast v),

(9.24)

where we used the equality
\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}=\delta_{\mathbb{M}}
. Note that since
\mathbf{P}_{\mu}=1+\mu\partial_{\mathring{x}}-[\mu]^{2}\Delta
and
\|K_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}=1
we have

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast\mathtt{w}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{f}}f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\|K_{\mu}^{\ast(\mathtt{f}-1)}\ast\mathtt{w}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{f}-1}f\|_{\mathcal{V}}+\mu\|K_{\mu}^{\ast(\mathtt{f}-1)}\ast(\partial_{\mathring{x}}\mathtt{w})\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{f}-1}f\|_{\mathcal{V}}

(9.25)

for any
\mathtt{f}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and
f\in\mathcal{V}
. Let
\mathtt{v}^{(\mathtt{h})}(\mathring{x}):=\partial^{\mathtt{h}}_{\mathring{x}}\mathtt{v}(\mathring{x})
for
\mathtt{h}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Applying the above bound recursively with
f=K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast v
,
\mathtt{w}\in\{\mathtt{v}^{(0)},\ldots,\mathtt{v}^{({\mathtt{g}}-\mathtt{f})}\}
and
\mathtt{f}\in\{{\mathtt{g}},{\mathtt{g}}-1,\ldots,1\}
we arrive at the statement of the lemma.
∎

Remark 9.11
.

Let
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
be such that for all
\mathring{x}\in\mathbb{R}
it holds
\mathtt{v}(\mathring{x})=0
unless
\mathtt{u}(\mathring{x})=1
. By Lemma
9.10
the following bound

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}v\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}v\|_{\mathcal{V}}

(9.26)

holds uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
and
v\in\mathcal{V}
.

9.4
Moment bounds

Lemma 9.12
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\sigma}\wedge\varepsilon_{\diamond}/2]
,
n\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\{0,1\}
be arbitrary. There exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that for every
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
the following bounds

\begin{gathered}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}(x)|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\sigma s)},\\
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}(f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{0;\nu,\mu})(x)|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{\varepsilon n}\,[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\sigma s)},\\
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}(f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa_{1};0,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa_{2};0,\mu})(x)|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim|[\kappa_{1}]^{\varepsilon}-[\kappa_{2}]^{\varepsilon}|^{n}\,[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\sigma s)}\end{gathered}

(9.27)

hold uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{H}
,
\kappa,\nu,\mu\in(0,1]
.

Proof.

We first observe that by the Minkowski inequality and the estimate

\int_{\kappa_{1}}^{\kappa_{2}}[\kappa]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\mathrm{d}\kappa\lesssim|[\kappa_{1}]^{\varepsilon}-[\kappa_{2}]^{\varepsilon}|

(9.28)

it is enough to prove that for
r\in\{0,1\}
the following bound

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial^{r}_{\kappa}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}(x)|^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\sigma s)}

(9.29)

holds uniformly for
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]\setminus\{(0,0)\}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
. Since
\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)<0
for any
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}
and
\mu\in(0,\kappa\vee\nu]
the above bound follows from Lemma
8.34
(B) and Remark
8.36
(note that the bound assumed in Lemma
8.34
is proved in Theorem
8.43
and Remark
8.44
). To prove the above bound for
\mu\in(\kappa\vee\nu,1]
consider the following list of indices

\mathtt{I}=((i,m,a,s,r),\ldots,(i,m,a,s,r)),\quad n(\mathtt{I})=n,~m(\mathtt{I})=nm.

(9.30)

Using the identity
\partial^{s}_{\mu}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\mathbf{I}(\partial^{s}_{\mu}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu})
we obtain for some
\mathtt{h}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{H}}\int_{\mathbb{H}^{n-1}}|\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}K^{\ast\mathtt{h}}_{\mu}\ast\partial^{s}_{\mu}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}(x_{1}),\ldots,K^{\ast\mathtt{h}}_{\mu}\ast\partial^{s}_{\mu}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}(x_{n})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}|\,\mathrm{d}x_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{n}\\
\leq\big\|K^{\ast\mathtt{h},\otimes(n+nm)}_{\mu}\ast\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial^{s}_{\mu}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},\ldots,\partial^{s}_{\mu}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\big\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\\[2.84526pt]
=\big\|K^{\ast\mathtt{h},\otimes(n+nm)}_{\mu}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\big\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{m}}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\sigma s)+(n-1)\mathsf{D}}

(9.31)

uniformly
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
\mu\in[\kappa\vee\nu,1]
. The last inequality above follows from Theorem
8.43
and
n\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)=\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})
. Since
\|K^{\ast\mathtt{f}}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}=1
and by Lemma
4.13
(D)
\|\mathbf{T}K_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\lesssim[\mu]^{-\mathsf{D}}
uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
for
\mathtt{f}=\mathsf{d}+1
the bound (
9.29
) with
{\mathtt{g}}=\mathtt{h}+\mathtt{f}
follows from Lemma
8.17
applied with
H=K_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}
.
∎

9.5
Uniform bounds and convergence

In this section we assume that the random fields
\varXi_{\nu}
,
\nu\in[0,1]
are coupled in such a way that the statement (A) of Lemma
9.9
holds true. We show that there exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and a universal family of random continuous functions

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{;{\mathtt{g}}},\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-},

(9.32)

such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
it holds

\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}[\mu]^{-\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}<\infty

(9.33)

almost surely and

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}[\mu]^{-\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}(f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}

(9.34)

in probability for all
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}
. Recall that
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}
iff
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
\varrho(i,m,a)<0
. Let us also remark that
f^{0,0,0}_{\nu,\mu}=\varXi_{\nu}
for all
\nu,\mu\in[0,1]
.

We first establish the convergence of
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
as
\nu\searrow 0
for fixed
\kappa\in(0,1]
. Note that this simple result, stated in the lemma below, relies only on rough estimates for moments of the regularized noise
\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}
and its convergence as
\nu\searrow 0
.

Lemma 9.13
.

Fix
\varepsilon,\kappa\in(0,1]
. We have

\sup_{\nu\in[0,1]}\mathopen{\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}}\sup_{\mu\in[0,1]}[\mu]^{n(\sigma-\varepsilon)s}\,\|\mathtt{u}\partial_{\mu}^{s}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}^{n}\mathclose{\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}}<\infty

(9.35)

and

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}}\sup_{\mu\in[0,1]}[\mu]^{n(\sigma-\varepsilon)s}\,\|\mathtt{u}\partial_{\mu}^{s}(f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0,\mu})\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}}=0

(9.36)

for all
s\in\{0,1\}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

The statement is true if
m>im_{\flat}
since then
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=0
. To prove the statement for
i=0
it is enough to investigate
f^{0,0,0}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}=\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}=M_{\kappa}\ast\varXi_{\nu}
. Using the fact that
M_{\kappa}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{M})
is supported in a unit ball we obtain that for any
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{v}=1
on the set
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{u}+[-1,1]
it holds

\|\mathtt{u}(\varXi_{\kappa;\nu}-\varXi_{\kappa;0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}=\|\mathtt{u}M_{\kappa}\ast\mathtt{v}(\varXi_{\nu}-\varXi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\\
\leq\|M_{\kappa}\ast\mathtt{v}(\varXi_{\nu}-\varXi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\leq\|\mathbf{P}^{\mathtt{g}}M_{\kappa}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{v}(\varXi_{\nu}-\varXi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}

(9.37)

for any
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Note that
\|\mathbf{P}^{\mathtt{g}}M_{\kappa}\|_{\mathcal{K}}<\infty
. As a result, for
i=0
the statement follows from Lemma
9.6
and Lemma
9.9
(A).

Fix
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and assume that the statement of the lemma is true for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
i<i_{\circ}
or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. Since the force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}=f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,0}\in\mathbb{R}
are deterministic the induction hypothesis implies

\sup_{\nu\in[0,1]}|f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}|<\infty,\qquad\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}|f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}-f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0}|=0

(9.38)

for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
as above and all
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}_{\sigma}
. Hence, by the deterministic estimates established in Lemma
7.11
and Remark
7.12
the statement of the lemma is true for
s=1
and
i=i_{\circ}
,
m=m_{\circ}
. Let us prove the statement for
s=0
and
i=i_{\circ}
,
m=m_{\circ}
. Note that if
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
, then the conditions (
9.38
) follow immediately from Assumption
8.38
. If
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
, then we verify these conditions using the equality

f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu}=\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{\nu}-\int_{0}^{1}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\partial_{\mu}^{s}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu,

(9.39)

the fact that
\partial_{\mu}^{s}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}
is stationary and the statement of the lemma with
s=1
. This proves that the conditions (
9.38
) hold true for
i=i_{\circ}
,
m=m_{\circ}
and all
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}_{\sigma}
. Hence, by Lemma
7.11
and Remark
7.12
the statement is true for
s=0
and
i=i_{\circ}
,
m=m_{\circ}
and all
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}_{\sigma}
. This proves the induction step.
∎

Lemma 9.14
.

Let
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
be arbitrary. There exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that it holds

\begin{gathered}\mathopen{\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{-n\varrho_{3\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}\,\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}}\lesssim 1,\\
\mathopen{\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{-n\varrho_{3\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}\,\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}(f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{0;\nu,\mu})\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{n\varepsilon},\\
\mathopen{\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\langle}$}}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{-n\varrho_{3\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}\,\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}(f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa_{1};0,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa_{2};0,\mu})\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\mathclose{\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\Big\rangle}$}}\lesssim|[\kappa_{1}]^{\varepsilon}-[\kappa_{2}]^{\varepsilon}|^{n}\end{gathered}

(9.40)

uniformly in
\kappa,\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\nu\in(0,1]
for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\sigma}\wedge\varepsilon_{\diamond}/2]
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
.

Proof.

We first note that by the Jensen inequality if the above bounds hold for some
n=n_{\circ}
, then they hold for all
n<n_{\circ}
. We assume that
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is such that
\varepsilon>\mathsf{D}/n
and note that
\varrho_{3\varepsilon}(i,m,a)\leq\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon
if
m\leq im_{\flat}
. The bounds in the statement of the lemma follow from Lemma
9.5
applied with
\rho=\varrho_{2\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon
and

\zeta_{\mu}=K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu},\quad C\lesssim 1,

(9.41)

\zeta_{\mu}=K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}(f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{0;\nu,\mu}),\quad C\lesssim[\kappa]^{n\varepsilon},

(9.42)

\zeta_{\mu}=K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}(f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa_{1};0,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa_{2};0,\mu}),\quad C\lesssim|[\kappa_{1}]^{\varepsilon}-[\kappa_{2}]^{\varepsilon}|^{n}.

(9.43)

The above
\zeta_{\mu}
and
C
satisfy the assumption of Lemma
9.5
by Lemma
9.10
, Lemma
9.12
, the identity

\partial_{\mu}(K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}f_{\mu})={\mathtt{g}}\,\mathbf{P}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}K_{\mu}\ast K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}f_{\mu}+K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}\partial_{\mu}f_{\mu},

(9.44)

and the uniform bound
\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}K_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-\sigma}
established in Lemma
4.16
.
∎

Lemma 9.15
.

Let
Y_{\kappa,\nu}
,
(\kappa,\nu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
be a family of random variables valued in a Polish space
(\mathcal{Y},d)
. Assume that:

(A)

\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}\,\sup_{\nu\in(0,1]}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}1\wedge d(Y_{\kappa,\nu},Y_{0,\nu})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=0
,

(B)

\lim_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\searrow 0}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}1\wedge d(Y_{\kappa_{1},0},Y_{\kappa_{2},0})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=0
,

(C)

\forall_{\kappa\in(0,1]}\,\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}1\wedge d(Y_{\kappa,\nu},Y_{\kappa,0})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=0
.

Then there exists a random variable
Y_{0,0}
in
(\mathcal{Y},d)
such that

\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}1\wedge d(Y_{0,\nu},Y_{0,0})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=0.

(9.45)

Proof.

The assumption (B) says that
Y_{\kappa,0}
,
\kappa\in(0,1]
, is Cauchy in probability. This implies that there exists
Y_{0,0}
such that
\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}1\wedge d(Y_{\kappa,0},Y_{0,0})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=0
. To conclude the proof it is enough to observe that by the triangle inequality for every
\kappa\in(0,1]
it holds

\limsup_{\nu\searrow 0}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}1\wedge d(Y_{0,\nu},Y_{0,0})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}\\
\leq\sup_{\nu\in(0,1]}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}1\wedge d(Y_{0,\nu},Y_{\kappa,\nu})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}+\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\,\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}1\wedge d(Y_{\kappa,\nu},Y_{\kappa,0})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}+\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}1\wedge d(Y_{\kappa,0},Y_{0,0})\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}

(9.46)

and subsequently take the limit
\kappa\searrow 0
of the expression on the RHS of the above inequality.
∎

Let us prove the claim formulated at the beginning of this section. To this end, we fix some sequence of functions
\mathtt{u}_{n}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, such that
\mathtt{u}_{n}(\mathring{x})=1
for
\mathring{x}\in[-n,n]
. For any
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}
and any bounded continuous functions
(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto v_{\mu}\in C(\mathbb{H})
we define the family of semi-norms

\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}[\mu]^{-\rho}\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}v_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}

(9.47)

parameterized by
\varepsilon\in\{2^{-n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}
and
\mathtt{u}\in\{\mathtt{u}_{n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}
. Let
\mathcal{Y}^{\rho}
be the Fréchet space obtained by the completion of the space of the functions
v_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}
of the above form equipped with the above family of semi-norms and let
d^{\rho}
be a metric inducing the same topology. The statement follows now from Lemma
9.15
applied to
Y_{\kappa,\nu}=f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;\nu,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}\in\mathcal{Y}^{\rho}
,
\rho=\varrho_{3\varepsilon}(i,m,a)
, Remark
9.11
and Lemma
9.13
and Lemma
9.14
. In order to prove the universality of the family of the random functions (
9.32
) we note that

f^{i,m,a}_{0,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}:=\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-},

(9.48)

in probability in the space
\mathcal{Y}^{\rho}
, where the effective force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0,\mu}
are constructed as specified in Def.
7.3
with the noise
\varXi_{\kappa;0}
obtained by mollification of the white noise and the (deterministic) force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
satisfying the following conditions specified in Assumption
8.38
. The irrelevant force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
, vanish identically and the relevant force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
, are fixed by the renormalization conditions
\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}f^{i,m,a}_{\kappa;0,1}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-2.33336pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}=\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{0}
.

10
Deterministic analysis

The deterministic results established in this section allow to prove the existence of the macroscopic scaling limit and its universality using a pathwise construction. Given (generic realizations of) the relevant effective force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
, and the initial data
\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}
we construct distributions
\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright}
and
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}
having the following properties. If the effective force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
, constructed from a noise
\varXi_{\nu}
and force coefficients
f_{\nu}^{i,m,a}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
, and the initial data
\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}
are close to the effective force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
, and the initial data
\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}
, then
\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}
and
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
are close to
\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright}
and
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}
, where
\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}
is the truncation of the formal power series solving Eq. (
1.11
) and
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
is the classical solution of Eq. (
3.13
). Both
\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}
and
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
are constructed as functions of the noise
\varXi_{\nu}
, the force coefficients
f_{\nu}^{i,m,a}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
, and the initial data
\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}
. The above-mentioned continuity result together with the results of the probabilistic analysis implies the existence of the limits
\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}=\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright}
and
\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}=\breve{\varPhi}_{0}
in probability.

Definition 10.1
.

A collection of objects

\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}),\qquad f^{0,0,0}_{\nu}\equiv\varXi_{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}^{;0}=C(\mathbb{H}),\qquad f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}\in\mathbb{R},\quad~(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I},

(10.1)

such that
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}=0
unless
i\leq i_{\flat}
and
m\leq m_{\flat}
and
|a|<\sigma
is called a boundary data for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
. A collection consisting of
\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T})
and a family of continuous functions

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}=\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{H}),\qquad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0},

(10.2)

is called a boundary data for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
.

Remark 10.2
.

Throughout this section
\nu\in(0,1]
is a fixed parameter. Recall that
\mathfrak{I}_{0}=\{(0,0,0)\}\cup\mathfrak{I}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}\subset\mathfrak{I}_{0}
is the set of relevant indices. We have
\gamma\equiv\gamma_{\varepsilon}:=\sigma-\varepsilon
, and the space
\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})\subset C^{\lceil\sigma\rceil-1}(\mathbb{T})
was introduced in Def.
5.6
.

Assuming that the boundary data for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
is admissible in the sense specified below we give a construction of
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
and
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
as functions of the respective boundary data and show that if the boundary data for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
and
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
are close to each other in the sense specified in Assumptions
10.9
and
10.11
, then
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}-\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}-\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
is small. To this end, we first construct the stationary effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
,
F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
. We express
\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}
,
\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright}
in terms of
F^{i,0,0}_{\nu,\mu}
,
F^{i,0,0}_{0,\mu}
. Next, we construct another stationary effective force coefficients
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}
. After that we fix some
t\in[0,\infty)
and
T\in(0,1)
and construct the effective force coefficients
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}
and
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}
depending on the initial data at the time
\mathring{x}=t
. Subsequently, for sufficiently small
T\in(0,1)
we express
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
,
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}
restricted to the time interval
[t,t+T]
in terms of
\tilde{F}^{i,0}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\tilde{F}^{i,0}_{0,\mu}
and the initial data at
\mathring{x}=t
. Finally, we construct the maximal solutions
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
,
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}
as functions of the respective boundary data. The idea of the construction is explained in Sec.
2.3
.

Definition 10.3
.

Given a boundary data
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, are defined recursively by the flow equation (
7.15
) together with the conditions

F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=0~~\textrm{if}~~m>im_{\flat},\qquad F^{0,0,0}_{\nu,0}=\varXi_{\nu},\qquad F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,0}=F_{\nu}^{i,m,a},\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I},

(10.3)

where for
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
we set

F^{i,m,a}_{\nu}:=\sum_{b\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}}\frac{1}{b!}\partial^{b}\mathbf{L}^{m}(f^{i,m,a+b}_{\nu})

(10.4)

and the map
\mathbf{L}^{m}
was introduced in Def.
6.2
. We also set
F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}:=F^{i,m,0}_{\nu,\mu}
and
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}:=\mathbf{I}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
, where the map
\mathbf{I}
was introduced in Def.
6.6
. Finally, we define a formal power series

\langle F_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\lambda^{i}\,\langle F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle,

(10.5)

where
\psi,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
are arbitrary. We omit
\mu
if
\mu=0
.

Remark 10.4
.

By the properties of the maps
\mathbf{L}^{m}
and
\mathbf{I}
it holds
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,0}=f^{i,m,a}_{\nu\phantom{0}}
. Furthermore, the terms of the sum on the RHS of Eq. (
10.4
) vanish identically unless
a,b\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{m}_{\sigma}
by the assumptions about
f_{\nu}^{i,m,a}
.

Lemma 10.5
.

For every
\mu\in[0,1]
and
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
are uniquely defined and satisfy the conditions
F^{0,0,0}_{\nu,\mu}=\varXi_{\nu}
and
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathcal{X}^{m,a}F^{i,m,0}_{\nu,\mu}
. Moreover, for any
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
the function

[0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{V}^{m}

(10.6)

is continuous in the whole domain and differentiable in the domain
\mu\in(0,1]
.

Remark 10.6
.

The above qualitative lemma is a special case of Lemma
7.5
.

Remark 10.7
.

Recall that for
R>0
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we set
R^{i,m}=R^{1+im_{\flat}-m}
.

Remark 10.8
.

Throughout this section we fix some arbitrary
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\sigma}\wedge\varepsilon_{\diamond})
, where
\varepsilon_{\sigma}
and
\varepsilon_{\diamond}
were introduced in Def.
4.5
and Def.
8.8
.

Assumption 10.9
.

Let
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
,
R>1
,
\delta\in(0,1]
,
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We assume that
\nu\in(0,1]
, a boundary data
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, and a boundary data
f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}
, are such that for all
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds:

(A)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
,

(B)

\|\mathtt{u}f_{\nu}^{i,m,a}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\delta R^{i,m}\,[\nu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
,

(C)

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}(f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
,

where the effective force coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, are constructed from the boundary data
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, as explained in Def.
10.3
.

Remark 10.10
.

At this stage the function
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
can be arbitrary. As we will see, in order to construct
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
and
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
up to some time
T
one has to choose
\mathtt{u}
such that
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
[-i_{\dagger}(i_{\dagger}+1),6i_{\flat}+T]
, where
i_{\dagger}=i_{\flat}+i_{\triangleright}m_{\flat}
and
i_{\triangleright}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
is the largest integer such that
\varrho(i_{\triangleright})+\sigma\leq 0
. Recall also the useful property stated in Remark
9.11
.

Assumption 10.11
.

Let
R>1
,
\delta\in(0,1]
,
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We assume that
\nu\in(0,1]
,
\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})
and
\phi_{0}\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T})
are such that for all
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds:

(A)

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,

(B)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\delta R\,[\nu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,

(C)

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast(\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}-\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\delta R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
.

Remark 10.12
.

Recall that
\beta\equiv\beta_{\varepsilon}=-\dim(\phi)-\varepsilon
,
\dim(\phi)
was introduced in Def.
2.10
and
\bar{K}_{\mu}
,
\mathbf{R}_{\nu}
were defined in Sec.
4
.

Definition 10.13
.

Let
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and
R>1
.
We say that a boundary data
\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}
,
f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}^{-}
for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
is admissible iff for every
\delta\in(0,1]
there exist
\nu\in(0,1]
and a boundary data
\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}
,
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
such that all of the conditions specified in Assumptions
10.9
and
10.11
are satisfied.

Remark 10.14
.

We refrain from giving a characterization of the set of admissible boundary data for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
. Let us only note that
f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, satisfy certain non-linear constraints which are consequences of the fact that the coefficients
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, appearing in Assumption
10.9
are constructed from the boundary data
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, with the use of the flow equation. Let us also remark that the set of admissible
\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T})
coincides with a ball in the Besov space
C^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})
.

In Sec.
10.1
we construct and bound the stationary effective force coefficients
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
,
F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
. Subsequently, in Sec.
10.2
we establish a Schauder-type estimates. In Sec.
10.3
we express the rough part of the solution
\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}
,
\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright}
in terms of the effective force coefficients
F^{i,0,0}_{\nu,\mu}
,
F^{i,0,0}_{0,\mu}
. We also construct another stationary effective force coefficients
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}
, which as argued in Sec.
2.3
, are useful to deal with the effect of the initial condition. In Sec.
10.4
we introduce the effective force coefficients
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}
and
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}
depending on the regular part of the initial data
\phi_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{reg}}
. In Sec.
10.5
we prove that the formal power series defining the effective force
\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
converges absolutely in sufficiently short time intervals. We also establish several properties of the effective force
\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
. In Sec.
10.6
we construct the solution of the SPDE in a short random time interval by expressing it in terms of the effective force
\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
. Finally, in Sec.
10.7
we show how to construct the maximal solution up to a possible finite-time blowup.

10.1
Bounds at stationarity

Theorem 10.15
.

Fix some
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
,
R>1
,
\delta\in(0,1]
and an admissible boundary data for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
. Let
\nu\in(0,\delta^{1/\varepsilon}]
and a boundary data for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
be arbitrary such that all of the conditions specified in Assumption
10.9
are satisfied. There exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and a universal family of differentiable functions

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m},\qquad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0},

(10.7)

satisfying the flow equation (
7.15
) such that it holds

(A)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}(F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and all
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
. The constants of proportionality in the above bounds depend only on
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and are otherwise universal.

Remark 10.16
.

Note that we assume that
\nu\in(0,\delta^{1/\varepsilon}]
. Under this assumption
[\nu]^{\varepsilon}\leq\delta
and the bounds (A), (B), (C) imply that

(
\tilde{\mathrm{C}}
)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}(F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,([\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon}\vee[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon})

for all
\mu\in(0,1]
.

Remark 10.17
.

In the proof we use the equalities:
R^{i,m}=R^{j,k+1}\,R^{i-j,m-k}
and

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(j,k+1,b)+[c]+\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i-j,m-k,d)+\sigma

(10.8)

valid for
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,i\}
,
k\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
,
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
,
b\in\mathfrak{M}^{k+1}
,
c\in\mathfrak{M}
,
d\in\mathfrak{M}^{m-k}
such that the multi-indices
a,b,c,d
satisfy the condition specified below Eq. (
7.10
). The first of the above identities follows trivially from the definition of
R^{i,m}
. The second one is an immediate consequence of Def.
8.8
of
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)
.

Proof.

We first note that for
m>im_{\flat}
we have
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=0
. For
\nu=0
the above equality is our definition of the LHS. As a result, for
m>im_{\flat}
the bounds (A), (B), (C) hold trivially. Next, we observe that
F^{0,0,0}_{\nu,\mu}=f^{0,0,0}_{\nu,\mu}
for
\mu\in[0,1]
. For
\nu=0
and
\mu\in(0,1]
this is our definition of the LHS. Recall also that for
m=0
it holds
\|v\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}=\|v\|_{\mathcal{V}}
and
K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}=K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}
. As a result, the bounds (A), (B), (C) with
i=0
are implied by Remark
9.11
and the bounds stated in Assumption
10.9
.

Fix
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mathtt{f}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and assume that the bounds (A), (B), (C) hold for
{\mathtt{g}}=\mathtt{f}
and all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
such that either
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. We shall show that there exists
\mathtt{h}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that the bounds (A), (B), (C) hold for
{\mathtt{g}}=\mathtt{h}
and all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
such that
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
. Moreover, we will prove that if
i_{\circ}>i_{\diamond}
, then one can choose
\mathtt{h}=\mathtt{f}
. It is enough to verify the statement formulated above to prove the theorem since there are only finitely many
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
i_{\circ}\leq i_{\diamond}
and
m_{\circ}\leq i_{\circ}\,m_{\flat}
and the bounds (A), (B), (C) hold for all
{\mathtt{g}}\geq\mathtt{f}
if they hold for
{\mathtt{g}}=\mathtt{f}
. For convenience, we recall that
i_{\diamond}
is the largest integer such that
\varrho(i_{\diamond},0,0)\leq 0
and
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
iff
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
a\in\mathfrak{M}^{m}
is such that
|\bar{a}|<\sigma_{\diamond}
, where
\sigma_{\diamond}=\sigma
if
\sigma\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\sigma_{\diamond}=\lceil\sigma\rceil-1
otherwise.

Let us turn to the proof of the inductive step. Fix any
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
. We first observe that by the flow equation (
7.15
) and the support properties of the effective force coefficients stated in Lemma
7.6
it holds

\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}^{\phantom{i}}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}\\
=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu},\mathtt{w}F^{j,k+1,b}_{\nu,\mu},\mathtt{v}F^{i-j,m-k,d}_{\nu,\mu}\big),

(10.9)

where
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
is arbitrary such that
\mathtt{v}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,2i]
and
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{v}-[0,(i-1)i]
. We define
\partial_{\mu}^{\phantom{i}}F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
by the RHS of the flow equation (
7.15
) with
\nu=0
. Hence, Eq. (
10.9
) remains valid for
\nu=0
. Using Remark
5.33
we obtain

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}F_{\nu,\mu}^{i,m,a}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\\
\times\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}F_{\nu,\mu}^{j,k+1,b}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{v}F_{\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k,d}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}

(10.10)

and

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}(F_{\nu,\mu}^{i,m,a}-F_{0,\mu}^{i,m,a})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sum_{b+c+d=\pi(a)}\frac{(k+1)a!}{b!c!d!}\\
\times\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}^{c}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\Big(\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(F_{\nu,\mu}^{j,k+1,b}-F_{\nu,\mu}^{j,k+1,b})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{v}F_{\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k,d}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}\\
+\|K_{0,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}F_{0,\mu}^{j,k+1,b}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{v}(F_{\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k,d}-F_{0,\mu}^{i-j,m-k,d})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}\Big).

(10.11)

To get the last bound we used the fact that the map
\mathbf{B}
is multi-linear. Let us also recall that by Lemma
4.23
we have

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{c}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon+[c]},

(10.12)

for
c\in\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}
. We note that the above bounds remain valid for
\nu=0
. Using the above bounds, the induction assumption and Remark
10.17
we get

(A
1
)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\sigma}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B
1
)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(C
1
)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}(F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon-\sigma}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
.

We observe that if
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
, then
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon\geq 0
by Remark
8.11
. As a result, for
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
the above bounds imply

(
\tilde{\mathrm{C}}_{1}
)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\partial_{\mu}(F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-2\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
.

We note that by Assumption
10.9
, the equality

F_{\nu}^{i,m,a}=\sum_{b\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}_{\sigma}^{m}}\frac{1}{b!}\,\partial^{b}\mathbf{L}^{m}(f^{i,m,a+b}_{\nu})

(10.13)

and Lemma
6.4
the bound (B) is true for
\mu=0
. Moreover, it holds

F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,0}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(10.14)

For
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
we define
F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
by the RHS of the above equality with
\nu=0
. Note that by Assumption
8.38
for
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
we have
F^{i,m,a}_{0,0}:=0
. As a result, if
\mu\in(0,\nu]
or
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
, then the bounds (A), (B), (C) follow from the bounds (A
1
), (B
1
), (C
1
) and Lemma
8.42
applied with
n=1
,
\epsilon=\varepsilon
and
\rho=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)
or
\rho=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon
.

Recall that for
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}=\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}\cup\mathfrak{I}^{+}
and
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathbf{I}F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
. For
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
and
\mu\in[0,1]
we set
f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}:=\mathbf{I}F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
. Note that
f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}_{0}
, is part of the boundary data for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
. By Lemma
6.8
and the results established so far we obtain

(A
2
)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B
2
)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}f_{\nu}^{i,m,a}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq R^{i,m}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C
2
)

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}(f^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-f^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m,a)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

for all
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
. In order to prove the bounds (A), (B), (C) for
\mu\in(\nu,1]
and
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
we shall use the bounds (A
2
), (B
2
), (C
2
), the validity of the bounds (A), (B), (C) for
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
established earlier and the identities

F^{i,0,0}_{\nu,\mu}=f^{i,0,0}_{\nu,\mu},\qquad F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}(f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu},F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}),\quad m\in\mathbb{N}_{+},

(10.15)

where the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
was introduced in Def.
6.9
. For
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
we define
F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}
by the RHS of the above equalities with
\nu=0
. If
i=1
, then we set
\mathtt{l}=\lceil\sigma\rceil
and note that
F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}=0
for all
|b|=\mathtt{l}
by locality. If
i>1
, then we set
\mathtt{l}=\sigma_{\diamond}
and note that
(i,m,b)\in\mathfrak{I}^{+}
for any
|b|=\mathtt{l}
by Assumption
3.13
. We also observe that by Def. (
6.9
) of the map
\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}
it holds

F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}-F^{i,m,a}_{0,\mu}=\mathbf{X}^{a}_{\mathtt{l}}(f^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}-f^{i,m,b}_{0,\mu},F^{i,m,b}_{\nu,\mu}-F^{i,m,b}_{0,\mu}).

(10.16)

The bounds (A), (B), (C) follow now from Theorem
6.14
applied with the constant
C
such that
C\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
or
C\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
.

The differentiability of the functions (
10.7
) follows from the construction. In order to prove that the family of functions (
10.7
) satisfies the flow equation (
7.15
) we use the bounds (A), (B), (C), the assumed admissibility of the family (
10.2
) and the fact that the family
F^{i,m,a}_{\nu,\mu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
, satisfies the flow equation.
∎

10.2
Schauder estimates

Definition 10.18
.

For
\mu\in[0,1]
we set
\hat{G}_{\mu}:=G_{\mu}-G_{1}
and
\hat{G}=G-G_{1}
, where
G_{\mu}
is the fractional heat kernel with the UV cutoff
\mu
introduced in Def.
4.19
.

Lemma 10.19
.

Fix
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\rho>0
. There exists a constant
C
such that if for some
f\in\mathcal{V}=C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{H})
,
R>0
,
\nu\in[0,1]
,
\eta\in(0,1]
and all
\mu\in[\eta,1]
it holds

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq R\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{-\sigma-\rho},

(10.17)

then it holds:

(A)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\eta}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\eta}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq CR\,[\nu\vee\eta]^{-\rho}
,

(B)

\|K_{\eta}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\hat{G}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq CR\,[\nu\vee\eta]^{-\rho}
.

Proof.

We first note that the equalities

\hat{G}_{\eta}=G_{\eta}-G_{1}=-\int_{\eta}^{1}\dot{G}_{\mu}\,\mathrm{d}\mu,\qquad\hat{G}=\hat{G}_{\eta}-\int_{0}^{\eta}\dot{G}_{\mu}\,\mathrm{d}\mu,

(10.18)

imply the following bounds

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\eta}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\eta}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\int_{\eta}^{1}\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathbf{P}_{\eta}^{{\mathtt{g}}}K_{\mu}^{\ast 2{\mathtt{g}}}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\\
\leq\int_{\eta}^{1}\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|\mathbf{P}_{\eta}^{\mathtt{g}}K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu

(10.19)

and

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\eta}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\hat{G}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\eta}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\hat{G}_{\eta}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}+\int_{0}^{\eta}\|K_{\eta}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\\
\leq\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\hat{G}_{\eta}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}+\|K_{\eta}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{V}}\int_{0}^{\eta}\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\,\mathrm{d}\mu.

(10.20)

By Lemma
4.10

\|\mathbf{P}_{\eta}^{\mathtt{g}}K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\leq 1
for
\mu\geq\eta
. Moreover, by Lemma
4.23
it holds

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\leq c\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon}[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma},\qquad\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\leq c\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon}[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}

(10.21)

for some
c>0
and all
\nu\in[0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,1]
. The lemma follows now from the above bounds and the estimates

\int_{\eta}^{1}[\nu\vee\mu]^{-\rho-\varepsilon}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\leq\sigma(\rho^{-1}+\varepsilon^{-1})\,[\nu\vee\eta]^{-\rho}

(10.22)

and

[\nu\vee\eta]^{-\rho-\sigma}\int_{0}^{\eta}[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\leq\sigma(\sigma^{-1}+\varepsilon^{-1})\,[\nu\vee\eta]^{-\rho}.

(10.23)

∎

10.3
Da Prato-Debussche trick

In this section we introduce the effective force
\hat{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
which, as argued in Sec.
2.3
, will be useful to deal with the effect of the initial condition. The definition
\hat{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
stated below is motivated by the so-called Da Prato-Debussche trick
[
13
]
.

Definition 10.20
.

Let
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}_{0}
be a boundary data,
F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, be the corresponding effective force coefficients and
F_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
be the corresponding effective force functional constructed as explained in Def.
10.3
. We set
f^{i}_{\nu,1}:=F^{i,0}_{\nu,1}
for
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}:=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{\triangleright}}\lambda^{i}f_{\nu,1}^{i}
,
\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}:=\hat{G}\ast f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}
. The effective force coefficients
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
are defined by the following relation between formal power series

\langle\hat{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle\equiv\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\lambda^{i}\,\langle\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle:=\langle F_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi+\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}]-f^{\triangleright}_{\nu},\psi\rangle

(10.24)

where
\psi,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
are arbitrary. We omit
\mu
if
\mu=0
.

Remark 10.21
.

Recall that
i_{\triangleright}
is the largest integer such that
\varrho(i_{\triangleright})+\sigma\leq 0
. This implies that
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)+\sigma<0
for all
\varepsilon>0
and
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\triangleright}\}
.

Remark 10.22
.

By Remark
2.13
the functional
\hat{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
satisfies the flow equation (
2.5
) in the sense of formal power series. Hence, the coefficients
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
satisfy the flow equation (
7.14
).

Remark 10.23
.

Note that as argued in Remark
2.1
it holds
f_{\nu,1}=F_{\nu,\mu}[\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast f_{\nu,1}]
in the sense of formal power series, where
f_{\nu,1}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\lambda^{i}\,f_{\nu,1}^{i}
. Consequently,
\hat{F}^{i,0}_{\nu,\mu}=0
if
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\triangleright}\}
and for any
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we have

\langle f^{i}_{\nu,1},\psi\rangle=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{m}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\end{subarray}}\langle F^{i_{0},k}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast f_{\nu,1}^{i_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast f_{\nu,1}^{i_{k}}\rangle.

(10.25)

As we will see below, the above identity allows to control
K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}f^{i}_{\nu,1}
using the bounds established in Sec.
10.1
. We also note that for
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\triangleright}\}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, or
i>i_{\triangleright}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds

\langle\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle\\
:=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq i_{\triangleright}\end{subarray}}\!\binom{m+k}{k}\langle F^{i_{0},k+m}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast f^{i_{1}}_{\nu,1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast f^{i_{k}}_{\nu,1}\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle,

(10.26)

where
\psi,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
are arbitrary. The sums in the above formulas are finite because
F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=0
for all
m>im_{\flat}
. Note that
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=0
for all
m>im_{\flat}
as well.

For completeness we state without a proof the following simple qualitative lemma.

Lemma 10.24
.

For every
\mu\in[0,1]
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the effective force coefficients
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
are uniquely defined. Moreover, for any
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
the function

[0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\mathtt{w}\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{V}^{m}

(10.27)

is continuous in the whole domain and differentiable in the domain
\mu\in(0,1]
.

Remark 10.25
.

In the proof of the lemma and the theorem stated below we will use the following equalities:
R^{i,m}=R^{i_{0},k+m}R^{i_{1},0}\ldots R^{i_{k},0}
and

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{0},k+m)+\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{1})+\sigma+\ldots+\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{k})+\sigma

(10.28)

valid for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
i,m,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i
. The first of the above identities follows from the definition
R^{i,m}=R^{1+im_{\flat}-m}
. The second one is an immediate consequence of Def.
8.8
of
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)
.

Lemma 10.26
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
10.15
there exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and a universal family of distributions
f^{i}_{0,1}=F^{i,0}_{0,1}\in\mathcal{D}
,
i\in\{0,\ldots i_{\triangleright}\}
, such that it holds

(A
1
)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}f^{i}_{0,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R^{i,0}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B
1
)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}f^{i}_{\nu,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R^{i,0}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C
1
)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(f^{i}_{\nu,1}-f^{i}_{0,1})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,0}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

and

(A
2
)

\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\ast\mathtt{w}f^{i}_{0,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R^{i,0}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)+\sigma}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B
2
)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\ast\mathtt{w}f^{i}_{\nu,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R^{i,0}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)+\sigma}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C
2
)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(f^{i}_{\nu,1}-f^{i}_{0,1})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,0}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)+\sigma-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\triangleright}\}
and all
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying the condition
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
. The constants of proportionality in the above bounds depend only on
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and are otherwise universal.

Proof.

We first note that by Lemma
10.19
(A) the bounds (A
1
), (B
1
), (C
1
) imply the bounds (A
2
), (B
2
), (C
2
). Next, we observe that
f^{0}_{\nu,1}=F^{0,0,0}_{\nu,\mu}
and define
f^{0}_{0,1}:=F^{0,0,0}_{0,\mu}
. As a result, the bounds (A
1
), (B
1
), (C
1
) hold for
i=0
by Theorem
10.15
.

Now let us fix
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and assume that the lemma holds for all
i<i_{\circ}
. We shall prove the lemma for
i=i_{\circ}
. To this end, we fix some
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
. Note that by Remark
10.23
and the support properties of the effective force coefficients stated in Lemma
7.6
it holds

\langle K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}f^{i}_{\nu,1},\psi\rangle\\
=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\end{subarray}}\langle K_{\nu,\mu}^{k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i_{0},k}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{k}}\rangle,

(10.29)

where
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
is such that
\mathtt{v}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,2i]
and
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{v}-[0,(i-1)i]
. Hence, by Def.
5.12
of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
we obtain

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}f^{i}_{\nu,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\end{subarray}}\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i_{0},k}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k}}\\[2.84526pt]
\times\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\ldots\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{k}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}

(10.30)

and

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(f^{i}_{\nu,1}-f^{i}_{0,1})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
l\leq k\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\end{subarray}}\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i_{0},k}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k}}\\[2.84526pt]
\times\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\ldots\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{l-1}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\\[5.69054pt]
\times\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{l}}-\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{l}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{l+1}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\ldots\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{k}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\\[11.38109pt]
+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\end{subarray}}\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(F^{i_{0},k}_{\nu,\mu}-F^{i_{0},k}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k}}\,\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\ldots\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{k}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}

(10.31)

These bounds remain valid for
\nu=0
. The bounds (A
1
), (B
1
), (C
1
) follow now from the induction hypothesis, Theorem
10.15
and Remark
10.25
applied with
m=0
.
∎

Remark 10.27
.

Let
\check{f}^{i}_{\nu,1}=\mathbf{Q}G_{1}\ast f^{i}_{\nu,1}
. In the next section we will need the bounds

(A)

\|\mathtt{w}\check{f}^{i}_{0,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R^{i,0}
,

(B)

\|\mathtt{w}\check{f}^{i}_{\nu,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R^{i,0}
,

(C)

\|\mathtt{w}(\check{f}^{i}_{\nu,1}-\check{f}^{i}_{0,1})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,0}
,

which easily follow from the bounds (A
1
), (B
1
), (C
1
) stated in the above lemma and the fact that
\mathbf{Q}G_{1}=-\dot{G}_{1}
is smooth and
\|\mathbf{P}^{{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{1}\|\lesssim 1
.

Remark 10.28
.

Recall that
\alpha\equiv\alpha_{\varepsilon}:=-\dim(\varPhi)-\varepsilon
. Note that
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)+\sigma\geq\alpha_{\varepsilon}
for any
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\diamond})
.

Lemma 10.29
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
10.15
there exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and a universal distribution
\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright}\in\mathcal{D}
such that

(A)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R^{i_{\triangleright},0}\,[\mu]^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R^{i_{\triangleright},0}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{\nu}-\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{0})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\delta R^{i_{\triangleright},0}\,[\mu]^{\alpha_{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

for all
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying the condition
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,2i_{\triangleright}(i_{\triangleright}+1)+2]
. The constants of proportionality in the above bounds depend only on
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and are otherwise universal.

Proof.

In the proof we use the results of the previous lemma. Recall that

\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}=\hat{G}\ast f_{\nu,1}^{\triangleright}:=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{\triangleright}}\lambda^{i}\,\hat{G}\ast f_{\nu,1}^{i}.

(10.32)

For
\nu=0
, this is the definition of the LHS. By the fact that
\hat{G}
is supported in
[0,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
and Remark
9.11
we obtain

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\hat{G}\ast\mathtt{v}f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\hat{G}\ast\mathtt{v}f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}

(10.33)

and

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{\nu}-\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{0})\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(\hat{G}\ast\mathtt{v}f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}-\hat{G}\ast\mathtt{v}f^{\triangleright}_{0})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\hat{G}\ast\mathtt{v}(f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}-f^{\triangleright}_{0})\|_{\mathcal{V}},

(10.34)

where
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
is such that
\mathtt{v}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,2]
and
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighbourhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{v}-[0,i_{\triangleright}(i_{\triangleright}+1)+2]
. The first of the above bounds is also valid for
\nu=0
. The statement of the lemma follows now from Lemma
10.19
(B) and the bounds (A
1
), (B
1
), (C
1
) proved in the previous lemma.
∎

Theorem 10.30
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
10.15
there exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and a universal family of differentiable functions

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto\hat{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m},\qquad i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

(10.35)

satisfying the flow equation (
7.14
) such that it holds

(A)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\hat{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}(\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\hat{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying the condition
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
. The constants of proportionality in the above bounds depend only on
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and are otherwise universal.

Proof.

It follows from Remark
10.23
and the support properties of the effective force coefficients stated in Lemma
7.6
that

\langle K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq i_{\triangleright}\end{subarray}}\binom{m+k}{k}\\
\times\langle K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i_{0},k+m}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f^{i_{1}}_{\nu,1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f^{i_{k}}_{\nu,1}\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle

(10.36)

for any
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{v}=1
one some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,2i]
and
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{v}-[0,(i-1)i]
. Consequently, by Def.
5.12
of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
we obtain

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq i_{\triangleright}\end{subarray}}\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i_{0},k+m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+m}}\\[2.84526pt]
\times\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f^{i_{1}}_{\nu,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\ldots\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f^{i_{k}}_{\nu,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}

(10.37)

and

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\hat{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
l\leq k\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq i_{\triangleright}\end{subarray}}\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}F^{i_{0},m+k}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+m}}\\[2.84526pt]
\times\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\ldots\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{l-1}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\\[5.69054pt]
\times\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{\nu,1}^{i_{l}}-\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{l}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{l+1}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\ldots\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{k}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\\[11.38109pt]
+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{0},\ldots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
i_{0}+\ldots+i_{k}=i\\
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\leq i_{\triangleright}\end{subarray}}\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m+k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}(F^{i_{0},m+k}_{\nu,\mu}-F^{i_{0},m+k}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m+k}}\\
\times\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\ldots\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}f_{0,1}^{i_{k}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}.

(10.38)

The bounds (A), (B), (C) follow now from Theorem
10.15
, the bounds (A
2
), (B
2
), (C
2
) stated in Lemma
10.26
and Remark
10.25
.
∎

10.4
Effect of initial condition

Let us fix some
t\in[0,\infty)
. The goal of most the remaining part of the deterministic analysis is the construction of
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
and
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}
as a function of the boundary data such that
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
is the solution of the equation

\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}=G\ast(1_{(t,t+6i_{\dagger})}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})+\delta_{t}\otimes\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}),

(10.39)

in the time interval
[t,t+T]
for sufficiently small
T\in(0,1)
depending on the constant
R
appearing in Assumptions
10.9
and
10.11
. Note that
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}(t,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})=\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}
. The time interval
(t,t+6i_{\dagger})
, where
i_{\flat}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
was introduced in Def.
2.6
, could in principle be replaced by any interval containing
(t,t+1)
. Moreover, recall that
\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi]=F_{\nu}[\varphi+\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright}]-f_{\nu}^{\triangleright}
is a local functional of
\varphi
depending only on its spatial derivatives. We remind the reader that the strategy of the proof was described in Sec.
2.3
. In Sec.
10.7
we show how to patch together the solutions defined in short time intervals and construct the maximal solution.

Definition 10.31
.

We set
f_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}:=\delta_{t}\otimes\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{0}
and
f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}:=\delta_{t}\otimes\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}
, where
\delta_{t}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
is the Dirac delta at
\mathring{x}=t
. We also set
\varPhi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}:=\hat{G}\ast f_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}
and
\varPhi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}:=\hat{G}\ast f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}
.

Remark 10.32
.

Note that
\varPhi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}
is compactly supported in time since the kernel
\hat{G}=G_{0}-G_{1}
is supported in
[0,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
. Recall that
\beta\equiv\beta_{\varepsilon}=-\dim(\phi)-\varepsilon
and
\dim(\phi)
was introduced in Def.
2.10
.

Lemma 10.33
.

Under Assumption
10.11
for
\mu\in(0,1]
and
\mathring{x}\in[t,t+T]
it holds

(A
1
)

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\varPhi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,

(B
1
)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\varPhi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\delta R\,[\nu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,

(C
1
)
