\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})-\varPhi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}))\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\delta R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
.

Moreover, for
\nu\in(0,\delta^{1/\varepsilon}]
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\sigma/2)
there exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that it holds

(A
2
)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast f_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}-\sigma}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B
2
)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R\,[\mu]^{-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(C
2
)

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast(\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}-f_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\delta R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}-\sigma-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

and

(A
3
)

\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\ast f_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B
3
)

\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim R\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C
3
)

\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\hat{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\ast(\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}-f_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\delta R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
.

The constants of proportionality in the above bounds are universal.

Proof.

The bounds (A
1
), (B
1
), (C
1
) follow immediately from Assumption
10.11
and the fact that
\sup_{\mathring{x}\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}}|\hat{G}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}<\infty
. In order to prove the remaining bounds we first show that for
\mathtt{f}={\mathtt{g}}+\mathtt{h}
, where
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
is as in Assumption
10.11
and
\mathtt{h}=\lfloor\sigma/2\rfloor+1
, it holds

(A
4
)

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B
4
)

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{\mathtt{f}}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim R\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C
4
)

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast(\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{\mathtt{f}}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}-\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\delta R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
.

The bound (A
4
) is implied by the bound (A) in Assumption
10.11
. For
\mu\in[0,\nu]
the bound (B
4
) follows from Assumption
10.11
(B) and
\|\bar{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})}=1
and for
\mu\in(\nu,1]
it is a consequence of Assumption
10.11
(A), (B) and the bound

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{\mathtt{f}}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{\mathtt{f}}}\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{h}}\ast\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})}\,\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{\mathtt{f}}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\lesssim\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{\mathtt{f}}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})},

(10.40)

where we used Lemma
4.13
(C) to obtain the last estimate. To prove the bound (C
4
) we use

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast(\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}-\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast(\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}-\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\|(\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}-1)\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{f}}\ast\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})},

(10.41)

Assumption
10.11
and the bound

\|(\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}-1)\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast\mathtt{h}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})}\lesssim[\nu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\leq\delta\,[\nu]^{\sigma-2\varepsilon}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\leq\delta[\mu]^{-\varepsilon},\quad\mu\in[\nu,1],

(10.42)

which is a consequence of Lemma
4.24
(D).

The bounds (A
2
), (B
2
), (C
2
) follow from the equality
K_{\mu}=\mathring{K}_{\mu}\otimes\bar{K}_{\mu}
and the estimate
\sup_{\mathring{x}\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathring{K}_{\mu}(\mathring{x})|\leq[\mu]^{-\sigma}
. To prove the bounds (A
3
), (B
3
), (C
3
) we use the estimate
\sup_{\mathring{x}\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}}|\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}\hat{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}\bar{x}\lesssim 1
, which follows from Remark
4.21
, and the equality
\bar{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}=\delta_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}}
.
∎

Definition 10.34
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. For
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we define

\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m):=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)\vee(3\varepsilon-\sigma),

(10.43)

where
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)
was introduced in Def.
8.8
. We also set

\varepsilon_{\triangleright}:=((i_{\triangleright}+1)\dim(\lambda)-\dim(\varPhi))/(m_{\flat}+4),

(10.44)

\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reg}}:=(\dim(\lambda)-\dim(\phi))/(m_{\flat}+4)\wedge\sigma/3,

(10.45)

where
i_{\triangleright}
is the largest integer such that
\varrho(i_{\triangleright})+\sigma\leq 0
.

Remark 10.35
.

In what follows we assume that
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\sigma}\wedge\varepsilon_{\diamond}\wedge\varepsilon_{\triangleright}\wedge\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reg}})
.

Remark 10.36
.

We remind the reader that
\varepsilon_{\sigma}=\sigma+1-\lceil\sigma\rceil
was introduced in Def.
4.5
,
\varepsilon_{\diamond}\leq\dim(\lambda)/(m_{\flat}+3)
was introduced in Def.
8.8
and
\dim(\lambda)>\dim(\phi)
,
\dim(\varPhi)\geq\dim(\phi)
by Def.
2.10
.

Remark 10.37
.

For
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\triangleright})
and
i>i_{\triangleright}
we have
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i)\geq\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{\triangleright}+1)>3\varepsilon-\sigma
. Moreover, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\diamond})
and any
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
it holds

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)\geq\varrho_{\varepsilon}(1,1)=\dim(\lambda)-\sigma-\varepsilon m_{\flat}\geq 3\varepsilon-\sigma.

(10.46)

Consequently, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\diamond}\wedge\varepsilon_{\triangleright})
we have
\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)
unless
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\triangleright}\}
and
m=0
. By construction
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=0
for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\triangleright}\}
and
m=0
. As a result, one can replace
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)
by
\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)
in Theorem
10.30
.

Definition 10.38
.

Given a boundary data for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
in the sense of Def.
10.1
we define the effective force coefficients
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
, by the relation

\langle\check{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle\equiv\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\lambda^{i}\,\langle\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle\\
:=\langle\hat{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi+(G-G_{\mu})\ast\check{f}^{\triangleright}_{\nu}+(G_{\mu}-G_{1})\ast f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}]+\check{f}^{\triangleright}_{\nu}-f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}},\psi\rangle,

(10.47)

where
\psi,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
are arbitrary,
\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright}:=\mathbf{Q}G_{1}\ast f_{\nu}^{\triangleright}
,
f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}=\delta_{t}\otimes\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}
and
\hat{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
,
f_{\nu}^{\triangleright}
were introduced in Def.
10.20
. We omit
\mu
if
\mu=0
.

Remark 10.39
.

By Remark
2.13
the effective force
\check{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
satisfies the flow equation (
2.5
) in the sense of formal power series. Hence, the coefficients
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
satisfy the flow equation (
7.14
). We have
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=0
for all
m>im_{\flat}
. We note that an analog of Lemma
10.24
holds true for
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
as well. Observe also that

\check{F}_{\nu}[\varphi]=\check{F}_{\nu,0}[\varphi]=\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi+\varPhi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}]+\check{f}^{\triangleright}_{\nu}-f_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}=F_{\nu}[\varphi+\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{\nu}+\varPhi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}]-\mathbf{Q}\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{\nu}+\delta_{0}\otimes\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}.

(10.48)

Theorem 10.40
.

Fix some
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
,
R>1
,
\delta\in(0,1]
and an admissible boundary data for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
. Let
\nu\in(0,\delta^{1/\varepsilon}]
and a boundary data for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
be arbitrary such that all of the conditions specified in Assumptions
10.9
and
10.11
are satisfied. There exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and a universal family of differentiable functions

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m},\qquad i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

(10.49)

satisfying the flow equation (
7.14
) such that

(A)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}

\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}(\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]

for all
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying the condition
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i(i+1)]
. The constants of proportionality in the above bounds depend only on
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathtt{w},\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and are otherwise universal.

Remark 10.41
.

The theorem is proved using the technique of the proof of Theorem
10.30
with the use of the bounds proved in Lemma
10.33
and Remark
10.27
as well as the identity
R^{i,m}=R^{i,k+m}R^{k}
and the bound

\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)\leq\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,k+m)+k\beta_{\varepsilon}

(10.50)

valid for
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let us prove the last bound. Without loss of generality we assume that
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Recall that
\beta_{\varepsilon}=-\dim(\phi)-\varepsilon
. We have

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,k+m)+k\beta_{\varepsilon}=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)+k(\dim(\varPhi)+\varepsilon+\beta_{\varepsilon})\geq\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m),

(10.51)

where the last inequality is a consequence of the bound
\beta_{\varepsilon}\geq-\dim(\varPhi)-\varepsilon
, which follows from Def.
2.10
. Furthermore, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\diamond}\wedge\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reg}})
we have

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,k+m)+k\beta_{\varepsilon}\geq\varrho_{\varepsilon}(1,1)+\beta_{\varepsilon}+(k-1)(\dim(\varPhi)+\varepsilon+\beta_{\varepsilon})\geq 3\varepsilon-\sigma,

(10.52)

where we used
\beta_{\varepsilon}\geq-\dim(\lambda)+\varepsilon(m_{\flat}+3)
and
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(1,1)=\dim(\lambda)-\sigma-\varepsilon m_{\flat}
. This proves the bound (
10.50
).

Definition 10.42
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. For
V\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
we define

\|V\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}:=\\
\sup_{\bar{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{M}^{m}}|V(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})|\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{x}\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m}.

(10.53)

Recall that
x=(\mathring{x},\bar{x})\in\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
. If
m=0
, then we write
\|V\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}=\|V\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}}
.

Lemma 10.43
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
k\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
,
G\in\mathcal{G}
,
W\in\mathcal{V}^{k+1}
and
U\in\mathcal{V}^{m-k}
. We have

\|\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{m}}\leq\|G\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|W\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k+1}}\|U\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}.

(10.54)

Proof.

Recall that by Def.
5.27

\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m})\\
:=\int_{\mathbb{M}^{2}}W(x;\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{k})\,G(y-z)\,U(z;\mathrm{d}y_{k+1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m})\,\mathrm{d}z,

(10.55)

We have

\|\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{m}}\\
\leq\sup_{\bar{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{M}^{m+2}}|W(x;\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{k})|\,|G(y-z)|\,|U(z;\mathrm{d}y_{k+1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m})|\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{x}\mathrm{d}z\\
\leq\sup_{\bar{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{M}^{k+1}}|W(x;\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{k})|\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{x}\\
\times\int_{\mathbb{M}}|G(z)|\,\mathrm{d}z~\sup_{x\in\mathbb{M}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{m-k}}|U(x;\mathrm{d}y_{k+1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m})|.

(10.56)

This coincides with the RHS of the bound (
10.54
).
∎

Definition 10.44
.

Given a boundary data for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
in the sense of Def.
10.1
the effective force coefficients
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, are defined recursively by the flow equation (
7.14
) together with the conditions

\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=0,~~m>im_{\flat},\qquad\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,0}=\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}=1_{(t,t+6i_{\dagger})}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu},~~i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

(10.57)

where the coefficients
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu\phantom{0}}=\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,0}
are constructed as explained in Def.
10.38
. We also define
\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]
and
\tilde{F}_{\nu}[\varphi]=\tilde{F}_{\nu,0}[\varphi]
by an analog of Eq. (
10.5
).

Remark 10.45
.

Since
F^{i,m}_{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m}
is supported on the diagonal the same is true for
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m}
and
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m}
by Def.
10.20
and Def.
10.44
. Furthermore, it holds

\tilde{F}_{\nu}[\varphi]=1_{(t,t+6i_{\dagger})}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi+\varPhi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright}),\qquad\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi]=F_{\nu}[\varphi+\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{\nu}]-f^{\triangleright}_{\nu},

(10.58)

where
f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}
was introduced in Def.
10.20
,
\varPhi^{\triangleright}_{\nu}=\hat{G}\ast f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}
and
\check{f}^{\triangleright}_{\nu}=\mathbf{Q}G_{1}\ast f^{\triangleright}_{\nu}
. We used the fact that the measure
1_{(t,t+6i_{\dagger})}\delta_{t}=0
vanishes.

Remark 10.46
.

Recall that
F^{i,m}_{\nu}=F^{i,m}_{\nu,0}
vanishes unless
i\leq i_{\flat}
and
m\leq m_{\flat}
. In consequence, by Remark
10.23

\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}=\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,0}
vanishes unless
i\leq i_{\dagger}
and
m\leq m_{\flat}
, where
i_{\dagger}=i_{\flat}+i_{\triangleright}m_{\flat}
. It follows that
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}=\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,0}
and
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}=\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,0}
vanish unless
i\leq i_{\dagger}
and
m\leq m_{\flat}
as well.

Lemma 10.47
.

For
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the effective force coefficients
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
are uniquely defined. Moreover, the function

[0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\in\mathcal{V}^{m}

(10.59)

is continuous in the whole domain and differentiable in the domain
\mu\in(0,1]
.

Remark 10.48
.

This simple qualitative lemma can be proved using the strategy of the proof of Lemma
7.5
; details are omitted.

Lemma 10.49
.

Let
\mu\in[0,1]
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be arbitrary. The following equality
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
is satisfied in the domain

\{(x,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{1+m}\,|\,\mathring{x}\in(t+2i\mu,t+6i_{\dagger})\}.

(10.60)

Proof.

We first note that the statement of the lemma is true if
i=0
or
\mu=0
or
m>im_{\flat}
. Next, we fix some
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and assume that the statement is true for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that either
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. We shall prove the statement for
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
. Since

\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,\qquad\partial_{\mu}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(10.61)

it is enough to show that
\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\partial_{\mu}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
in the domain (
10.60
). We first note that by the flow equation (
7.14
) and the induction hypothesis it holds

\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}(k+1)\,\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}_{\mu},\check{F}^{j,k+1}_{\nu,\mu},\tilde{F}^{i-j,m-k}_{\nu,\mu}\big)

(10.62)

in the domain (
10.60
). Next, we observe that for any
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds

\mathrm{supp}\,\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\subset\{(x,x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{1+m}\,|\,\mathring{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathring{x}_{m}\in[\mathring{x}-2(i-1)\mu,\mathring{x}]\}.

(10.63)

The proof of this fact is the same as the proof of Lemma
7.6
. Using the above support property, the fact that
\mathrm{supp}\,\dot{G}_{\mu}\subset[0,2\mu]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
and the induction hypothesis we obtain

\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}(k+1)\,\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}_{\mu},\check{F}^{j,k+1}_{\nu,\mu},\check{F}^{i-j,m-k}_{\nu,\mu}\big)

(10.64)

in the domain (
10.60
). By the flow equation (
7.14
) the RHS of the above equation coincides with
\partial_{\mu}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
. This completes the proof of the inductive step.
∎

Lemma 10.50
.

For every
\mu\in[0,1]
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds

\mathrm{supp}\,\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\subset([t,t+6i_{\dagger}]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})^{1+m}.

(10.65)

Remark 10.51
.

This can be proved using the strategy of the proof of the previous lemma.

Theorem 10.52
.

Let
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
[t-i_{\dagger}(i_{\dagger}+1),t+6i_{\dagger}]
. Fix some
R>1
,
\delta\in(0,1]
and an admissible boundary data for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
. Let
\nu\in(0,\delta^{1/\varepsilon}]
and a boundary data for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
be arbitrary such that all of the conditions specified in Assumptions
10.9
and
10.11
are satisfied. There exists
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and a universal family of differentiable functions

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\in\mathcal{D}^{m},\qquad i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

(10.66)

satisfying the flow equation (
7.14
) such that for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds

(A)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(C)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

The constants of proportionality in the above bounds depend only on
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
and are otherwise universal.

Remark 10.53
.

The proof relies crucially on the results established in Theorem
10.40
and the fact that the force coefficients
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
coincide with
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
in the domain specified in Lemma
10.49
and vanish outside the domain specified in Lemma
10.50
. We will also use the equality
R^{i,m}=R^{j,k+1}\,R^{i-j,m-k}
and the bound

\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)\leq\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(j,k+1)+\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i-j,m-k)+\sigma,

(10.67)

valid for
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,i\}
,
k\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
, and which follows from the definition of
\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}
and Eq. (
10.8
).

Proof.

We first note that the theorem is trivially true if
m>im_{\diamond}
since in this case
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=0
and
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}:=0
. Next, we fix some
i_{\circ},m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and assume that the theorem is true for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that either
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. We shall prove the theorem for
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
. We note that the strategy of the proof of the induction step is different in the cases
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\dagger}\}
and
i>i_{\dagger}
.

Let
\mathtt{v}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\mathtt{v}(\mathring{x})=1
for
\mathring{x}\in(-\infty,t+2i_{\dagger}]
and
\mathtt{v}(\mathring{x})=0
for
\mathring{x}\in[t+3i_{\dagger},\infty)
. For
\mu\in(0,1]
we define
\mathtt{v}_{\mu},\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
by

\begin{gathered}\mathtt{v}_{\mu}(\mathring{x}):=1-\mathtt{v}(\mathring{x}/\mu)-\mathtt{v}((6i_{\dagger}-\mathring{x})/\mu),\\
\mathtt{w}_{\mu}(\mathring{x}):=\mathtt{v}(\mathring{x}/\mu)-\mathtt{v}(-\mathring{x}/\mu)+\mathtt{v}((6i_{\dagger}-\mathring{x})/\mu)-\mathtt{v}((\mathring{x}-6i_{\dagger})/\mu).\end{gathered}

(10.68)

Note that
\partial_{\mu}\mathtt{w}_{\mu}=-\partial_{\mu}\mathtt{v}_{\mu}
on some neighborhood of
[t,t+6i_{\dagger}]
and

\begin{gathered}\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{v}_{\mu}\subset[t+2i_{\dagger}\mu,t+6i_{\dagger}-2i_{\dagger}\mu],\\
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\subset[t-3i_{\dagger}\mu,t+3i_{\dagger}\mu]\cup[t+6i_{\dagger}-3i_{\dagger}\mu,t+6i_{\dagger}+3i_{\dagger}\mu].\end{gathered}

(10.69)

Hence, by Lemma
10.49
and Lemma
10.50
we obtain

\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathtt{v}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}+(1-\mathtt{v}_{\mu})\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathtt{v}_{\mu}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}+\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}

(10.70)

and

\partial_{\mu}(\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu})=\mathtt{w}_{\mu}(\partial_{\mu}^{\phantom{i}}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu})-(\partial_{\mu}\mathtt{v}_{\mu})\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathtt{w}_{\mu}(\partial_{\mu}^{\phantom{i}}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu})-(\partial_{\mu}\mathtt{v}_{\mu})\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}

(10.71)

for any
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\dagger}\}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

We first observe that by Lemma
10.54
(A) applied with
\mathtt{v}_{\mu}/\mathtt{w}
we have

(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{1})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}_{\mu}\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\!\lesssim\!\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
,

(\check{\mathrm{B}}_{1})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}_{\mu}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\!\lesssim\!\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
,

(\check{\mathrm{C}}_{1})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{v}_{\mu}(\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}(\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
,

where
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
is arbitrary such that
\mathtt{w}=1
on some neighborhood of
[t,t+6i_{\dagger}]
. Next, we note that it holds
\partial_{\mu}\mathtt{v}_{\mu}=[\mu]^{-\sigma}\dot{\mathtt{v}}_{\mu}
, where

\dot{\mathtt{v}}_{\mu}(\mathring{x}):=\dot{\mathtt{v}}(\mathring{x}/\mu)-\dot{\mathtt{v}}((6i_{\dagger}-\mathring{x})/\mu),\quad\dot{\mathtt{v}}(\mathring{x}):=-\mathring{x}\,\partial_{\mathring{x}}\mathtt{v}(\mathring{x}).

(10.72)

Moreover, we have

\mathrm{supp}\,\dot{\mathtt{v}}\subset[t+2i_{\dagger}\mu,t+3i_{\dagger}\mu]\cup[t+6i_{\dagger}-3i_{\dagger}\mu,t+6i_{\dagger}-2i_{\dagger}\mu].

(10.73)

Hence, the application of Lemma
10.54
(B) with
\mathtt{u}_{\mu}=\dot{\mathtt{v}}_{\mu}/\mathtt{w}
allows to conclude that it holds

(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{2})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast(\partial_{\mu}\mathtt{v}_{\mu})\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
,

(\check{\mathrm{B}}_{2})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast(\partial_{\mu}\mathtt{v}_{\mu})\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
,

(\check{\mathrm{C}}_{2})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast(\partial_{\mu}\mathtt{v}_{\mu})(\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}(\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
,

where
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
is again arbitrary such that
\mathtt{w}=1
on some neighborhood of
[t,t+6i_{\dagger}]
. Recall also that by Theorem
10.40
it holds

(\check{\mathrm{A}})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(\check{\mathrm{B}})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(\check{\mathrm{C}})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}(\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

for
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\dagger}\}
provided
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
is chosen in such a way that in addition
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathtt{w}-[0,i_{\dagger}(i_{\dagger}+1)]
, which is always possible by our assumption on
\mathtt{u}
. By the flow equation (
7.14
) we obtain

\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=-\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}(k+1)\,\mathbf{Y}_{\pi}\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}_{\mu},\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{j,k+1}_{\nu,\mu},\tilde{F}^{i-j,m-k}_{\nu,\mu}\big).

(10.74)

For
\nu=0
the flow equation (
7.14
) is the definition of
\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}
. Consequently, this equality remains valid for
\nu=0
. Note that for
i=0
the RHS of the flow equation vanishes identically. The above flow equation together with Lemma
10.43
imply that

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i,m}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{m}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}(k+1)\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\\
\times\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{j,k+1}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k+1}}\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}

(10.75)

and

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i,m}-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}^{i,m})\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{m}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}(k+1)\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\\
\times\Big(\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{j,k+1}-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}^{j,k+1})\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k+1}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}\\[2.84526pt]
+\|K_{0,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}^{j,k+1}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k+1}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k}-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}^{i-j,m-k})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}\Big).

(10.76)

These bounds are also valid for
\nu=0
. Let us recall that by Lemma
4.23
it holds

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon}[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}

(10.77)

uniformly in
\nu\in[0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,1]
. As a result, by the induction hypothesis and Lemma
10.54
(A) we obtain

(\tilde{\mathrm{A}}_{1})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{B}}_{1})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}_{1})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

Hence, by Eq. (
10.71
) and the results established earlier we arrive at

(\tilde{\mathrm{A}}_{2})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}(\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{B}}_{2})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}(\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu})\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}_{2})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}(\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

We observe that by the flow equation (
7.14
) and Remark
5.33
the bounds (
10.75
) and (
10.76
) remain true when
\mathtt{w}_{\mu}
is replaced with
1
and
{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}
is replaced with
\mathcal{V}^{m}
. As a result,

(\tilde{\mathrm{A}}_{3})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{B}}_{3})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}_{3})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
.

Now we show how to use the results gathered above in order to conclude the proof of the induction step. We first consider the case
i>i_{\dagger}
or
\mu\in[0,\nu)
. We note that

\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(10.78)

For
\nu=0
and
i>i_{\dagger}
we define
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0}=0
and use the above equation with
\nu=0
to define
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
. Using
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}=1_{(t,t+6i_{\dagger})}\check{F}_{\nu}^{i,m}
and the bound
(\check{\mathrm{B}})
aplied with
\mu=0
we obtain

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,0}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,0}\ast\mathtt{w}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\nu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}

(10.79)

for any
\mathtt{w}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathtt{w}=1
on some neighborhood of
[t,t+6i_{\diamond}]
. We note that
\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon\geq 0
for
i>i_{\dagger}
by Remark
8.11
. As a result, for
i>i_{\dagger}
the bounds
(\tilde{\mathrm{A}}_{3})
,
(\tilde{\mathrm{B}}_{3})
,
(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}_{3})
imply

(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}^{\prime}_{3})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-2\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
.

Hence, for
i>i_{\dagger}
or
\mu\in[0,\nu)
the theorem follows from Lemma
8.42
and the bounds
(\tilde{\mathrm{A}}_{3})
,
(\tilde{\mathrm{B}}_{3})
,
(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}^{\prime}_{3})
.

It remains to investigate the case
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\dagger}\}
. We note that by the previous paragraph and Lemma
10.54
(B) applied with
\mathtt{u}_{\mu}=\mathtt{w}_{\mu}
we have

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\sigma}\,\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\sigma}\,[\nu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}

(10.80)

for any
\mu\in[0,\nu)
. We have

\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}(\mathtt{w}_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta,\qquad\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\mathtt{v}_{\mu}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}+\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}.

(10.81)

For
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\dagger}\}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we define

(\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}):=\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}(\mathtt{w}_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta,\qquad\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}:=\mathtt{v}_{\mu}\check{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}+(\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}).

(10.82)

Consequently, by Lemma
4.10
it holds

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\leq\int_{0}^{\mu}\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\eta}\ast\partial_{\eta}(\mathtt{w}_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\eta})\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(10.83)

and

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast(\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\\
\leq\int_{0}^{\mu}\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\eta}\ast(\partial_{\eta}(\mathtt{w}_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\eta})-\partial_{\eta}(\mathtt{w}_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\eta}))\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(10.84)

The estimate (
10.83
) remains valid for
\nu=0
. Since
\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)\geq 3\varepsilon-\sigma
by the bounds
(\tilde{\mathrm{A}}_{2})
,
(\tilde{\mathrm{B}}_{2})
,
(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}_{2})
we have

(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}^{\prime}_{2})

​
\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}(\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\!\lesssim\!\delta R^{i,m}[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)+\sigma-2\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
.

Using the bounds
(\tilde{\mathrm{A}}_{2})
,
(\tilde{\mathrm{B}}_{2})
,
(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}^{\prime}_{2})
as well as the estimates (
10.83
) and (
10.84
) we obtain

(\tilde{\mathrm{A}}_{4})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)+\sigma}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{B}}_{4})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}_{4})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon+\sigma}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
.

As a result, by Lemma
10.54
(C) it holds

(\tilde{\mathrm{A}}_{5})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{B}}_{5})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim R^{i,m}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,

(\tilde{\mathrm{C}}_{5})

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{w}_{\mu}(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\delta R^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
.

To finish the proof of the inductive step in the case
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\dagger}\}
it is now enough to use Eq. (
10.70
), the above bounds as well as the bounds
(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{1})
,
(\check{\mathrm{B}}_{1})
,
(\check{\mathrm{C}}_{1})
and
(\check{\mathrm{A}})
,
(\check{\mathrm{B}})
,
(\check{\mathrm{C}})
.
∎

Lemma 10.54
.

Let
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathtt{u}_{\mu}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
,
\mu\in(0,1]
.

(A)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}_{\mu}V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
if
\forall_{\mathtt{f}\in\{0,\ldots,{\mathtt{g}}\}}\|\partial_{\mathring{x}}^{\mathtt{f}}\mathtt{u}_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\mu^{-\mathtt{f}}
.

(B)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\mathtt{u}_{\mu}V\big\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,[\mu]^{\sigma}
if
\forall_{\mathtt{f}\in\{0,\ldots,{\mathtt{g}}\}}\|\partial_{\mathring{x}}^{\mathtt{f}}\mathtt{u}_{\mu}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\mu^{1-\mathtt{f}}
.

(C)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}+1}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast V\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}}\,[\mu]^{-\sigma}
.

All of the above bounds are uniform in
\nu\in[0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,1]
and
V\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
.

Proof.

We consider below only the case
m=0
. The general case can be proved using exactly the same technique, but the notation is more complicated. For
m=0
we have
K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}=K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}
and
\mathcal{V}^{m}=\mathcal{V}
,
{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{m}={\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}
. Let us set
V=v
. By Lemma
9.10
it holds

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}_{\mu}v\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\sum_{\mathtt{f}=0}^{\mathtt{g}}\mu^{\mathtt{f}}\,\|(\partial_{\mathring{x}}^{\mathtt{f}}\mathtt{u}_{\mu})(K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast v)\|_{\mathcal{V}}.

(10.85)

The inspection of the proof of Lemma
9.10
shows that the same bound holds true for
\mathcal{V}
replaced with
{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}
. As a result, we obtain

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}_{\mu}v\|_{\mathcal{V}}\lesssim\sum_{\mathtt{f}=0}^{\mathtt{g}}\mu^{\mathtt{f}}\,\|\partial_{\mathring{x}}^{\mathtt{f}}\mathtt{u}_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast v\|_{\mathcal{V}}

(10.86)

and

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\mathtt{u}_{\mu}v\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}\lesssim\sum_{\mathtt{f}=0}^{\mathtt{g}}\mu^{\mathtt{f}}\,\|\partial_{\mathring{x}}^{\mathtt{f}}\mathtt{u}_{\mu}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\,\|K_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast v\|_{\mathcal{V}}.

(10.87)

These estimates together with the assumed bounds for
\mathtt{u}_{\mu}
imply Parts (A) and (B). Part (C) follows from

\|K_{\mu}^{\ast({\mathtt{g}}+1)}\ast v\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast v\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}}~\sup_{x\in\mathbb{M}}\sup_{\mathring{z}\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}}K_{\mu}(x-z)\,\mathrm{d}\bar{z}\lesssim\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast v\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}}\,[\mu]^{-\sigma}.

(10.88)

This completes the proof.
∎

10.5
Convergence of perturbative expansion

Definition 10.55
.

For
\tilde{R}>0
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we set

\tilde{R}^{i,m}:=\frac{\tilde{R}^{1+2(im_{\flat}-m)}}{4(i+1)^{2}\,4(m+1)^{2}}.

(10.89)

Theorem 10.56
.

There exists a constant
\tilde{c}\geq 1
and
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that under assumptions of Theorem
10.52
it holds:

(A)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\tilde{R}^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(B)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\tilde{R}^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)\vee 0}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)\vee 0}
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,

(C)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq 2\delta\tilde{R}^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}
,
\mu\in[\nu,1]
,

where
\tilde{R}=\tilde{c}\,R
.

Remark 10.57
.

Recall that by Remark
10.46

\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,0}
vanishes unless
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\dagger}\}
. The same is true for
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,0}
since
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,0}
vanishes unless
\varrho(i,m)\leq 0
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can restrict attention to the case
m\leq im_{\flat}
since otherwise
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
is identically zero. Theorem
10.52
and Lemma
4.23
imply that there exist
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\tilde{c}\geq 1
such that the theorem holds for all
i\leq i_{\dagger}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Moreover we choose
\tilde{c}\geq 1
in such a way that the bound

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\leq C^{-1}\,\tilde{R}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon}

(10.90)

is satisfied for all
\nu\in[0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,1]
, where
\tilde{R}=\tilde{c}\,R
and

C:=(1/\varrho_{\varepsilon}^{\diamond}+1/(\dim(\varPhi)+\varepsilon))\sigma+3i_{\dagger}m_{\flat}\,\sigma/\varepsilon,\qquad\varrho_{\varepsilon}^{\diamond}:=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{\dagger}+1)-\varepsilon>0.

(10.91)

The existence of such
\tilde{c}
follows from Lemma
4.23
. We note that

\frac{(m+1)\sigma}{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon}+\frac{(m+1)\sigma}{\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)}\leq\frac{(m+1)\sigma}{\varrho_{\varepsilon}^{\diamond}+(\dim(\varPhi)+\varepsilon)m}+\frac{(im_{\flat}+1)\sigma}{\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)}\leq C,

(10.92)

for
i>i_{\dagger}
and
m\leq im_{\flat}
. Now let us fix some
i_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
i_{\circ}>i_{\dagger}
, and
m_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Assume that theorem is true for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that either
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. We shall prove the theorem for all
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
. By the flow equation (
7.14
), Remark
5.33
we have

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i,m}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}(k+1)\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\\
\times\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{j,k+1}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}

(10.93)

and

\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i,m}-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}^{i,m})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}(k+1)\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\\[2.84526pt]
\times\Big(\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{j,k+1}-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}^{j,k+1})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}\\[5.69054pt]
+\|K_{0,\mu}^{k+1;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}^{j,k+1}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{k+1}}\,\|K_{\nu,\mu}^{m-k;{\mathtt{g}}}\ast(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}^{i-j,m-k}-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}^{i-j,m-k})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}\Big).

(10.94)

These bounds remain valid for
\nu=0
. Using the inequality

\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{1}{4(1+j)^{2}\,4(1+i-j)^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4\,(1+i)^{2}}

(10.95)

we obtain

\tilde{R}~\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\tilde{R}^{j,k+1}\tilde{R}^{i-j,m-k}\leq\tilde{R}^{i,m}.

(10.96)

The bounds stated above, the bound (
10.67
) and the induction hypothesis imply

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq C^{-1}\,(m+1)\,\tilde{R}^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma},

(10.97)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\\
\leq C^{-1}\,(m+1)\,\tilde{R}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\tilde{R}^{j,k+1}\tilde{R}^{i-j,m-k}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)}\\
\leq C^{-1}\,(m+1)\,\tilde{R}^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)},

(10.98)

\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\\
\leq 2\delta C^{-1}\,(m+1)\,\tilde{R}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\tilde{R}^{j,k+1}\tilde{R}^{i-j,m-k}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon i/i_{\dagger}}\\
\leq 2\delta C^{-1}\,(m+1)\,\tilde{R}^{i,m}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon(i/i_{\dagger}-1)-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon i/i_{\dagger}}.

(10.99)

These bounds are valid for all
\mu\in(0,1]
. For
i>i_{\dagger}
it holds
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,0}=0
and

\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}=\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,\qquad\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}=\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\eta}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(10.100)

As a result, the induction step is a consequence of the bounds (
10.92
) and Lemma
8.42
applied with
n=1
,
\epsilon=\varepsilon\,(i/i_{\dagger}-1)>0
. This completes the proof.
∎

Definition 10.58
.

Let
{\mathtt{g}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be as in Theorem
10.56
.

Definition 10.59
.

The families of sets
\mathcal{V}_{\nu,\mu}
,
\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}
,
(\nu,\mu)\in[0,1]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
, are defined as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\nu,\mu}

\displaystyle:=\{J_{\nu}\ast K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\varphi\in\mathcal{V}\,|\,\varphi\in\mathcal{V}\},

(10.101)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}

\displaystyle:=\{J_{\nu}\ast K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\varphi\in\mathcal{V}\,|\,\varphi\in\mathcal{V},\,\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{V}}<3/4\}.

For sufficiently small
T
and
(\nu,\mu)\in[0,T]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
we define the functionals

\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}\,:\,\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}\to\mathcal{D},\qquad\mathrm{D}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}\,:\,\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}\times\mathcal{V}_{\nu,\mu}\to\mathcal{D}

(10.102)

by the following formulas

\langle\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\lambda^{i}\,\langle\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle,

(10.103)

\langle\mathrm{D}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi,\zeta],\psi\rangle:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\lambda^{i}\,(m+1)\,\langle\tilde{F}^{i,m+1}_{\nu,\mu},\psi\otimes\zeta\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle,

(10.104)

where
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}
and
\zeta\in\mathcal{V}_{\nu,\mu}
.

Remark 10.60
.

By Lemma
4.10
if
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}
, then
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\eta}
for any
\eta
in a small enough neighbourhood of
\mu
.

Theorem 10.61
.

The bounds established in Theorem
10.56
imply that for
T
such that

0<T\leq(|\lambda|\vee\tilde{R})^{-4m_{\flat}(i_{\triangleright}+1)/\varepsilon}\leq 1

(10.105)

the functionals
\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}
and
\mathrm{D}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}
are well defined for all
(\nu,\mu)\in[0,T]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and the following statements are true.

(A)

For any
\mu\in[0,T]
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}
and
\zeta\in\mathcal{V}_{\nu,\mu}
it holds

\begin{gathered}\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq[\nu\vee\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma},\\
\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathrm{D}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi,\zeta]\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq[\nu\vee\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}\zeta\|_{\mathcal{V}}.\end{gathered}

(10.106)

These bounds are also valid for
\nu=0
and
\mu\in(0,T]
.

(B)

For any
\mu\in[0,T]
,
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}
it holds

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi_{1}]-\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi_{2}])\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq[\nu\vee\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}\,\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{\mathtt{g}}(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})\|_{\mathcal{V}}.

(10.107)

This bound is also valid for
\nu=0
and
\mu\in(0,T]
.

(C)

For any
\nu,\mu\in(0,T]^{2}
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}
it holds

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[\varphi]-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}[\varphi])\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq 2\delta\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}.

(10.108)

(D)

Given
\mu\in(0,T]
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu}
the flow equation

\partial_{\eta}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\eta}[\varphi]=-\mathrm{D}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\eta}[\varphi,\dot{G}_{\eta}\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu,\eta}[\varphi]]

(10.109)

is satisfied for all
\eta
in some sufficiently small neighbourhood of
\mu
. This remains valid for
\nu=0
.

Proof.

We first prove the following bound

\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon i/(i_{\triangleright}+1)\geq 2\varepsilon-\sigma

(10.110)

for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Since
\tilde{\varrho}_{\varepsilon}(i,m)=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)\vee(3\varepsilon-\sigma)
the bound holds trivially for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\triangleright}+1\}
. For
i>i_{\triangleright}+1
we use

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\varepsilon i/(i_{\triangleright}+1)\geq\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{\triangleright}+1)-\varepsilon+(i-i_{\triangleright}-1)(\dim(\lambda)-\varepsilon m_{\flat}-\varepsilon)

(10.111)

and
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{\triangleright}+1)>3\varepsilon-\sigma
as well as
\dim(\lambda)>\varepsilon(m_{\flat}+3)
. As a result, we obtain the following estimate

|\lambda|^{i}\,\tilde{R}^{1+2(im_{\flat}-m)}[\nu\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)\vee(3\varepsilon-\sigma)}\\
\leq|\lambda|^{i}\,\tilde{R}^{1+2(im_{\flat}-m)}\,T^{\varepsilon i/(i_{\triangleright}+1)}[\nu\vee\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}\leq[\nu\vee\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}

(10.112)

for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\nu,\mu\in[0,T]^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tilde{R}>1
such that the bound (
10.105
) is satisfied. Consequently, for
T
satisfying the condition (
10.105
) by Theorem
10.56
we have

(A
0
)

|\lambda|^{i}\,\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{0,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq[\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}/(4(1+i)^{2}\,4(1+m)^{2})
,

\mu\in(0,T]
,

(B
0
)

|\lambda|^{i}\,\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq[\nu\vee\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}/(4(1+i)^{2}\,4(1+m)^{2})
,

\mu\in[0,T]
,

(C
0
)

|\lambda|^{i}\,\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq 2\delta\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}/(4(1+i)^{2}\,4(1+m)^{2})
,

\mu\in(0,T]
.

Note that for
\mu\in(0,\nu]
the third bound is a consequence of the first two.

Let
\varphi,\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\zeta\in\mathcal{V}
be such that
\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{V}},\|\varphi_{1}\|_{\mathcal{V}},\|\varphi_{2}\|_{\mathcal{V}}<3/4
. It follows from Def.
5.12
of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
that the following bounds are satisfied:

(A)

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[J_{\nu}\ast K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\varphi]\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}|\lambda|^{i}\,\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{m},

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\mathrm{D}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[J_{\nu}\ast K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\varphi,J_{\nu}\ast K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\zeta]\|_{\mathcal{V}}\\
\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}(m+1)\,|\lambda|^{i}\,\|K^{m+1;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m+1}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m+1}}\,\|\zeta\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{m},

(B)

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[J_{\nu}\ast K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\varphi_{1}]-\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[J_{\nu}\ast K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\varphi_{2}])\|_{\mathcal{V}}\\
\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{m}|\lambda|^{i}\,\|K^{m+1;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m+1}_{\nu,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m+1}}\,\|\varphi_{1}\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{k}\,\|\varphi_{2}\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{m-k}\,\|\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\|_{\mathcal{V}},

(C)

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[J_{\nu}\ast K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\varphi]-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}[J_{\nu}\ast K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\varphi])\|_{\mathcal{V}}\\
\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}|\lambda|^{i}\,\|K^{m;{\mathtt{g}}}_{\nu,\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}-\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{0,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{m}.

In order to show Parts (A), (B) and (C) of the theorem it is enough use the above bounds and the estimates

\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4(1+i)^{2}}\frac{(3/4)^{m}}{4(1+m)^{2}}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4(1+i)^{2}}\frac{(3/4)^{m}}{4(1+m)}\leq 1.

(10.113)

Part (D) follows from the fact that the coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\nu,\mu}
satisfy the flow equation (
7.14
), the absolute convergence of the series defining the functionals
\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}
and
\mathrm{D}\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}
and the bound
\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon}[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}
.
∎

10.6
Local solution theory

Lemma 10.62
.

Let
T\in(0,1]
be as in Theorem
10.61
. Set
\tilde{f}_{0,T}:=\tilde{F}_{0,T}[0]
and
\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}:=\tilde{F}_{\nu,T}[0]
. We have:

(A
1
)

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{f}_{0,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq[\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}
,
\mu\in(0,T]
,

(B
1
)

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq[\nu\vee\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}
,
\mu\in[0,T]
,

(C
1
)

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast(\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}-\tilde{f}_{0,T})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq 6\delta\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}
,
\mu\in(0,T]

and

(A
2
)

\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{0,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq 1/3
,
\mu\in[0,T]
,

(B
2
)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq 1/3
,
\mu\in[0,T]
,

(C
2
)

\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast(\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}-\tilde{f}_{0,T})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\delta
,
\mu\in[0,T]
.

Moreover,
\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}=\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}]
for
\mu\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

We first note that the lemma is true for
\mu=T
by Theorem
10.61
(A), (C). Next, choose
\kappa\in[0,T]
and assume that the bounds (A
1
), (B
1
), (C
1
) hold true for all
\mu\in[\kappa,T]
. Then by Lemma
4.10
we have

\begin{gathered}\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\eta}\ast\tilde{f}_{0,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\tilde{R}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{f}_{0,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\tilde{R}\,[\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}\leq\tilde{R}\,[\eta]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma},\\
\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\eta}\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\tilde{R}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\tilde{R}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma}\leq\tilde{R}\,[\nu\vee\eta]^{2\varepsilon-\sigma},\\
\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\eta}\ast(\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}-\tilde{f}_{0,T})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\tilde{R}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast(\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}-\tilde{f}_{\nu,0})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq 6\delta\tilde{R}\,[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\leq 6\delta\tilde{R}\,[\eta]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\end{gathered}

(10.114)

for all
\eta\in[\tau\mu,\mu]
,
\mu\in[\kappa,T]
and some
\tau\in(0,1)
that depends only on
\tilde{R}
. Recall the bound (
10.90
)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\dot{G}_{\mu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\leq C^{-1}\,\tilde{R}~[\mu]^{\varepsilon-\sigma}\,[\nu\vee\mu]^{\sigma-\varepsilon},\qquad C\leq 3\sigma/\varepsilon,

(10.115)

and note that the bound (
10.105
) implies
\tilde{R}^{2}\,T^{\varepsilon}\leq 1
. Consequently, we obtain

\begin{gathered}\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{0,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\int_{\mu}^{T}\|\mathbf{P}_{\eta}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\eta}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\eta}\ast\tilde{f}_{0,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\leq 1/3,\\
\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\int_{\mu}^{T}\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\eta}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\eta}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\eta}\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\leq 1/3,\\
\|\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{{\mathtt{g}}}(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast(\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}-\tilde{f}_{0,T})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\int_{\mu}^{T}\|\mathbf{P}_{\eta}^{2{\mathtt{g}}}\dot{G}_{\eta}^{\phantom{{\mathtt{g}}}}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\eta}\ast(\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}-\tilde{f}_{0,T})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\leq 2\delta\end{gathered}

(10.116)

for all
\mu\in[\tau\kappa,T]
. This proves the bounds (A
2
), (B
2
), (C
2
) and shows that

(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{0,T}\in\mathcal{B}_{0,\mu},\quad(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}\in\mathcal{B}_{\nu,\mu},\quad(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast(\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}-\tilde{f}_{0,T})\in\mathcal{V}_{0,\mu}

(10.117)

for all
\mu\in[\tau\kappa,T]
. Using Theorem
10.61
and Remark
2.1
we prove the identity
\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}=\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}]
for all
\mu\in[\tau\kappa,T]
. By the last identity we obtain

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}]\|_{\mathcal{V}}

(10.118)

and

\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast(\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}-\tilde{f}_{0,T})\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}_{\nu,\mu}[(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}]-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}[(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}])\|_{\mathcal{V}}\\
+\|K^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mu}\ast(\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}[(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}]-\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}[(G_{\mu}-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{0,T}])\|_{\mathcal{V}}.

(10.119)

As a result, the bounds (A
1
), (B
1
), (C
1
) for all
\mu\in[\tau\kappa,T]
follow from Theorem
10.61
(A), (B), (C). To complete the proof of the theorem it is enough to apply the above reasoning recursively with
\kappa=\tau^{n}T
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
∎

The above lemma applied with
\mu=0
implies immediately the following theorem.

Theorem 10.63
.

Set
\tilde{\varPhi}_{0}:=(G-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{0,T}
and
\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}:=(G-G_{T})\ast\tilde{f}_{\nu,T}
. It holds

\|\tilde{\varPhi}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq 1/3,\qquad\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq 1/3,\qquad\|\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}-\tilde{\varPhi}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq 2\delta.

(10.120)

for any
\nu\in[0,T]
. Moreover, the following equation is satisfied

\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}=(G-G_{T})\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}].

(10.121)

Remark 10.64
.

Recall that a boundary data and an enhanced data were introduced in Def.
10.1
and
\beta\equiv\beta_{\varepsilon}=-\dim(\phi)-\varepsilon
.

Theorem 10.65
.

Let
t\in[0,\infty)
and
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
[t-i_{\dagger}(i_{\dagger}+1),t+6i_{\dagger}]
. There exists a constant
\breve{c}\geq 1
such that the following is true. Fix some
R>1
,
\delta\in(0,1]
and an admissible boundary data for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
. Let
\nu\in(0,\delta^{1/\varepsilon}]
and a boundary data for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
be arbitrary such that all of the conditions specified in Assumptions
10.9
and
10.11
are satisfied. There exists
T\in(0,1)
depending only on the value of the constant
R
such that for all
\mathring{x}\in[t,t+T]
and all
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds:

(A)

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\breve{c}\,R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,

(B)

\|\mathbf{R}_{\nu}\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\breve{c}\,\delta R\,[\nu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,

(C)

\|\bar{K}_{\mu}^{\ast{\mathtt{g}}}\ast(\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})-\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}))\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\breve{c}\,\delta R\,[\mu]^{\beta_{\varepsilon}}
,

where

\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}:=\varPhi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}+\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}\in C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})),\quad\breve{\varPhi}_{0}:=\varPhi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}+\tilde{\varPhi}_{0}\in C([t,t+T],\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})).

(10.122)

Moreover,
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
satisfies the following equation

\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}=G\ast(1_{(t,\infty)}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})+\delta_{t}\otimes\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}).

(10.123)

Proof.

The bounds (A), (B), (C) follow immediately from the previous theorem and Lemma
10.33
. In particular, bound (B) implies that
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}\in C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
. Recall that
\mathrm{supp}\,G_{T}\subset[T,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
. In order to prove that
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
satisfies Eq. (
10.123
) in the domain
[t,t+T]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
it is enough to note that in this domain the equation
\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}=(G-G_{T})\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}]
is equivalent to the equation
\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}=G\ast\tilde{F}_{\nu}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\nu}]
and it holds
\tilde{F}_{\nu}[\varphi]=1_{(t,\infty)}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi+\varPhi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})
and
\varPhi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}=G\ast(\delta_{t}\otimes\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})
in the domain
[t,t+T]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

10.7
Construction of maximal solution

In order to construct the universal function
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}\in C([0,\breve{T}_{0}),\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}))
with maximal life time and conclude the construction we first discuss briefly the classical solution theory for the equation
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}=G\ast 1_{(0,\infty)}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})+\delta_{0}\otimes\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu}
in the space
C([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
. The classical existence and uniqueness of result is stated in Theorem
10.74
. We use this result to prove the uniqueness of the maximal function
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}
constructed in Theorem
10.76
.

Remark 10.66
.

The spaces
\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})
and
C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
, where
\gamma\equiv\gamma_{\varepsilon}=\sigma-\varepsilon
, were introduced in Def.
5.6
and Def.
5.7
. Recall that

\|\phi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}=\|\mathbf{R}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})},\qquad\|\varphi\|_{C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))}=\|\mathbf{R}\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}([t,t+T]\times\mathbb{T})}.

(10.124)

By
C_{R}([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
we denote the ball in the space
C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
of radius
R>0
centered at zero.

Remark 10.67
.

Note that
G_{T}=0
in the domain
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
. By Lemma
4.23
and Remark
4.21
there exists
R\geq 1
such that

\|\mathbf{R}(G-G_{T})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{M})}\leq R\,[T]^{\varepsilon},\quad\sup_{\mathring{x}\in\mathbb{R}}\|(G-G_{T})(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}})}\leq R.

(10.125)

Remark 10.68
.

Recall that a boundary data
f^{0,0,0}_{\nu}\equiv\varXi_{\nu}\in C(\mathbb{H})
,
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}
, is such that
f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}=0
unless
i\leq i_{\flat}
and
m\leq m_{\flat}
and
a\in\bar{\mathfrak{M}}^{m}
,
|a|<\sigma
. It follows that the force coefficients
\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}=\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu,0}\in\mathcal{D}^{m;0}
introduced in Def.
10.20
vanish unless
i\leq i_{\dagger}=i_{\flat}+i_{\triangleright}m_{\flat}
and
m\leq m_{\flat}
and the following bound

\|(\delta_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes J^{\otimes m})\ast\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu}\|_{L^{\infty}([t,t+T]\times\mathbb{T})}\leq R

(10.126)

is true for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, where the constant
R>1
depends on the choice of the boundary data and the time interval
[t,t+T]
. Recall that
\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright}=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{\triangleright}}\lambda^{i}\mathbf{Q}G_{1}\ast f_{\nu}^{i}
and the force
\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi]
is defined by the equality

\langle\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle:=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\langle\hat{F}^{i,m}_{\nu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle,

(10.127)

where
\psi,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{M})
. As a result, for any
\varphi,\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in C_{\breve{R}}([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
it holds

\begin{gathered}\|\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi]\|_{L^{\infty}([t,t+T]\times\mathbb{T})}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{i_{\dagger}}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{\flat}}\lambda^{i}R\,\breve{R}^{m},\qquad\|\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright}\|_{L^{\infty}([t,t+T]\times\mathbb{T})}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{i_{\triangleright}}\lambda^{i}R,\\
\|\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi_{1}]-\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi_{2}]\|_{L^{\infty}([t,t+T]\times\mathbb{T})}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{i_{\dagger}}\sum_{m=0}^{m_{\flat}}m\,\lambda^{i}R\,\breve{R}^{m-1}\,\|\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\|_{C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))}.\end{gathered}

(10.128)

Remark 10.69
.

Let
\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})
be such that
\|\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq R
for some
R>1
. Then for any
T>0
it holds

G\ast(\delta_{t}\otimes\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})=(G-G_{T})\ast(\delta_{t}\otimes\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})\in C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))

and

\|G\ast(\delta_{t}\otimes\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})\|_{C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))}\leq R\,\sup_{\mathring{x}\in\mathbb{R}}\|(G-G_{T})(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{L^{1}(\bar{\mathbb{M}})}\leq R^{2}.

(10.129)

Lemma 10.70
.

Let
t\in[0,\infty)
,
R>1
and
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
be such that

\|\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq R,\qquad\|\mathtt{u}\varXi_{\nu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\leq R,\qquad|f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}|\leq R,\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I},

(10.130)

and
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighbourhood of
[t-i_{\triangleright}(i_{\triangleright}+1)-2,t+1]
. There exists
T\in(0,1)
depending only on
R
such that the equation

\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}=G\ast(1_{(t,\infty)}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})+\delta_{t}\otimes\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})

(10.131)

has a unique solution
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
in the space
C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
.

Remark 10.71
.

Since
\mathrm{supp}\,G_{T}\subset[T,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathsf{d}}
for any
t\in[0,\infty)
Eq. (
10.131
) posed in the space
C([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
is equivalent to the same equation with
G
replaced by
G-G_{T}
.

Proof.

The proof is very elementary and we give only its outline. Let
\breve{R}=2R^{2}
. It follows from the above remarks that for sufficiently small
T>0
depending on
R
the map
S_{\nu}
defined by the equality

S_{\nu}[\varphi]:=(G-G_{T})\ast(1_{(t,\infty)}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\varphi]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})+\delta_{t}\otimes\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})

(10.132)

leaves invariant the ball
C_{\breve{R}}([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
and has the unique fixed point
\varPhi_{\nu}\in C_{\breve{R}}([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
.

To prove the uniqueness of the solution assume that there is another fixed point
\underaccent{\bar}{\varPhi}_{\nu}
in some larger ball
C_{\breve{\mathrm{R}}}([t,t+T],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
. Then there exists some time
\mathrm{T}\in(0,T]
such that the map
S_{\nu}
leaves invariant the above ball and has a unique fixed point there. This fixed point has to coincide with the restriction of both
\varPhi_{\nu}
and
\underaccent{\bar}{\varPhi}_{\nu}
to the time interval
[t,t+\mathrm{T}]
. In order to prove that these functions coincide in the whole time interval
[t,t+T]
we iterate the above argument using the lemma below.
∎

Lemma 10.72
.

Suppose that for some
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq s_{1}<s_{2}<\infty
the function
\breve{\varPhi}^{(1)}_{\nu}\in C([t_{1},s_{1}],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
solves the equation

\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}=G\ast(1_{(t_{1},\infty)}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})+\delta_{t_{1}}\otimes\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}})

(10.133)

and the function
\breve{\varPhi}^{(2)}_{\nu}\in C([t_{2},s_{2}],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
solves the equation

\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}=G\ast(1_{(t_{2},\infty)}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})+\delta_{t_{2}}\otimes\breve{\varPhi}^{(1)}_{\nu}(t_{2},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})).

(10.134)

Then the function
\breve{\varPhi}^{(12)}_{\nu}\in C([t_{2},s_{1}],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
obtained by restricting
\breve{\varPhi}^{(1)}_{\nu}
to the time interval
[t_{2},s_{1}]
solves Eq. (
10.134
) and the function
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}^{[12]}\in C([t_{1},s_{2}],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
defined by

\breve{\varPhi}^{[12]}_{\nu}(x)=\begin{cases}\breve{\varPhi}^{(1)}_{\nu}(x)~~\textrm{for}~~x\in[t_{1},s_{1}]\times\mathbb{T},\\
\breve{\varPhi}^{(2)}_{\nu}(x)~~\textrm{for}~~x\in(s_{1},s_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}\end{cases}

(10.135)

solves Eq. (
10.133
).

Remark 10.73
.

In order to prove the above lemma it is enough to use the semigroup property of the heat kernel
G
of the fractional Laplacian
(-\Delta)^{\sigma/2}
.

Using Lemma
10.70
and Lemma
10.72
stated above one proves the following theorem.

Theorem 10.74
.

Let
T>0
,
R>1
and
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
be such that

\|\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq R,\qquad\|\mathtt{u}\varXi_{\nu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H})}\leq R,\qquad|f^{i,m,a}_{\nu}|\leq R,\quad(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I},

(10.136)

and
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighbourhood of
[-i_{\triangleright}(i_{\triangleright}+1)-2,T]
. There exists
\breve{T}_{\nu}\in(0,T]
and a function
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}\in C([0,\breve{T}_{\nu}],\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
such that:

(A)

\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
satisfies Eq. (
3.13
),
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}=G\ast(1_{(0,\infty)}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})+\delta_{0}\otimes\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu})
,

(B)

\|\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}<R
for all
\mathring{x}\in[0,\breve{T}_{0})
and, in addition, if
\breve{T}_{\nu}<T
, then
\|\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}=R
for
\mathring{x}=\breve{T}_{\nu}
.

The pair
(\breve{T}_{\nu},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
with the above properties is unique.

Remark 10.75
.

By definition
\breve{T}_{\nu}(R,T)
is a non-decreasing function of
R
and
T
and for
\mathrm{R}\geq R
and
\mathrm{T}\geq T
the first of the following functions

\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}(R,T)\in C([0,\breve{T}_{\nu}],\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})),\qquad\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{T})\in C([0,\breve{\mathrm{T}}_{\nu}],\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}))

(10.137)

coincides with the restriction of the second function to the interval
[0,\breve{T}_{\nu}]
, where
\breve{T}_{\nu}=\breve{T}_{\nu}(R,T)
and
\breve{\mathrm{T}}_{\nu}=\breve{T}_{\nu}(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{T})
. Let

\breve{T}_{\nu}^{\mathrm{max}}:=\lim_{R\nearrow\infty}\lim_{T\nearrow\infty}\breve{T}_{\nu}(R,T)\in(0,\infty].

(10.138)

There is a unique function
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}^{\mathrm{max}}\in C([0,\breve{T}_{\nu}^{\mathrm{max}}),\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}))
such that the restriction of
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}^{\mathrm{max}}
to
[0,\breve{T}_{\nu}(R,T)]
coincides with
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}(R,T)
.

Theorem 10.76
.

Let
T>0
be some finite time horizon and let
\mathtt{u}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\mathtt{u}=1
on some neighborhood of
[-i_{\dagger}(i_{\dagger}+1),6i_{\dagger}+T]
. Fix some
R>1
,
\delta\in(0,1]
and an admissible boundary data for
(\varPhi_{0}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{0})
. Let
\nu\in(0,\delta^{1/\varepsilon}]
and a boundary data for
(\varPhi_{\nu}^{\triangleright},\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu})
be arbitrary such that all of the conditions specified in Assumptions
10.9
and
10.11
are satisfied. Let
\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}\in C([0,\breve{T}_{\nu}),\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
be the maximal classical solution of Eq. (
3.13
)

\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}=G\ast(1_{(0,\infty)}(\hat{F}_{\nu}[\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}]+\check{f}_{\nu}^{\triangleright})+\delta_{0}\otimes\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\nu})

(10.139)

constructed in Theorem
10.74
and Remark
10.75
. There exists a universal time
\breve{T}_{0}\in(0,T]
and a universal function
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}\in C([0,\breve{T}_{0}],\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}))
such that:

(A)

\|\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}<R
for all
\mathring{x}\in[0,\breve{T}_{0})
and, in addition, if
\breve{T}_{0}<T
, then
\|\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}=R
for
\mathring{x}=\breve{T}_{0}
,

(B)

for all sufficiently small
\nu\in(0,1]
it holds
\breve{T}_{\nu}\geq\breve{T}_{0}
and there exits a constant
c>0
such that
\|\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}-\breve{\varPhi}_{0}\|_{C([0,\breve{T}_{0}],\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}))}\leq c\,\delta
.

Remark 10.77
.

The pair
(\breve{\varPhi}_{0},\breve{T}_{0})=(\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(R,T),\breve{T}_{0}(R,T))
depends only on the choice of the initial data
\phi^{\mathrm{reg}}_{0}
, the choice of the renormalization parameters
\mathfrak{f}^{i,m,a}_{0}
,
(i,m,a)\in\mathfrak{I}^{-}
, and the choice of the constants
T>0
and
R>1
.

Remark 10.78
.

We define the maximal solution
(\breve{T}_{0}^{\mathrm{max}},\breve{\varPhi}_{0}^{\mathrm{max}})
as in Remark
10.75
.

Proof.

Let
t=0
. By Theorem
10.65
for all sufficiently small
\mathrm{T}>0
depending only on
R
there exists a function
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}\in C([t,t+\mathrm{T}],\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}))
such that
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}=\lim_{\nu\searrow 0}\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}
in the domain
[t,t+\mathrm{T}]\times\mathbb{T}\subset\mathbb{H}
. If
\|\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(\mathring{x},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}<R
for all
\mathring{x}\in[t,t+\mathrm{T}]
, and
t+\mathrm{T}<T
, then we repeat the above procedure with
t
replaced by
t+\mathrm{T}
. Otherwise the procedure terminates. The function
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}\in C([0,\breve{T}_{0}],\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}))
with all the desired properties is constructed by patching together the functions
\breve{\varPhi}_{0}\in C([t,t+\mathrm{T}],\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}))
.

In the construction described above we implicitly used the following two facts. (1) For every finite
R
and
T
the above procedure always terminates after finitely many of steps. (2) Suppose that the conditions stated in Assumption
10.11
hold true for
\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}=\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(t,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})
and
\phi_{\nu}^{\mathrm{reg}}=\breve{\varPhi}_{\nu}(t,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})
with
R>1
and
\delta=\delta_{0}\in(0,1/\breve{c})
, where the constant
\breve{c}\geq 1
was introduced in Theorem
10.65
. Then by the bound
\|\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(t+\mathrm{T},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}<R
and Theorem
10.65
(B), (C)
the conditions in Assumption
10.11
hold true also for
\phi_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}=\breve{\varPhi}_{0}(t+\mathrm{T},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})
and

with the same
R>1
and
\delta=\breve{c}\,\delta_{0}
.
